# Whole-System DIM/Profile V2 — Working Workbook

**Step 01 baseline · 31 October 2026 · Read this first**

**Status:** Initial project/evidence baseline **verified and recorded**. No new Domain Information Model (DIM) class, OGC API – Connected Systems (CSAPI) Profile requirement, data transformation, or client behavior is adopted here. This workbook is the **cumulative, human-readable working area** for the [43-step plan](../PLAN_DIM_Profile_V2_Whole_System_Recovery_to_Delivery_v0.1_31OCT2026.md), not another independent research campaign.

## 1. What we are trying to build

Imagine opening the same Resource Details view for two very different sensing systems. One has a single cabled probe producing temperature and humidity; another has numerous internal sensing modules; another has a separate logger and weather sensor; yet another replaced a physical temperature probe halfway through its historical record.

A good client must recognize **what the equipment is, what it can do, what it actually did, how the pieces relate, and how much the source really establishes**. It must make those facts understandable without needing new, hard-coded presentation rules for each manufacturer. At the same time, it must not pretend that an advertised option is installed, a device manual is an actual unit record, a logger is necessarily the sensing element, or an old observation is today's equipment condition.

The intended outputs remain **three different things**:

- **DIM:** a defensible, *implementation-independent* set of common meanings and relationships for the whole sensing System, with appropriate specializations and explicit limits.
- **CSAPI application Profile:** a separate, testable way for producers to communicate those justified meanings through existing CSAPI, Sensor Model Language (SensorML), SWE Common, and other appropriate standard structures.
- **Glaux proof:** the **same meaning-aware reading path** interprets more than one independently documented equipment arrangement, then displays useful and correctly qualified information in the existing Resource Details UI.

**The project can be successful within a stated scope without claiming all vendors, all 182 source artifacts, or adoption as an OGC standard.** The final scope and overarching domain/chassis name remain research questions, not decisions made by this workbook.

## 2. Starting evidence: four cases with different standing

The cases below are **starting evidence**, not completed cross-case comparisons or proven installations. Their detailed source fact audit and content-area reconciliation belong to **Step 03**; their source-native system reconstructions belong to **Steps 05–08**.

| Starting case | Why it helps challenge a common model | What the current source actually supports |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Dragino S31-LB](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md) | A cabled external probe, two measured properties, node, battery, LoRaWAN and documented settings | Manufacturer reference design and published message examples; **no authenticated individual installation or present settings** |
| [AirGradient Open Air Max](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md) | A more complex assembly of boards, sensors, communications and reporting roles | Documented product arrangement and source examples; **not a verified fielded unit or joined configuration/history** |
| [Campbell CR1000X + first-generation ClimaVue 50](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md) | The sensing assembly and the logger/reporting equipment have distinct jobs | Documented reference pairing and separate publisher examples; **the examples are not evidence of one complete running installation** |
| [Bennett’s Point (ACE Basin)](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json) | A real historical measurement record and dated replacement of two individually identifiable EE181 probes | **Two source-published historical temperature measurements** and separate operator equipment-use history, joined by a project attribution; **not** complete station configuration, current telemetry or a verified CSAPI Deployment graph |

**One concrete question that still matters:** At Bennett’s Point, the physical probe senses the air; station equipment logs an average. Our [offline representation](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-wire/README.md) is station-oriented, while the [live 52°North experiment](https://github.com/OS4CSAPI/glaux-client/blob/work/details-view-20261023/Docs/Design/BENNETT_OBSERVATION_CONTEXT_BRIDGE.md) uses probe-owned DataStreams plus parallel archival report streams. That difference is an **unresolved modeling/representation choice**, not evidence that one approach is universally correct. The source CSV itself does **not** identify a probe serial; the association comes from separate equipment history.

**Historical context only:** TRSS and OmniSense motivated the original problem, but their preserved examples are not compulsory experimental anchors and include synthetic/unverified cases. Smart Citizen Kit 2.3 remains valuable product-design research; it does not establish an authenticated physical kit.

## 4. What we have NOT proved — and must keep visible

**No common reader yet.** Existing experiments include particular rendering paths and manually authored terms. Passing a demo does not mean we can interpret another independently described System without new vendor/case code.

**No integrated, accepted whole-System DIM yet.** The first five conceptual meanings are promising but are not mandatory classes. Earlier source selection was not fully neutral; later donors may introduce, split, combine or replace those distinctions. The [corrective drift study](../DIM_Profile_V2_AI_Hallucination_and_Drift_Case_Study_v0.1_30OCT2026.md) prohibits automatically choosing power/“operational condition” or any other unearned priority.

**No comprehensive, equivalent real dataset for all cases.** A reference design is useful for testing design-time semantics but cannot establish an installed component, selected setting, current condition or actual command outcome.

**No fully solved Bennett time/quality/Profile encoding.** The [31-October observation-encoding review](../Candidate_Specification/Observation_Encoding_Decision_31OCT2026.md) documents real server limitations. The preserved 15-minute window and original NOAA QA token are additional **archival report context**, not native interval-valued `phenomenonTime` or a certified source `resultTime`. **Two distinct measurements are represented by four server Observation resources.** No physical installation geometry was inferred.

**No assumed donor equality or conformance.** The research corpus contains 182 *artifacts*, not 182 independently assessed standards. The [donor-first review](../Concept_Donor_Comparison/Donor_Contributions.md) records incomplete and uneven coverage. The original source documents govern actual definitions; a mapping asserted by this project is not a donor-standard requirement.

## 5. Step 02 — How we will judge whether meaning survives

**Status: Step 02 COMPLETE, 31 October 2026.** This is a common testing method, **not** a new DIM class, an adopted Profile rule, or an executed cross-vendor test. It answers one human question: *Can the same client accurately understand what two different equipment sources mean, or does information become lost, invented, or confused along the way?*

For example, if a manufacturer says **one external probe measures temperature and humidity**, it would be wrong for our client to show **two installed probes** simply because two outputs exist. Our rubric must identify the original fact, the intended common meaning, how we encoded it, what the client actually displayed, and **where an error first appeared**.

### 5.1 Five questions for every claim or relationship

| Question | What we must write down |
| --- | --- |
| **1. Source: What was actually established?** | Original source wording, link/edition/locator, value and units if relevant, **which subject** the fact concerns, its time and scope, and its evidence standing. Do not silently replace manufacturer terminology with ours. |
| **2. DIM: What does it mean beyond that manufacturer?** | Proposed source-neutral meaning and relationships, original donor support and exact qualifiers. State **fits / needs revision / unresolved**; do not force new material into the existing five candidate headings. |
| **3. Profile/producer: How can it be communicated?** | Relevant CSAPI/SensorML/SWE Common resource, field, link or vocabulary, with the standard’s actual authority versus our proposed binding identified. Record **actual encoded output** if produced; otherwise mark **not tested**. |
| **4. Client: What did Glaux really understand and show?** | Actual interpreted subject/relationship, visible text, source/uncertainty qualifiers and raw fields. Disclose any **vendor-name or case-ID-specific rule** instead of claiming a common interpreter. |
| **5. Fidelity: Did the meaning survive?** | Compare source against actual encoded and displayed results; assign the result below, identify the **first demonstrated loss/misstatement point**, and link the evidence. A build passing is not enough. |

**Minimum reusable test record:** case; source document/locator and native claim; evidence category; subject, time and applicability; candidate DIM meaning with rationale; proposed Profile binding; actual produced CSAPI record (or “not produced”); actual shared Glaux output (or “not run”); fidelity result; first supportable fault location; source/implementation/test links. Make one comparison per independently testable **fact or relationship**, not one per pretty UI card. Retain different representations of the *same* observation as one underlying source event, not different measurements.

### 5.2 Two separate classifications — *what kind of evidence* versus *did it work*

The project’s evidence labels from the [plan](../PLAN_DIM_Profile_V2_Whole_System_Recovery_to_Delivery_v0.1_31OCT2026.md) are attached **to individual claims**, not indiscriminately to an entire device or standard. A report can contain source-backed product-design information, project calculations and a later successful software test at the same time.

| Evidence label | What it means — and does not mean |
| --- | --- |
| `SOURCE_FACT` | Explicitly supported source information **within its actual scope**. A manufacturer’s design claim is a fact *about the design*, not a verified field installation. |
| `SOURCE_EXAMPLE` | A source-published sample/record, **not** evidence that our selected hardware actually emitted it. Preserve its publication context. |
| `DERIVED` | A reproducible calculation or join made by this project from named source inputs; for example, Bennett’s Point UTC conversion or equipment-history attribution. Do not say the CSV supplied the serial. |
| `CANDIDATE_MAPPING` | Our interpretation or proposed DIM/Profile correspondence, **not** an adopted donor or OGC requirement. |
| `VERIFIED_RUNTIME` | An actual, versioned software/server test with recorded input, output and assertion. It proves only those assertions, not source authenticity or universal interoperability. |
| `UNKNOWN` | The source did not establish the fact or its applicability. Keep it unknown; **not zero, false, absent hardware, or a current status value**. |
| `REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE` | A source-grounded bad identification explicitly ruled out; e.g., **two measured properties ≠ two physical probes**. Useful for negative tests. |

**Fidelity result** is independently assigned *after* comparing inputs and outputs:

| Result | Exact use |
| --- | --- |
| **PRESERVED** | Executed check confirms source subject, value/meaning, relationship and qualifiers remain correct. |
| **PRESERVED WITH QUALIFICATION** | Meaning survives, explicitly identified as, for example, **documented capability** or **historical report**, not installed state or current telemetry. |
| **SOURCE UNAVAILABLE / NOT ASSERTED** | No source evidence for that requested fact. Correctly saying “unknown” is **not** an integration failure. |
| **NOT TESTED** | One or more required model/producer/client stages haven’t actually been exercised; documentation or a plausible mapping is not a pass. |
| **LOST / OMITTED** | The source-established fact disappears from an inspected later stage where our defined test expected it. Cite both sides. |
| **MISREPRESENTED** | A later stage invents, changes, merges or overstates source subject, identity, state, meaning, time, unit or relationship. **Hard failure**, however attractive the page. |
| **CONFLICT / UNRESOLVED** | Source claims conflict or sound competing meanings/bindings remain unsettled. Show alternatives, not fake completion. |

**Do not compute a success percentage yet.** Counts of PASS in old research plans, records returned by a server and display cards are not a shared denominator for source-to-client meaning.

### 5.3 Diagnose the earliest supported problem, not the most visible symptom

| Where the problem begins | What to check |
| --- | --- |
| **Source/evidence gap** | Was this fact ever documented for this subject, time and product version? If no, use `UNKNOWN`, not “lost in CSAPI.” |
| **DIM meaning gap** | Does our model collapse distinct physical components, functions, channels, observer/reporting roles, settings or events? |
| **Profile/binding gap** | Is the DIM distinction clear, but the specified standards carrier, semantic definition or required link wrong, unjustified or missing? |
| **Producer/adapter loss** | Was a source-backed meaning dropped or changed while producing the CSAPI representation? |
| **API/server/format loss** | Did a correct submitted value fail the actual API round trip because of this implementation or encoding? Record endpoint and response. |
| **Shared reader/UI error** | Was the correct meaning retrieved but misinterpreted, put in the wrong view or presented as a stronger claim? |
| **Cannot localize yet** | We lack adjacent before/after artifacts. Record the needed comparison; **do not guess the cause**. |

An error may appear in Glaux but originate earlier. More than one problem may exist. The aim is to fix **the first justified cause**, not patch the screen until it looks convincing.

### 5.4 Concrete positive and negative examples — Dragino external probe

The [Dragino S31-LB source sheet, C-I01–C-I03, C-I08 and C-I10](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md) documents the manufacturer’s **reference design**: one cabled external **SHT31 temperature/humidity probe** and the two supported measurement properties. The sheet explicitly has **no authenticated physical unit or current telemetry**. So the source supports a product-design fact—not a claim that any specific unit is deployed or taking a reading.

**Positive scenario (NOT YET EXECUTED):** one common set of DIM/Profile meanings communicates **one physical sensing component** with **two supported measured properties/outputs**. Without a manufacturer-specific rule, Glaux shows one *documented* probe arrangement and two *supported* outputs, clearly qualified as reference design. If this succeeds with a second independently documented, different system using the **same interpretation path**, the specific design-level relation can earn **PRESERVED WITH QUALIFICATION**. Today it is **NOT TESTED**.

**Negative scenario (would be MISREPRESENTED):** a client turns the two outputs into **two separately installed probes**, or shows a present temperature reading simply because the product manual says temperature is supported. Both would exceed the source evidence. If the produced CSAPI resource already invents the second probe, the fault starts in the **producer** (or earlier model/binding); if the representation correctly describes one probe and the client renders two, the fault is in the **reader/UI**. If the DIM *requires* one physical sensor per output, that is the **DIM's error**. This is a `REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE` test, not a newly invented standard rule.

**Existing historical counterexample (not a new test):** [Bennett’s Point](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json) contains **two real historical temperature measurements** while the [current preservation experiment](https://github.com/OS4CSAPI/glaux-client/blob/work/details-view-20261023/Docs/Design/BENNETT_OBSERVATION_CONTEXT_BRIDGE.md) has four server Observation resources: two original scalar records and two richer representations. A client showing **four physical measurements** would be **MISREPRESENTED**. A client that shows all four as explicitly labeled *resources* might preserve raw source transparency, but has **not automatically proved general, source-neutral deduplication**. Prior round-trip tests cover specific data preservation, not broad interoperability.

### 5.5 Later success requires cross-case interpretation, not merely a successful endpoint

Within a bounded case, report what actually passed **at its evidence level**, and what remains unknown or untested. A source design can test *design-level meanings*; it cannot prove installed state, executed control or current condition. A server response can prove a response was returned, but not the underlying historical truth of a manufacturer/field record.

For the plan’s eventual cross-vendor proof, require at least **two meaningfully different, independently documented arrangements** processed through **one common interpretation**, without vendor- or case-ID-dependent meaning rules. The same interpretation must preserve important differences and unknowns instead of filling gaps with plausible but invented facts. Bennett’s Point is a candidate *separate historical-unit/time test*, not equivalent to all the model-level cases.

**Outcome of Step 02:** We have defined a falsifiable testing method, but **no new Profile conformance result, software test, model choice or cross-vendor PASS**. Case-source coverage and permissible evidence claims will be reconciled in Step 03.

## 6. Step 03 — What evidence do the four cases *actually* provide?

**Status: Step 03 COMPLETE, 31 October 2026.** This is an **evidence-coverage reconciliation**, not the source-native full-system reconstructions of Steps 05–08, an all-source re-audit, or a DIM/Profile decision. We revisited the existing [manufacturer source sheets](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/README.md), their [original audit registers](../Controlled_Corpus/08_Frozen_Corpus_Manifest_and_Handoff.md#4-frozen-source-reference-inventory--not-an-upstream-byte-archive), the [qualified corpus decision](../Controlled_Corpus/07_Corpus_Qualification_Decision.md), and the [Bennett evidence record](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json). We also spot-checked selected original manufacturer material and its exact editions, rather than assuming the older summaries were normative standards or new telemetry.

**What stands out in ordinary language:** All four cases can help us understand a sensing System, but **they do not represent four comparable, verified operating installations**. Dragino, AirGradient and the Campbell/ClimaVue pairing tell us what products *are designed or documented to do*, with source-published example messages; Bennett’s Point instead tells us something about *specific equipment actually used at a station on specific dates*. Comparing those evidence levels as if they were equal would defeat the [Step 02 fidelity rules](#5-step-02--how-we-will-judge-whether-meaning-survives).

**Version and preservation controls:** The earlier [eight-step corpus plan and manifest](../Controlled_Corpus/08_Frozen_Corpus_Manifest_and_Handoff.md) preserve **A01–A12, C01–C06 and D01–D09** references (27 source IDs / 32 original URLs), their original 29–30 Oct audit dates and replay limitations. This is a *source-reference/working-record freeze*, **not** an archive of every publisher's original file bytes or redistribution rights. Pinned code commits are source editions; mutable manufacturer guidance is accessed by date, not silently treated as a firmware release. The original source texts, exact sections and existing gaps control claims; this Step 03 did not re-run every checker, download all manuals or reacquire the 5.26 MB NOAA CSV. Spot checks included the three immutable original GitHub source files, Dragino's manual, Campbell's 08/2024 wiring page and 2026 status sample, and AirGradient's power-mode article. The original Bennett PDF's indexed text was accessible; its image screenshot could not be retrieved in this review, so the published working evidence and specific operator locators remain the basis for its dated equipment history.

### 6.2 What each case can answer about the *whole sensing System*

**Read the cells literally:** **Design** = documented product/reference capability or structure (`SOURCE_FACT` at *design* scope); **Example** = `SOURCE_EXAMPLE` from original manufacturer material; **History** = real operator source evidence for the *stated* station/time; **Derived** = our separate interpretation; **Unknown** = unsupported, not a numeric zero. A case can legitimately have design information and no actual-state data. This is **coverage**, not a pass/fail score or adopted DIM module list.

| Information question | AirGradient Open Air Max | Dragino S31-LB | Campbell CR1000X + ClimaVue 50 | Bennett’s Point |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **What system/model or individual?** | **Design:** gas-equipped `-CE` product assembly; no unit serial. | **Design:** S31-LB product/node and cabled SHT31; no installed Device EUI. | **Design:** two named product kinds; no installed unit identity in this reference case. | **History:** station `acebpmet`, two identified EE181 probe serials across dated use periods, recorded CR1000X logger serial **13916**; **Derived:** association of the selected CSV rows to the probes. |
| **What physical parts or relationships?** | **Design:** boards/modules (including two PMS5003s), BMS, solar/battery/cellular; options and outputs are not extra installed sensors. | **Design:** battery/radio node with **one external** three-metre SHT31. Gateway is a dependency, not part of proven unit inventory. | **Design:** sensor assembly, data logger and cable; SDI-12 interface. Installed enclosure/power accessories not verified. | **History:** separate station/logger and probe equipment, with time-scoped use; no complete physical bill of materials or encoded Deployment relations. |
| **What is measured or reported?** | **Design/Example:** air-quality, temperature/humidity, PM and gas-related outputs; Wi-Fi/cellular examples have different shapes; cloud reports are separate examples. | **Design/Example:** temperature/humidity and battery/status message fields; the published FPort-3 example has eight historical entries, **not** identified real-node history. | **Design:** ClimaVue output definitions (including weather properties); **Example:** a **CR1000X logger-status** message, **not weather measurements** from the stated pairing. | **History:** two actual station **ATemp** summaries (15 Apr 29.1 °C, 18 Apr 27.3 °C, 2024, both 12:00 fixed EST) with separate `F_ATemp="<0> "` tokens; not evidence for other parameters or their sensors. |
| **Supported/selected configuration?** | **Design:** portal/source configuration and power/interval modes; **Unknown:** settings effective on any unit. | **Design:** AT/downlink choices, alarm thresholds, reported defaults; **Unknown:** installed region/firmware, applied settings. | **Design/Example:** address/scan guidance and historic program; **Unknown:** the actual program/setting used by one installed pair. | **History:** operator says CDMO-supplied logger program used; **Unknown:** actual complete running 2024 configuration export and any present configuration. |
| **Power, state or diagnostics?** | **Design/Example:** battery/solar, voltage fields, LED errors/modes; no genuine unit-condition history; gas-calibration cautions. | **Design/Example:** 8,500 mAh nameplate, voltage encoding and mode/status fields; **Unknown:** real battery remaining capacity, observed condition. | **Design/Example:** logger `PanelTemp`, `Battery`, ports, status fields; *the publisher's example*, not observed health of the reference ClimaVue station. | **History/metadata:** logger and station data-collection/QA procedures described. Battery voltage is a *named archive parameter*, but **not one of the two extracted observations**; no present probe health is established. |
| **Communications and reporting roles?** | **Design:** Wi-Fi and cellular paths; **Example:** device formats vs public cloud example schemas, not an authenticated joined pipeline. | **Design:** LoRaWAN uplinks, BLE/UART configuration, FPort formats, gateway/server examples; not a verified session. | **Design:** sensor-to-logger **SDI-12** and separately documented logger-to-server MQTT API; not one tested combined installation. | **History/metadata:** logger recording, LoggerNet downloads and GOES hourly station upload described; **not** proof of any one live 2026 session. |
| **Commands, requests and outcomes?** | **Design:** possible configuration/updates, local indications; **Unknown:** an executed request, acknowledgement, outcome or active mode. | **Design:** detailed AT/downlink commands; **Example:** printed settings responses, not a command sent here. | **Design:** SDI-12 and logger programming/control interface; **Unknown:** real command session/acknowledgement and result. | **History:** recorded field maintenance and an April 17 logger operating-system update; **Unknown:** complete command transcript or response sequence. |
| **Installation, history and lifecycle?** | **Unknown:** actual site/unit/service periods; publisher examples contain placeholder coordinates and serial. | **Unknown:** actual unit/location/service history; the source-published eight-record history is a format example. | **Unknown:** real station, equipment-use intervals and continuous observations for the reference pairing; example geometry is `[0,0,0]`. | **History:** EE181 sensor 4 in use **8 Jun 2023–16 Apr 2024**, sensor 3 **returned 16 Apr 2024** (also prior service 2022–23); exact swap minute unknown, “still in use” only as of **31 Dec 2024**, not present day. |
| **Quality, time and source authority?** | **Example:** units/scaling, source-specific gas cautions and unresolved AFE fields; examples not one synchronized observation. | **Example:** time bytes and raw flags; display time zone, decoder labels and calibration status remain qualified. | **Example:** timestamped **logger-only** status, 13 unresolved detailed status meanings; PanelTemp ≠ outdoor air. | **History/Derived:** temperature-specific initial-QA flag `<0>` and original stamp, EST→UTC conversion, *nominal* prior 15-minute window, equipment-history inference; raw CSV flag retains whitespace. |

**Comparison fairness:** A “Design” cell isn't inferior engineering; it is a different type of evidence from an operator's actual observation. A manufacturer can define a command or physical interface rigorously without an authenticated installation. Conversely, Bennett’s Point can prove a dated replacement and station output without proving every mode, hardware part or control function. Keep these scopes separate when we later ask whether **the same meanings** work across cases.

### 6.3 Which comparisons are justified now, and which are not?

**Justified at present, for bounded source interpretation:**

- **Physical sensing arrangement:** Dragino's one external SHT31 versus AirGradient's internal/multiple sensing modules versus Campbell's separate ClimaVue assembly/logger. This can challenge candidate *physical part versus function/output* distinctions. It cannot establish deployed unit identity.
- **Output and reporting roles:** a manufacturer's device payload versus its cloud/service example (AirGradient), an addressed probe/node plus radio packets (Dragino), a sensor interface versus logger's published status output (Campbell), and station-averaged data with a separately known historical probe (Bennett). **One API response or value is not automatically the sensing device itself.**
- **Claim standing:** capabilities/defaults in manuals can be compared with other capabilities/defaults, and actual historical Bennett use with the dated operator log. We must not describe a product's supported option as “currently selected,” or make Bennett's sensor as-of-2024 statement a 2026 condition claim.
- **Original field meaning:** Campbell `PanelTemp` is logger panel temperature, not the same subject as Bennett `ATemp` air temperature. Likewise, an AirGradient raw electrode value isn't automatically the same as a calibrated gas concentration.

**Not justified yet:**

- Comparing four **verified physical deployments**, sensor health histories or active configurations: only Bennett supplies the specific historical attribution records, and those are incomplete for whole-System operation.
- Joining AirGradient's hardware/firmware/cloud examples into one observed unit, or Campbell's 2019 program, 2021 package, 2024 manual and 2026 status sample into one running installation.
- Treating Dragino's eight sample history entries as evidence that a specific registered S31-LB produced them, or treating manufacturer code as a formal API conformance certification.
- Counting four CSAPI Observation resources in the Bennett experiment as four actual measurements; two are *additional representations* of the same two source records. Neither server read-back nor a polished Glaux screen settles which station/logger/probe role should own the DataStream.
- Declaring a single final “chassis,” mandatory DIM taxonomy, Profile requirement or live client compatibility from this evidence inventory. Those belong to later donor/model/binding/test steps.

**Owner-visible source problem remaining:** We have useful and varied product **design evidence**, but too little cohesive **same-unit, same-configuration, same-history** material for a broad operating-system test. This is a **data-availability and association limitation**, not proof the candidate concepts are wrong or that the research must stop. Step 04 will make **one bounded** check for the most relevant missing linked evidence; it may fail without changing the honest ceiling of our later work.

### 6.4 Step 03 verification and handoff

- **Inputs reconciled:** the three [source-qualified system sheets](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/README.md), [A/C/D audit registers and frozen manifest](../Controlled_Corpus/08_Frozen_Corpus_Manifest_and_Handoff.md), [Bennett source evidence](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json), the [prior comparison](../Concept_Donor_Comparison/Equipment_Evidence.md) and the Step 02 test vocabulary. No manual/source material was republished into the repository.
- **Spot checks** establish that the inspected pinned manufacturer code/docs and selected currently accessible original HTML/PDF still match the relevant *structural and example-scope* statements. The immutable source commits remain the basis for precise claims; mutable live HTML is not treated as an authenticated historical product edition. Original Bennett metadata was not completely re-audited from a fresh downloaded PDF; this step uses the pinned source evidence record plus the accessible PDF text. No new certification of original source completeness is claimed.
- **Result:** four cases classified; known source editions and inspected material identified; permitted evidence use and whole-System information-area coverage reconciled; incomplete records and risky joins explicitly held. **No new source-to-DIM/Profile mapping or cross-vendor test passed.**
- **Next, Step 04:** one bounded check for higher-quality, coherently linked operating evidence using *already known source leads*. **Do not** begin the full Dragino/AirGradient/Campbell/Bennett system reconstruction (Steps 05–08) or a new donor campaign during this evidence check.

## 7. Step 04 — Bounded attempt to acquire linked operating evidence

**Status: Step 04 COMPLETE, 31 October 2026.** **Outcome: a qualified historical station/channel evidence packet was acquired; a complete operating-System packet was NOT.** This was one bounded follow-up on an **existing documented source lead**, not a new donor search, manufacturer comparison, server implementation or change to previously qualified cases. Neither raw waveform files nor provider XML were published into the repository or Glaux.

### 7.1 Why this one lead?

The [earlier Raspberry Shake & Boom audit](../Controlled_Corpus/04_Candidate_B_Evidence_Audit.md) had identified the strongest available missing connection: **an actual named station associated by its manufacturer with a particular RS&BOOM V3 instrument**, together with public historical waveform and instrument metadata endpoints. That audit could inspect the manufacturer’s description but **could not retrieve the XML or miniSEED file contents**; therefore its candidate remained **CONDITIONAL / outside the primary A/C/D set**.

We chose **`AM.R9D92.00`** and only the provider’s documented **July 22, 2018, 11:23–11:25 UTC** window. The [manufacturer original](https://manual.raspberryshake.org/bru2.html#some-background-on-pa-bru2) identifies this particular station as **RS&BOOM V3** and separately describes two channels, **EHZ** (vertical seismic velocity) and **HDF** (infrasound/atmospheric pressure). The original says the instruments were co-located for comparison. These are **manufacturer-published model/station assertions**; the identifier is a **network/station/location code**, not by itself a hardware serial-number attestation.

### 7.2 What was retrieved and verified

The direct shell/container request failed to resolve the public service host, and the ordinary web reader rejected the XML/binary response types. We used **read-only browser-side requests through the authorized Cloudflare Browser Rendering capability** to inspect the public endpoint. Only summarized response metadata and decoded fixed record headers are retained here; **no waveform samples or provider response XML have been redistributed or added to the repository**.

| Source/request | Actual result (31 Oct 2026) | Supported meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Manufacturer’s co-location and 2018 example](https://manual.raspberryshake.org/bru2.html), RS&BOOM V3/AM.R9D92 entries | Directly inspected provider description and retrieval command | **`SOURCE_FACT` about the manufacturer’s stated station/model relationship**, not an audited internal hardware inventory or 2026 state |
| [FDSN station channel metadata — R9D92](https://data.raspberryshake.org/fdsnws/station/1/query?network=AM&station=R9D92&level=channel&format=text) | HTTP **200**, text response. Reports **EHZ** and **HDF** with several time-scoped channel entries; each includes a **2018-07-21 15:16:43.001** start entry covering the target date; both report **100 samples/s**. EHZ metadata labels **Velocity / M/S**, HDF **Atmospheric Pressure / PA**, with scale/context values. | Actual **source-served station/channel metadata**; source units and response scale describe metadata, **not yet validated waveform calibration**. Historical equipment channel definitions can change across epochs |
| [FDSN seismic waveform — EHZ, 2018-07-22 11:23–11:25 UTC](https://data.raspberryshake.org/fdsnws/dataselect/1/query?net=AM&sta=R9D92&loc=00&cha=EHZ&start=2018-07-22T11:23:00&end=2018-07-22T11:25:00) | HTTP **200**, `application/vnd.fdsn.mseed`, **17,920 bytes**. Decoded the first miniSEED fixed header: `AM.R9D92.00.EHZ`, year **2018**, day **203**, time **11:22:59**, nominal sample-rate factor **100**, multiplier **1**. First record reports **434 samples**. | **`VERIFIED_RUNTIME`:** archive actually delivered binary waveform records for this named historical channel, and the first header agrees with the requested station/channel and 100 Hz context; **not** a decoded, calibrated time series or completeness check |
| [FDSN infrasound waveform — HDF, same request window](https://data.raspberryshake.org/fdsnws/dataselect/1/query?net=AM&sta=R9D92&loc=00&cha=HDF&start=2018-07-22T11:23:00&end=2018-07-22T11:25:00) | HTTP **200**, `application/vnd.fdsn.mseed`, **26,112 bytes**. First miniSEED fixed header: `AM.R9D92.00.HDF`, year **2018**, day **203**, time **11:22:57**, factor **100**, multiplier **1**. First record reports **253 samples**. | The same named station also returns an **actual infrasound-channel waveform response**. This is stronger evidence of two reported sensing modalities than a model-only description |

The first fixed-header timestamps **precede** the requested 11:23 start. They are the timestamps of **the first returned records**, not a proof of the requested cut’s exact included sample bounds, event onset, clock accuracy, or 120 seconds of complete decoded observations. **No waveform sample values, duration/completeness statistics, calibration-corrected pressure/velocity values, or sample-to-event interpretations were calculated.** FDSN channel `Scale`/`ScaleUnits` are metadata; we did not apply or validate the relevant instrument response XML. The provider’s own documentation warns that reported station coordinates have been obfuscated; **do not map those metadata coordinates as verified real installation geometry**.

**Source-level provenance matters:** The station/model relationship comes from the manufacturer’s [RS&BOOM comparison page](https://manual.raspberryshake.org/bru2.html); the channel epochs/units come from the [live station-metadata response](https://data.raspberryshake.org/fdsnws/station/1/query?network=AM&station=R9D92&level=channel&format=text); and the two binary fixed headers come from the respective [EHZ](https://data.raspberryshake.org/fdsnws/dataselect/1/query?net=AM&sta=R9D92&loc=00&cha=EHZ&start=2018-07-22T11:23:00&end=2018-07-22T11:25:00) and [HDF](https://data.raspberryshake.org/fdsnws/dataselect/1/query?net=AM&sta=R9D92&loc=00&cha=HDF&start=2018-07-22T11:23:00&end=2018-07-22T11:25:00) FDSN responses. This is a **source-grounded join at station/channel and date**, not a verified internal bill of materials or physically read product serial.

### 7.3 What is still missing, and the important rights boundary

| Needed for a **complete real operating-System packet** | Evidence after this check | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Real named instrument and bounded reference product type | **Manufacturer identifies station R9D92 as RS&BOOM V3**; actual FDSN network/station/channel records were retrieved | Stronger than the previous audit’s model-only source leads, **but not an independently inspected unit serial or hardware revision** |
| Actual historical data for the same station | **Both EHZ and HDF binary responses retrieved; identifiers and first fixed headers verified** | Partial actual recorded-data coverage; complete waveform parsing, response calibration and quality/availability audit **not performed** |
| Time-scoped channel/response and recording characteristics | Channel metadata includes time epochs, reported 100 sps and source sensitivity/units; response endpoint returned XML but the response content was not semantically audited | Metadata coverage exists; **do not assign model defaults, physical filters or calibrated units to raw samples** |
| Actual local operating configuration and equipment inventory | **Not acquired**: installed Pi/board revision, filter, firmware, physical component serial, applied configuration, power/status and maintenance history | **`UNKNOWN`**, not evidence that the equipment lacks these functions |
| Distribution/publication rights for project and eventual CSAPI/Glaux usage | [Provider license](https://raspberryshake.org/license/), “Legacy & FDSN Web Services” and “Redistribution of Legacy Data” | **Not cleared for our intended application**. Historical discrete research requests and public download availability do **not** grant blanket redistribution permission. The license says commercial archive use requires negotiation and prohibits re-serving obtained waveforms through another FDSN service/data center/server; commercial platform integration has further restrictions. **Do not place these bytes into our public GitHub, Oracle CSAPI or deployed Glaux without a specific rights review/permission** |

Provider’s FDSN guide: [FDSN Web Services](https://manual.raspberryshake.org/fdsn.html). Provider publication attribution: **Raspberry Shake / DOI 10.7914/SN/AM**. No conclusion is offered here about whether the eventual project is legally classed as academic or commercial; the specific redistribution/usage grant must be checked against the intended activity.

### 7.4 Decision for the next phases — improve the evidence, not the scope by stealth

**Acquisition result: PARTIAL QUALIFIED PACKET ACQUIRED.** We can now point to a manufacturer-attributed **real historical station** with **both seismic and infrasound channel metadata and verified binary recording headers**. This improves the former Raspberry Shake lead, but it **does not turn its older conditional whole-System audit into an automatic PASS**, certify a complete installed system/configuration, validate calibrated geophysical measurements, or establish acceptable republishing rights.

**Gate A evidence-adequacy conclusion:** The four already-selected cases and the Step 02 rubric remain valid for **their declared scopes**, so the 43-step plan **may proceed to Step 05 without fabricating data**. We still lack a broadly complete actual same-unit + configuration + condition + history dataset. That remains an explicit **evidence/association gap** and a ceiling on later empirical validation. `AM.R9D92` is a **supplementary actual two-channel data lead**, not a replacement for Dragino, AirGradient, Campbell or Bennett’s Point. Later work may use its documented source semantics, but any future waveform transformation/publication must independently address rights and the untested decoding/calibration steps; do not automatically open another acquisition campaign.

**Step 04 stopped here.** No other manufacturer candidate was audited; no full station integration, software/firmware reverse engineering, CSAPI writes, profile requirements or public data redistribution occurred.

## 9. Step 05 — Dragino S31-LB source-native reference System

**Status: Step 05 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026.** This is an examination of the **Dragino S31-LB** primary-battery node with a **three-metre external SHT31 probe**, not the integrated-probe S31B, the solar-powered LS, or an authenticated installed device. Its source-native construction, interfaces and messages are described **before** DIM design or CSAPI binding. Reuse the [previous C audit](../Controlled_Corpus/05_Candidate_C_Evidence_Audit.md) and [full itemized System sheet](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md); do not reopen their qualification decisions.

### 9.1 The complete documented arrangement in ordinary language

The manufacturer describes an **S31-LB LoRaWAN end node** with **one separate, cabled SHT31 temperature/humidity probe**. The SHT31 is external to the node's enclosure and connected by a three-metre cable. The node provides the battery, electronics, radio, configurable sampling/reporting logic and LoRaWAN communications. Data travel from the probe to the node, through **external LoRaWAN gateway/network infrastructure**, and onward to a server/application in the documented usage pattern. The example gateway and network server are **communication dependencies**, not established physical constituents of the S31-LB. [Original product page](https://dragino.com/products/temperature-humidity-sensor/item/264-s31-lb-ls.html); [original manual §§1.1–1.8, 2.1–2.3](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/).

**Most important structural fact:** **one physical SHT31 probe measures two properties: temperature and relative humidity.** Two reported quantities or fields do not imply two installed probes, and no independent network identity was established for the probe. The manufacturer explicitly differentiates the **external-probe S31-LB** from the **integrated-probe S31B-LB**, and the primary-battery **LB** from solar-powered **LS**. This statement concerns the documented product configuration, **not** a physically authenticated individual.

### 9.2 Source-native subjects, capabilities and unverified facts

| Source subject | Manufacturer-supported reference meaning | Individual-unit / evidence boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **S31-LB node** | A battery-powered LoRaWAN end node with radio, enclosure/control electronics and registration support; manufacturer supplies model and firmware/region reporting structures. [Sheet C-I01, C-I03, C-I12–C-I17](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#1-identity-construction-communication-and-technical-limits) | No observed physical unit, Device EUI, serial, credentials, board or firmware revision, selected radio band or joined network session. A decoder's hard-coded **Node_type = S31-LB** identifies its output convention, not a unique device. |
| **SHT31 external probe** | One digital temperature/humidity probe, three-metre cable. Specified temperature measurement range **−40 to +80 °C** and stated resolution **0.1 °C**; relative humidity **0–99.9% RH** and stated sensor resolution **0.01% RH**. [Manual §§1.2–1.3, 1.8](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/) | No authenticated probe serial, installation or calibration certificate. The manual's printed ±0.2 temperature-accuracy interval extends to **90 °C**, beyond its own stated +80 °C range: preserve, do not silently repair. Transmitted humidity scaling is **0.1% RH**, distinct from the sensor specification. |
| **Primary power source** | **8,500 mAh** nonrechargeable Li/SOCl₂ battery, **ER26500 + SPC1520** pack, specified supply **2.5–3.6 V**; design sleep/transmit-current claims. [Manual §§1.3, 4; sheet C-I04–C-I06, C-P01–C-P05](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#2-power-and-operating-behavior) | No actual battery voltage, age, charge percentage, lifetime or health. Nameplate capacity is **not remaining capacity**; documented electrical limits are not measured operation. |
| **Wireless/network interfaces** | **LoRaWAN 1.0.3 Class A**, default OTAA join, documented default **20-minute uplinks**, regional variants, **BLE 5.1**, **UART AT commands** and LoRaWAN downlinks for configuration. [Manual §§1.2, 1.5–1.6, 2.1–2.2, 3.1](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/) | No observed gateway, running server, selected interval/region or active connection. The manual's depicted gateway does not become part of the node. |
| **Behavior/configuration** | Supported deep-sleep/working/IDLE modes, button/LED behavior, thresholds, intervals, data logging/recovery, time synchronization, 5-V output duration, interrupt mode, history access and calibration. [Manual §§1.4–1.6, 2.5, 3.3](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/) | No verified effective mode, selected setting, command request, acknowledgement, completed action, applied calibration or measured output. A printed **OK** example is not a reply from our equipment. |

The claims in the middle column are **SOURCE_FACT at manufacturer reference-design scope**. They do **not** assert current state, configuration or physical deployment. Missing unit facts are **UNKNOWN**, not zero, false, a defective device or a failed interoperability test.

### 9.3 The three different message/record forms must remain different

**FPort 2 — measurement and alarm/settings-related formats.** In normal mode, the manufacturer defines **battery voltage**, timestamp, alarm/input/mode bits, **SHT31 temperature** and **SHT31 humidity**. The [pinned Dragino decoder](https://github.com/dragino/dragino-end-node-decoder/blob/635417693adb215b32ac367c8e8909d9d609a120/S31-LB%26S31B-LB/S31-LB_v1.3_TTN_decoder.txt) emits fields including **BatV, Data_time, EXTI_Trigger, Door_status, TempC_SHT31, Hum_SHT31** and a fixed **Node_type**. **Door_status** is a decoder label for an input bit, **not evidence an external door sensor was installed**. A different mode on FPort 2 returns **SHTEMP_MIN/MAX** and **SHHUM_MIN/MAX** alarm-threshold settings: those are **not** fresh environmental observations. Our evidence contains formats and isolated scalar examples, **not a complete original captured FPort-2 sample or alarm reply**. [Sheet C-F01–C-F07 and C-T01–C-T04](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#3-native-message-and-decoder-field-inventory); [manual §2.3](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/).

**FPort 3 — archived/history message.** A history entry comprises **11 bytes** containing ignored leading bytes, humidity, temperature, raw flags and Unix epoch seconds. The manual publishes **one eight-entry demonstration message**, independently expanded in [sheet C-E01–C-E08](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#4-every-record-in-the-original-published-history-example). For instance its first entry converts to **56.6% RH**, **27.2 °C**, Unix epoch **1684899041**, or **2023-05-24T03:30:41Z** after our UTC rendering. The **bytes** are SOURCE_EXAMPLE; their converted numbers/timestamp are **DERIVED**. Neither proves a particular S31-LB recorded them. The pinned decoder emits a concatenated **DATALOG string**, **not** a typed array of independent observation resources; its date-formatting function uses the JavaScript **local** time zone, so the rendered string must not be labeled UTC without an explicit conversion. [Manual §2.5.4](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/); [pinned decoder](https://github.com/dragino/dragino-end-node-decoder/blob/635417693adb215b32ac367c8e8909d9d609a120/S31-LB%26S31B-LB/S31-LB_v1.3_TTN_decoder.txt).

**FPort 5 — requested device-status format.** It can report **model code, firmware version, regional frequency band, sub-band and battery voltage**. That is a **supported status structure**, not an acquired reply from a unit. **Voltage (V) is not remaining battery percentage (%)**, and a device-model code is not a physical-unit serial. No original complete FPort-5 packet belongs to the current evidence set. [Manual §2.3.1](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/); [sheet C-S01–C-S05](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#3-native-message-and-decoder-field-inventory).

**Original source inconsistencies remain visible.** The mode expression **(bytes[6] & 0x7C) >> 2** in the manufacturer decoder yields **decimal 31** for a mode field of 0x7C, whereas the manual prints **0x31** in one explanation; the checked code compares with numeric **31**. A published manual humidity scalar example also contains an arithmetic inconsistency, documented in the data sheet. Do not rewrite the source, infer deployed firmware defects, treat these examples as actual records or promote a convenient decoder interpretation into a normative Profile rule.

### 9.4 What this System supports is not what a unit has done

Documented configuration includes transmit interval (**AT+TDC**, with AT arguments in **milliseconds** versus example downlink intervals in **seconds**), temperature and humidity thresholds (**AT+SHTEMP**, **AT+SHHUM**), alarm spacing (**AT+ATDC**), interrupt mode (**AT+INTMOD**), external 5-V-output duration (**AT+5VT**), history storage/poll/clear, time synchronization (**SYNCMOD**, **PNACKMD**, **AT+SYNCTDC**) and two-point calibration. The [complete source-sheet command index C-C01–C-C18](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#5-configuration-and-command-inventory) retains exact manual sections and units.

**No selected effective setting, acknowledged request, completed action, current diagnostic or actual command outcome was acquired.** Even the documented no-history response of eleven zero bytes must not be interpreted as actual zero-valued environmental readings. The decoder's synthetic FPort-5 tests and its sample **1.0.0**, **EU868** and **2.885 V** values do **not** establish an installed firmware, region or voltage. Default setting ≠ observed setting; supported command ≠ executed action; status-field definition ≠ actual operating status. These **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE** cases are research safeguards, **not** adopted DIM classes or CSAPI Profile SHALLs.

### 9.5 What this arrangement allows us to ask later — not decide today

1. Can one common interpretation preserve **one physical cabled probe and two measured properties** without inventing two independent probes?
2. Can it distinguish **the sensing probe, the node that packages/transmits the values, and external gateway/server infrastructure** while respecting different relationship types?
3. Can it preserve the difference between **source message definitions**, **manufacturer-published examples**, **status possibilities** and **authenticated actual unit history**, including time/identity standing?
4. Can it distinguish **advertised controls/defaults** from **effective configuration**, requested actions, acknowledgements and observed outcomes?
5. Can it keep **design battery capacity** separate from **reported battery voltage and unknown remaining capacity**, and source sensor resolution separate from transmitted field precision?

These questions will be compared with the other equipment cases and donor-native vocabulary in later steps. This step **does not** choose a domain/chassis name, DIM elements, a CSAPI DataStream owner, Profile binding, required client panel or standard conformance outcome.

The connector returned decoder blob **59af776a4b7ce287dc4c3030763e215b31f645f8**, consistent with the preserved 30 October [source-sheet register](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#9-sources-and-preservation). The manual is mutable: reading it now does not authenticate an operating hardware/firmware release.
- **Still unknown:** an authenticated physical unit/probe and individual IDs, installed radio/firmware/configuration, operating status, real samples and history, calibration/maintenance, executed command/acknowledgement/outcome and a real joined network. See [sheet C-G01–C-G08](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md#8-gaps-and-unavailable-material).
- **Result:** **source-native whole-System reference reconstruction completed** using existing source material. This is **not** a DIM/Profile/client conformance test; no new class, SHALL, CSAPI resource, source adapter or Glaux view is adopted or implemented. The original C audit and itemized sheet stay unchanged.
- **Historical handoff after Step 05 (Step 06 now completed in [§10](#10-step-06--airgradient-open-air-max-source-native-reference-system)):** reconstruct **AirGradient Open Air Max** as a documented multi-board/multi-sensor reference arrangement, with outputs, configuration/communications/power and clear actual-unit unknowns. Do not start Campbell or donor adjudication at the same time.


## 10. Step 06 — AirGradient Open Air Max source-native reference System

**Status: Step 06 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026.** **Bounded subject:** the source-described, gas-equipped **AirGradient Open Air Max O-M-1PPSTON-CE** assembled reference configuration. This is **not** an identified fielded monitor, an asserted matched hardware/firmware build, the non-gas model, or proof of interchangeability with the commercial **O-M-1PPSTON-CE-S** suffix. The purpose is to understand the manufacturer's *whole reference arrangement* before choosing DIM meanings or CSAPI Profile representations. Reuse the [AirGradient original-source audit](../Controlled_Corpus/03_Candidate_A_Evidence_Audit.md) and [complete itemized source sheet](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md); the previous evidence qualification and its gaps are unchanged.

### 10.1 Picture the manufacturer's assembly, not a cloud field list

AirGradient describes a **weatherproof outdoor air-quality monitor** built around an **ESP32-C6 main printed circuit board (PCB)**. The main board includes processor, communications and power-support circuitry and connectors for several **physically distinguishable sensing modules**. The reference assembly includes **two direct-connected Plantower PMS5003 particulate sensors**, a **SUNX module containing SenseAir Sunlight CO₂ sensing**, an **SGP41 module** for raw volatile-organic-compound/nitrogen-oxide channels, an **SHT40/SHT4x module** for temperature and relative humidity, and an **AlphaSense analogue-to-digital (ADC) module with the named NO₂ A43F and O₃ A431 electrochemical elements**. A **separate 4G LTE Cat 1 cellular card** plugs into the main board. The enclosure supports wall or pole mounting; a solar panel and three-cell battery arrangement support autonomous operation. [Original hardware README, pinned commit 89aacf5, Overview / Key components / Plug-in modules](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md).

**The key difference from Step 05's Dragino:** this *one documented monitor assembly* contains **more than one physical particulate sensing device**, **other physical sensing modules**, an embedded controller and several external/internal communication or power paths. Yet the many reported PM, gas, environmental and diagnostic fields cannot be counted as one physical part per output. **Two PMS5003 devices do not become PM1, PM2.5, PM10 and particle-count “sensors” each simply because those quantities appear in a report.** The source does not establish that every module has an independent network identity or that there is one independently discoverable API resource per physical component.

The source-native arrangement can be read as: **sensing devices/modules → local board/controllers and measurement processing → transport-specific device messages → carrier/network → separate cloud/dashboard or configurable endpoint**. The solar panel, cell pack, charger and power switches support the monitor; the carrier, server, dashboard and physical mounting site are **dependencies/context**, not asserted internal parts. The source does not prove a real installed station or any particular device-to-cloud message journey. This is a *documented design arrangement*, not a DIM part-of graph or a CSAPI resource design.

### 10.2 Physical constituents, functions, interfaces and evidence

| Manufacturer subject / connection | What the source supports (**SOURCE_FACT**, product/design scope) | What we must not infer |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Assembled monitor / main board** | Gas-equipped **O-M-1PPSTON-CE** hardware description; **ESP32-C6-WROOM-1-N8** on the main PCB, switched sensor rails, watchdog, UART bridge, USB-C/solar charging circuitry, battery management and module sockets. [Hardware README §§Overview, Design structure, Key components](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md) | No physically inspected board, hardware revision, firmware release match, manufacturing serial or installed inventory. The source's hardware BQ25798 charger and separately inspected firmware **BQ25672** class are not a verified chip equivalence or demonstrated incompatibility. |
| **Particulate sensing** | **Two PMS5003 physical devices** attached directly to PM1 and PM2 sockets using UART via a WK2132 bridge; not separate daughterboards. The [pinned firmware README §Sensor Support](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/firmware-openair-max/blob/84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc/README.md) additionally describes per-port fallbacks to **SPS30** and mixed support. | The documented reference remains a **PMS5003 pair**. A firmware-supported SPS30 alternative is **not** proof of its installation. Multiple PM output quantities are not hardware identities, and the source excerpts do not establish a general per-sensor API/aggregation rule. |
| **CO₂ sensing** | **SenseAir Sunlight nondispersive infrared (NDIR)** device on the SUNX daughterboard, local UART connection; product design specifies 0–5,000 ppm. [Hardware README, Plug-in modules](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md) | A manufacturer capability/range does not provide a tested CO₂ concentration, calibration state or unit identity. |
| **TVOC/NOx sensing** | **Sensirion SGP41 module**, I²C connection, two named raw channel families in the firmware examples. [Hardware README](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md); [firmware README payload examples](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/firmware-openair-max/blob/84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc/README.md) | Two reported channel labels are **not two discrete SGP41 physical modules**; raw values are not the same as index or ambient concentration outputs. |
| **Temperature and relative humidity** | One **SHT40/SHT4x module**, I²C; temperature and humidity are distinct properties from one documented sensing module. [Hardware README, Plug-in modules](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md) | Two physical modules are not implied by the two measured properties. No installed serial or independent performance record. |
| **NO₂ and O₃ gas sensing** | **AlphaSense ADC assembly** with separately named **NO₂ A43F** and **O₃ A431** sensing elements, reporting working/auxiliary electrode signals. Local ADC/interface assembly is different from the chemical elements and from reported concentrations. [Hardware README](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md) | Raw electrode millivolts ≠ processed parts-per-billion concentration; independent calibration and validity are not established. No unit serial or verified gas result. |
| **Cellular/Wi-Fi and expansion** | Plug-in **CE card** for **LTE Cat 1** and onboard **2.4 GHz Wi-Fi**; extra I2C3 and IO1 connectors appear as **spare expansion** positions. External carrier/cloud and user destination are separate from the enclosure. [Hardware README](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md) | An unused expansion connector does **not** establish installed additional sensors. Neither network mode nor a live network/cloud session was observed. |

**Source-edition guard:** the hardware README's pinned commit is **89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983** (blob **6a915a09e7d34bd11b6b0dbe6698898d36976466**); the independently pinned firmware README commit is **84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc** (blob **1322b58c7ed70ec0f4900bd557ec09fa65e03732**). The [current commercial product page](https://www.airgradient.com/professional/products/open-air-max/) uses **O-M-1PPSTON-CE-S** and says approximately 10,000 mAh, whereas the pinned hardware describes **three 3,500 mAh 18650 cells connected in series**. Preserve those distinct source statements; **do not treat the product suffixes as identical or add series-connected cell capacities** to invent a battery-pack rating.

### 10.3 Power, communications, collection and reporting are different arrangements

**Power path and limits.** The original hardware README describes a **10 W solar panel**, a **three-cell 18650 Li-ion series pack**, battery-management circuitry and solar/USB-C charging. The [manufacturer power guide](https://www.airgradient.com/documentation/kb/airgradient-open-air-max-power-battery-backup-and-solar) describes an included **10 W / 18 V panel**. **USB-C can power the monitor with the battery switch OFF**, but both **battery backup and charging require the switch ON**; solar use requires the panel connection and the switch ON. These are **supported physical choices and operating instructions**, not a recorded switch position, battery condition, charging session or maintenance action. The product page warns that the **current hardware version** does not charge its internal batteries at or below 0 °C; that is a product-version claim, not a verified field deployment temperature record.

**Local sensing versus remote report.** The [pinned firmware README](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/firmware-openair-max/blob/84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc/README.md) describes a **default cellular path**: normally collect **three-minute** sensor sets and send a group of **three sets at approximately nine-minute intervals**, via HTTP or MQTT. The *cellular device example* is **52 comma-delimited integers**: first an interval **180 seconds**, then **three sets of 17 values** with defined scalings. It does not carry a full absolute timestamp, serial, signed calibration record or identified individual device in that example body. The **cellular loss buffer** can resend unsent sets later, but the firmware documentation says it is **volatile across power loss**; this is not evidence of guaranteed delivery.

**Different Wi-Fi device path.** The firmware README's Wi-Fi path is **HTTP JSON**, normally **one 17-field sample every three minutes** and **no local retransmission cache**. Its annotated example includes **rco2, atmp, rhum, pm01, pm25, pm10, tvocRaw, noxRaw, pm003Count, wifi, volt, light, measure0–measure4**. The example contains explanatory comments, so removing comments to parse it as strict JSON is **DERIVED** test preparation, not evidence that actual Wi-Fi devices send invalid JSON. The original data sheet [§3 lists all 17 fields and corresponding cellular sequence/scaling](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md#3-complete-device-example-field-inventory). A different network route and data schema are not two separate monitors or two independently captured histories.

**Timing depends on stated firmware context.** AirGradient's [separate power-mode guide](https://www.airgradient.com/documentation/kb/airgradient-open-air-max-status-led-and-reporting-intervals), explicitly **firmware 0.7.0 onward**, gives standard **cellular 3-minute measurement / 9-minute transmission**, standard **Wi-Fi 3/3**, **9.5–9.9 V** power-saving **cellular 30/90**, and **less than 9.5 V** deep power-saving **cellular 60/180** (minutes). It describes **90-minute** standard configuration/update checks and **daily** checks in power-saving modes, while the independently pinned README describes **hourly** configuration and firmware checks. Both source contexts are retained; no active firmware, applied threshold, selected mode, undocumented voltage-boundary behavior or generalized Wi-Fi-saving mode is asserted. [Source-sheet A-M01–A-M04](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md#power-mode-guide-a07-explicitly-firmware-070-onward).

### 10.4 Outputs, example values, cloud representations and quality

The two *device documentation examples* and two *manufacturer-published cloud API examples* are **different provenance and interface contexts**, **not one observed device run**. In the device Wi-Fi example, **atmp = 28.5 °C**, **rhum = 75.1%**, **pm25 = 27.7 µg/m³**, **volt = 11.24 V** and **light = 1.4 V**; its cellular counterpart illustrates raw integer scaling, including **285/10 = 28.5 °C** and **1124/100 = 11.24 V**. The first cellular panel entry is **14/100 = 0.14 V**, **not the same** as the separately authored Wi-Fi **1.4 V**. They cannot be silently synchronized or “fixed” by guesswork. The source data sheet keeps all 17 device-example fields and three cellular groups; these numbers are **SOURCE_EXAMPLE** with any arithmetic explicitly **DERIVED**, not real telemetry. [Original firmware README, both Example Payload Breakdown sections](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/firmware-openair-max/blob/84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc/README.md).

The manufacturer's [15 September 2026 public forum reply #3](https://forum.airgradient.com/t/max-api-working-example-with-o-and-no-data/7345) publishes **two separate cloud response examples**: a *public/world-location* path with **28 keys**, and a *location* path with **36 keys**, **43 distinct keys** overall. They include different source names such as device **pm25** versus cloud **pm02**, model/location data, illustrative timestamps and distinct raw/corrected fields. The location example has a placeholder **serialno**, coordinates **0.0 / 0.0**, **firmwareVersion = null**, and quoted **batteryVoltage/panelVoltage** strings. The public example has **offline = false**, a service-level example flag—not proof of real installed physical health or a completed polling cycle. The different examples are **not joined time-series records**, and a key correspondence is **not** an executed producer-to-cloud crosswalk. [Complete native cloud field inventory A-API01–A-API43](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md#4-complete-manufacturer-cloud-example-inventory).

Keep **what was sensed**, **what was reported**, and **what processing is established** distinct. The firmware calls **tvocRaw/noxRaw** raw channel outputs; cloud examples also expose **tvocIndex/noxIndex** and **O₃/NO₂ concentration-looking values**. **measure0–measure3** (or the cloud electrode names) describe gas **working/auxiliary electrode signals**, not independently verified ambient ppb concentrations. **measure4 / afeTemp** has unresolved physical-unit/temperature conversion in the source record; do **not** render it as °C by analogy. The cloud **corrected** key suffixes name derived-looking outputs but do not establish an algorithm, input lineage or tested calibration applied to this example. Index-labeled example magnitudes exceed expected common index scales; do not silently normalize them. [Source sheet §§3–4, A-G04–A-G05](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md#6-explicit-gaps--items-withheld-from-stronger-use).

**A particularly important manufacturer caveat:** in [forum replies #5–#6, 17 September 2026](https://forum.airgradient.com/t/max-api-working-example-with-o-and-no-data/7345), AirGradient explained that some zero gas values reflected **missing calibration parameters**, advised **ignoring O₃ at that time** while its correction factor was unfinished, and qualified possible negative NO₂ values. These are **dated source warnings**, not an assertion that all future hardware or outputs are invalid. The two examples therefore show usable **field shapes, source labels and distinct processing stages**, but **not validated air-quality truth** for these gas values. Similarly, a local error-LED description is a **supported diagnostic indicator**, not acquired health history; [source sheet A-L01–A-L13](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md#local-indicator-meanings-complete-a02-first-boot-list) preserves the exact documented patterns.

### 10.5 Configurable controls and missing real operating state

The pinned firmware documents a **System Settings Portal**, started by holding the boot button for at least **five seconds**, for selecting cellular versus Wi-Fi transport, cellular APN, Wi-Fi credentials and the HTTP destination. [Configuration.cpp lines 95–135 at the pinned commit](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/firmware-openair-max/blob/84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc/main/Configuration.cpp#L95-L135) parses keys including **co2CalibrationRequested**, **abcDays** and **ledBarTestRequested**. Its existence in code means these **requests/settings are supported by the inspected implementation path**. It does **not** prove a specific monitor received a configuration, acknowledged it, applied it, successfully performed a calibration or completed an LED test. Firmware-over-the-air checks and local LED patterns are similarly **supported behavior**, not actual events in a verified device log.

No **authenticated physical monitor** or full installed module/serial inventory, selected battery switch, regional radio/carrier registration, boot version, matched hardware/firmware release, selected cloud endpoint, running configuration, actual command/outcome, measurement history, diagnostic record or calibration certificate was acquired in this step. The previously unsuccessful public lab API access remains **historical unverified acquisition evidence**, **not proof the endpoint is offline**, and **not an acquisition task restarted here**. The previously disputed manufacturer PDF editions remain excluded from positive edition-specific claims. These are **UNKNOWN**, never zero, absent equipment or observed failures. [Source audit §F](../Controlled_Corpus/03_Candidate_A_Evidence_Audit.md#f-open-issues-and-deliberate-exclusions); [source sheet §6](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md#6-explicit-gaps--items-withheld-from-stronger-use).

### 10.6 What this source arrangement asks of later common interpretation

- Can one description distinguish the **whole enclosure/monitor, main board, two actual particulate sensors, daughterboards, chemical sensing elements, power equipment, spare sockets and external services**, rather than counting each output as another hardware unit?
- Can the same proposed meaning preserve **a physical sensor or module producing several properties**, **a pair of physical sensors supporting a class of reported quantities**, and **raw electrode signals versus processed/calibrated gas quantities** without assuming an aggregation or correction formula?
- Can different **cellular device batches**, **single-reading Wi-Fi device JSON**, **cloud-location examples** and **dashboard fields** be kept as distinct **reporting representations with their native keys, units, time semantics, provenance and source-version context**?
- Can documented **transport choices, battery/panel design, power-mode rules, error LEDs and requested calibrations** remain different from **selected settings, observed readings, executed actions, verified health and completed outcomes**?
- Can the interpretation preserve **unknown installed identity and firmware, gas-calibration caveats, unresolved AFE units and contradictory source timing/variant statements** rather than filling them with attractive guesses?

These are *source-native questions* for the Step 09 comparison and later fair donor work, **not adopted answers**. No preferred chassis/domain name, DIM entity, Profile SHALL, CSAPI resource/DataStream ownership, semantic rule or Glaux UI behavior has been selected or executed.

The GitHub source blobs match the pre-existing audit/source-sheet preservation references. **No downloaded PDF edition, complete schematics, device execution, live lab query or firmware build/compatibility test was added.** Mutable website claims remain edition-scoped by inspection date.

## 11. Step 07 — Campbell CR1000X and first-generation ClimaVue 50 source-native arrangement

**Status: Step 07 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026.** **Case boundary:** a manufacturer-documented reference pairing of **one CR1000X measurement/control datalogger** and **one first-generation ClimaVue 50 compact weather-sensing assembly** connected through an SDI-12 cable/interface. It requires suitable logger power/protection and sensor mounting, but **no particular station, enclosure SKU, battery/solar arrangement, network service or physical unit was identified**. This is neither a CR1000Xe nor a ClimaVue 50 G2, nor a verified operating installation. Reuse the existing [source audit](../Controlled_Corpus/06_Candidate_D_Evidence_Audit.md) and [source-native itemized sheet](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md) rather than restarting qualification.

### 11.1 One weather-sensing assembly and a separately programmable logger

**The ClimaVue 50 senses.** Campbell describes a single all-in-one weather instrument with functions for precipitation, solar radiation, sonic wind speed/direction, air temperature, relative humidity, vapor/barometric pressure, lightning and tilt. Its **aR7! command defines fourteen ordered output values**, but **fourteen quantities are not fourteen independent installed weather sensors**. It also distinguishes **air temperature** from **humidity-sensor temperature**. We have definitions of the outputs, not measured values from an identified individual. [Campbell, ClimaVue 50 Product Manual, 08/2024, §§5, 8 and Table 8-1, printed pp. 21–23](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=25).

**The CR1000X requests, processes and records.** A CRBasic program in a physically separate datalogger sends SDI-12 measurement requests, receives sensor reply values, assigns them to variables, can transform or aggregate them, and can store them in data tables. The datalogger also has its **own** supply and internal battery-voltage fields, panel temperature, program/scan state, ports, communications and diagnostics. **Logger PanelTemp is the temperature of the logger wiring panel, not ClimaVue outdoor air temperature.** Logger **WatchdogErrors** is not the same source subject as a ClimaVue **aV!** sensor watchdog result. [Original 9/2019 CR1000X example program, Appendix B](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/eu/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=43); [CR1000X Status Table](https://help.campbellsci.com/crbasic/cr1000x/Content/Instructions/StatusTable.htm).

**The source-native flow is:** **weather-sensing functions within the ClimaVue assembly → its addressed SDI-12 responses → the separately powered CR1000X running a selected program → logger variables/calculations and data tables → optional logger reporting/network service**. This is a **documented possible arrangement**, **not** a tested connection from a real station. The physical sensor, cable, datalogger, support power and remote broker are not one undifferentiated component. Nor are the individual weather functions proven independent addressable units.

### 11.2 What is physically connected, supported or unknown?

| Source-native item | **SOURCE_FACT** for the documented design/reference | Unverified actual-unit condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **ClimaVue 50 assembly and power** | One first-generation integrated sensing assembly, cable and continuous power. Published sensor range **3.6–15.0 Vdc**; the example connection uses **12V**. [08/2024 manual §§6–7](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=16) | No actual sensor serial, installed revision, voltage reading, mounting, calibration or maintenance record. |
| **CR1000X logger** | Separate programmable measurement/processing/recording device; suitable external logger supply, nominal 12 Vdc context, protection and chosen terminals are part of a workable reference configuration. [Earlier D audit §A](../Controlled_Corpus/06_Candidate_D_Evidence_Audit.md#a-case-boundary-and-comparability) | No logger serial, selected enclosure/power accessory, firmware, running program, actual measured panel/battery values or installed communications hardware. |
