| **Four conductor/return functions** | [Manual Table 7-1, printed p. 13](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=17): **white = SDI-12 signal** to configured C/U terminal; **clear shield = G**; **brown = 12V**; **black power ground = G**. | Manufacturer wiring instructions **do not verify physical installation** or wire continuity at a particular station. |
| **Sensor SDI-12 logical address** | Factory **default 0**. Multiple sensors may share a supported SDI-12 bus only when their **addresses are distinct**. Address can be queried/changed; a programmed logger chooses which address to contact. [08/2024 manual §7.1, Table 8-1](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=17) | Address **0** is a default/example, not a verified deployed address, not a serial number and not a CSAPI System identity. |
| **Program choice and schedule** | [Historical 9/2019 Appendix B](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/eu/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=44) shows **C1, address 0 and a 60-second logger scan**, plus hourly/daily data tables. Manual guidance recommends approximately 60-second scan use and more than ten seconds between scans. | The program is **SOURCE_EXAMPLE**, not an applied configuration or compiled/run code. Scan period is not automatically the sensor's internal phenomenon-time interval. |
| **Network/reporting context** | A [later Campbell MQTT API Status guide](https://help.campbellsci.com/datalogger-api/mqtt/status.html) documents publishing **logger status** to a topic under a configured base topic/device identifier and via a selected interval. | No MQTT broker, connection, selected logger reporting mode, HTTP endpoint or actual end-to-end ClimaVue-to-cloud records are established for this pairing. |
| **Site and servicing** | Original manual documents leveling/orientation, mounting and maintenance requirements. Its rainfall measurement needs the instrument close to level; ice, heavy wind/rain and blockage can affect measurements. [08/2024 manual §§2, 7–9](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf) | No installed mast geometry, orientation/tilt logs, weather exposure, repair/cleaning history or site position. |

### 11.3 Two distinct data families: sensor responses versus datalogger status

**Sensor weather response — fourteen defined output positions, no acquired response.** The [original 08/2024 Table 8-1](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=25) lists **aR7!** values, in order: (1) solar flux density **W/m²**; (2) precipitation **mm**; (3) lightning strikes **count**; (4) strike distance **km**; (5) wind speed **m/s**; (6) wind direction **degrees**; (7) ten-second gust **m/s**; (8) air temperature **°C**; (9) vapor pressure **kPa**; (10) **absolute** barometric pressure **kPa**; (11) relative humidity as a **fraction 0–1**, not percent; (12) humidity-sensor temperature **°C**; (13) north/south tilt **degrees**; and (14) west/east tilt **degrees**. These are **source definitions**, **not** fourteen measurements acquired in this project. Other **aM!, aM1!, aM3!, aC!** command groups are alternative supported readout groupings, not a physical inventory. [Source sheet D-R01–D-R14](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md#2-every-climavue-ar7-response-position).

**Sensor identity and diagnostics — definitions, no acquired reply.** Table 8-1 defines **aI!** for an address/model/serial-containing identification string; **aV!** for **sensor OS version, hardware version, sensor watchdog errors and uptime**; **?!** for address query; and **aAb!** for setting a new address. A model label, an SDI-12 bus address and a sensor serial have different referents. **No real aI!/aV! response, actual serial, installed software version or successful address-change event was recorded.** [08/2024 manual, Table 8-1, printed pp. 22–23](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=26); [source sheet §3](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md#3-sensor-identity-verification-and-controls).

**Not every returned value is a new measurement.** The manual states the **aR7!** request resets internal averaging/totalizing/maximizing and may return **the previous value** if the relevant new measurement was not made, with a special exception for the interrupt-based lightning strike count. Consequently, **response receipt time is not proof of a fresh phenomenon time**. A logger program's 60-second scan is a selected design setting, not a universal sensor internal sampling rate. [08/2024 manual, Table 8-1 footnotes, printed p. 23](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=27).

**Separate CR1000X status example — not ClimaVue weather.** Campbell's [MQTT API Status reference](https://help.campbellsci.com/datalogger-api/mqtt/status.html) publishes a **GeoJSON-shaped logger-status example** with **31 ordered field names paired positionally with 31 example values** (18 numbers, 3 strings, 10 Booleans). The [JSON format documentation](https://help.campbellsci.com/datalogger-api/mqtt/json_data_formats.html) explains the positional pairing. In that *publisher example*, **PanelTemp = 25.91098 °C** means **logger wiring-panel temperature**, **Battery = 13.2368 Vdc** means **logger system supply voltage**, **LithiumBattery = 3.70477 V** refers to the internal lithium battery, and **SW12Volts(1/2) = true** reports switched-output **state**, not a measured 12-V value. The **loggerID ABCD-EFGH-1234**, **coordinates [0,0,0]** and timestamp **2025-06-04T18:29:23.271Z** are illustrative. They **do not** identify or locate a verified ClimaVue station or its recorded weather measurements. [CR1000X Status Table definitions](https://help.campbellsci.com/crbasic/cr1000x/Content/Instructions/StatusTable.htm); [source sheet D-S01–D-S31](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md#5-complete-cr1000x-status-example-inventory).

**Do not join these examples.** The **2019 pairing-specific program**, the separately offered **2021 program download**, the **2024 sensor manual**, and the **2026 MQTT logger-status example** are **different documents/editions/subjects**. No manufacturer evidence shows that the displayed status example was generated by that program or belonged to a logger with an attached ClimaVue. The source sheet retains **13 status-example fields** whose full detailed meaning/units were not established by the limited D06 extraction. Do not guess their meaning or treat 31 status values as 31 environmental observations.

### 11.4 A logger program can create new reported meanings

The [9/2019 original manual, Appendix B, printed B-1–B-3](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/eu/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=43), provides an actual **published CR1000X program listing**, but **no program execution**. It shows a fourteen-position **CVData array**, **C1/address 0**, **60-second scan**, hourly/daily tables and separate logger **Battery()** and **PanelTemp()** calls. It also illustrates a daily **I!** sensor-identity query to check for a possible sensor swap or OS update. **This is a proposed/programmed check in a manufacturer example, not an acquired replacement log or real sensor serial history.**

The program **converts relative humidity fraction into percent**, **converts pressure units and calculates a sea-level adjustment using an assumed 1,382 m site elevation**, derives a solar-energy total using the scan period, and **substitutes the preceding valid wind values** when certain wind readings are invalid/negative. A **carried-forward wind value is not newly measured wind**, and a **sea-level-adjusted pressure result is not the directly reported sensor absolute pressure**. The assumptions, source values and transformations must remain distinguishable. [2019 Appendix B B-1–B-3](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/eu/manuals/climavue50.pdf#page=43); [2021 program-download description](https://www.campbellsci.com/resources/software/climavue50-example-program).

**A bounded original-source verification improved today:** the **2019 EU PDF cover and program pages B-1–B-3 were inspected as actual page images**. The program on printed B-1 visibly contains the literal **“Cont SeaLevelAdj=15.29091”**, which previously could only be seen in extracted text. We can now say that **this exact printed anomaly is real, not merely a text-extraction error**. It remains unclear whether the manufacturer intended different source syntax, whether the whole program compiles, and how it compares with the unavailable **2021 v1 packaged program**. **Do not silently repair or claim the program is runnable.** The original audit remains a dated record of what was known at its time.

### 11.5 Unverified operating configuration and questions for later work

**The whole physical reference arrangement is supportable, but the actual operating System is not reconstructed.** We have no authenticated logger serial, sensor serial, selected SDI-12 address, physical cable/power inspection, installed CRBasic program, matched OS/revision, actual sensor weather response, full station status, verified control exchange, measurement history, field-quality record, geographic position or maintenance timeline. The manufacturer's published logger-status example, even though formatted as JSON with timestamps and a geometry, is **SOURCE_EXAMPLE**, not an actual station record. **UNKNOWN is not zero, false, failed equipment or evidence of no capability.**

**Source-driven comparison questions (not new DIM conclusions):**

1. Can a common model distinguish **one multifunction sensing assembly** from the **separate logger that powers/addresses, processes, stores and optionally publishes its values**, without counting 14 outputs as 14 physical Systems?
2. Can it distinguish the **sensor model, serial and SDI-12 address**, the **logger identity/program**, and a possible **MQTT topic**, including their different lifetime/uniqueness scopes?
3. Can sensor-reported **absolute pressure**, logger-computed **sea-level pressure**, raw **fractional humidity** and converted **percent humidity** remain traceable instead of being labeled the same result?
4. Can it explain **sensor-local uptime/watchdog** independently of the **logger's Status Table**, and separate **fresh measurement** from **previous or program-carried-forward value**?
5. Can the system keep manufacturer **design/default/example information** separate from an **observed individual installation, commanded action, executed configuration and verified history**?

No DIM root/chassis, physical/functional classes, CSAPI resource/DataStream ownership, Profile binding, normative requirement or Glaux Resource Details allocation is adopted here. Later donor analysis must be allowed to change the provisional interpretations.

### 11.6 Source standing, verification and handoff

**Primary sources reread 1 November 2026:** Campbell Scientific, [ClimaVue 50 Product Manual revision 08/2024](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf), printed Table 7-1 p. 13 and Table 8-1 pp. 21–23 (**PDF pages 17, 25–27 visually checked**); Campbell Scientific, [ClimaVUE 50 European manual revision 9/19](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/eu/manuals/climavue50.pdf), original cover and Appendix B B-1–B-3 (**PDF pages 1, 43–45 visually checked**); [ClimaVue 50 Example Program v1 description, 8 July 2021](https://www.campbellsci.com/resources/software/climavue50-example-program) (content still login-gated/unobtained); [CR1000X Status Table](https://help.campbellsci.com/crbasic/cr1000x/Content/Instructions/StatusTable.htm); [MQTT API logger status example](https://help.campbellsci.com/datalogger-api/mqtt/status.html); and [JSON Data Formats](https://help.campbellsci.com/datalogger-api/mqtt/json_data_formats.html). The source-audit and sheet locators were retained. **No full publisher PDF/program was redistributed**. No live status request, hardware test, program compilation or new decoder run was performed.

**Step 07 deliverable:** one **source-native whole-reference-arrangement reconstruction** in this existing workbook. The earlier D audit, source sheet, preserved corpus and candidate DIM/Profile files are unchanged. **No app/Glaux code, Cloudflare deployment, live CSAPI server resource or new Profile requirement was changed or tested.**

**Historical handoff after Step 07; Step 08 now complete in [§12](#12-step-08--bennetts-point-actual-station-time-scoped-probes-and-historical-temperature-reports):** reconstruct **Bennett’s Point, ACE Basin** as a *genuine but bounded historical station/logger/probe and equipment-replacement case*. Separate the two real source temperature measurements from the equipment-use logs and **project-derived probe attribution**, and keep the **station-led versus probe-led DataStream reporting choice** explicitly unresolved. Stop before Step 09 four-case comparison.


## 12. Step 08 — Bennett's Point: actual station, time-scoped probes and historical temperature reports

**Status: Step 08 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026.** **Scope:** the genuine **2024 Bennett's Point / acebpmet meteorological station history**, focusing on two selected air-temperature summary records on either side of the **16 April 2024 EE181 probe replacement**. Unlike the manufacturer-only reference arrangements in Steps 05–07, the reserve documents an actual station, a separately identifiable datalogger, individually serialized sensing probes, replacement/maintenance periods and archived station summaries. **This does not constitute a complete authenticated operating-System dataset or prove every part of the installed station.** No DIM, Profile or CSAPI ownership decision is made here.

### 12.1 What physically existed, what it did, and who published the history

The **ACE Basin National Estuarine Research Reserve** describes a persistent monitoring station at **Bennett's Point**, code **acebpmet**, which monitors the surrounding **environmental air**, not the equipment's own temperature. The operator's [2024 meteorological metadata, sections 2–6, printed pp. 1–3](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=2), identifies meteorological sensing equipment, a tower, a protected Campbell datalogger, backup storage, software, telemetry and reporting processes. The dated metadata is a **source account of historical operations**, not a 2026 live inventory or a deployed CSAPI Systems graph.

**Physical arrangement:** the metadata locates the *station* at **32.55934° N, 80.45456° W**, describes a **6.1 m tower** and a temperature/relative-humidity instrument mounted at approximately **3.3–3.35 m**, plus other weather sensors, an enclosure, power/solar support and GOES telemetry. These are **station-level, operator-documented site and mounting facts**. Neither the two CSV rows nor the deployed test CSAPI probe resources independently carry geodetic coordinates for the **particular physical EE181 serials**. **Do not copy the station location into each probe System as its verified individual position** or invent a Deployment geometry. The earlier live Glaux Map's truthful *no System position* fallback is therefore compatible with a documented **station** location. [Operator metadata §5, printed pp. 2–3](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=3); [Bennett map provenance](https://github.com/OS4CSAPI/glaux-client/blob/work/details-view-20261023/Docs/Design/BENNETT_LIVE_MAP_INTEGRATION.md).

**The logger is not the EE181.** The operator identifies **Campbell CR1000X logger 2, serial 13916**, in use **4 May 2022–still in use as of 31 December 2024**, alongside a listed **inactive CR1000X serial 13915**. It also identifies **Campbell TX321 GOES transmitter serial 2335**, installed on 4 May 2022. The logger's **CR1000X.Std.07.02** operating-system update is dated **17 April 2024**; two program file names appear in the record, but this document is **not** an actual complete program/configuration export for each selected measurement. These dated individual hardware roles differ from the **manufacturer-only ClimaVue 50/CR1000X reference** in Step 07; **there is no ClimaVue installation asserted for Bennett's Point**. [Operator metadata §9, printed pp. 10–11](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=11).

**The documented reporting path has stages:** actual temperature/humidity and other instruments are wired to the datalogger; the CR1000X executes the **CDMO-supplied** meteorological sampling/logging program, collects **5-second** input data and stores **15-minute** summary outputs. The **GOES transmitter** sends four 15-minute summary sets on an **hourly** schedule toward NOAA; separately, local LoggerNet/card/USB workflows support download and backup. The Centralized Data Management Office (CDMO) and reserve apply automated and further quality-assurance/quality-control review and publish the archive. Thus **physical sensing, computing an average, storing it, transferring it and publishing an archival record are distinguishable source roles**. We have not replayed those specific hardware transfers or authenticated each original 5-second sample. [Operator metadata §§2, 4, printed pp. 1–2](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=2).

### 12.2 The same station used two different individual EE181 probes

**The physical model is constant; the individual equipment is not.** [Operator equipment-use history, §9, printed p. 6](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=6) names two serials of Campbell Scientific's **EE181 temperature/humidity product kind**:

| Source-labeled probe and unique serial | Dated use at Bennett's Point | What follows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **EE181 Temp/RH sensor 4 — 222016000109CE** | **8 June 2023–16 April 2024**; last calibration listed **20 May 2022** | It is the documented **earlier physical unit** for our selected 15 April temperature interval. A use-end *date* is not an exact replacement clock time. |
| **EE181 Temp/RH sensor 3 — 21281600077814** | **4 May 2022–8 June 2023**; then **16 April 2024–still in use as of 31 December 2024**; last calibration **4 March 2024** | The returning unit is the **same identified physical probe** reused in a later interval, not a newly created third unit because it has two periods of service. As-of-2024 is **not** a claim of current 2026 installation. |

The operator's earlier history also used **sensor-number labels in previous model eras**: a label such as “sensor 3” by itself must never replace the **model and actual serial** as the physical-identity evidence. This is a source-based warning about relying on local labels, not a prescribed global DIM identifier policy.

**What exactly happened on 16 April:** The operator reports the CR1000X powered down at **09:45 EST** for a **multi-instrument** replacement/maintenance window, with work complete and the logger back on at **13:30 EST**. The records describe a calibrated temperature/humidity instrument being installed, along with barometric, wind and photosynthetically active radiation sensor work and a rain-gauge calibration attempt. The **13:45** station summary was rejected due to uncertainty in averaging after maintenance. The selected equipment-use histories connect the **EE181 unit 4 → unit 3** transition to **16 April**, but **no individual probe-disconnection or reconnection minute** was supplied; do not set it to 09:45, 13:30 or midnight. A separate **17 April** logger operating-system update and the rejected **12:00** reading are **not an additional probe replacement**. [Operator metadata §13, printed p. 13](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=13).

### 12.3 Two actual archived station-temperature records — and a separate attribution step

The project previously acquired the [2024 NOAA/CDMO station archive](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/swmp_data_archives/acebpmet2024.csv), recorded as **5,260,824 bytes**, SHA-256 **3f3ee3e5458c00adee770dc9b18a6246e590937a3f0c6c5f48cfc73cd8ae4950** on 31 October 2026, and preserved the *selected raw source fields* in [this pinned evidence record](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json). **In Step 08 the operator's original PDF and NOAA parameter definitions were directly rechecked, but the current CSV download could not be fetched through the available tools** (unsupported binary/octet-stream through web; local DNS/download failure). We therefore reuse the **previously captured, source-attributed rows and fingerprint** rather than claiming to have freshly checked current upstream CSV bytes. CDMO may subsequently revise the CSV; this is the original snapshot's evidence standing.

| Earlier preserved CSV line and source station stamp | Actual archived air-temperature mean | Original source temperature quality token | Independently derived nominal mean window (UTC) | **Project attribution from equipment-use history** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **10130**; **15 Apr 2024, 12:00 EST** | **29.1 °C** | **F_ATemp = “<0> ”**, including the trailing space | **15 Apr 2024, 16:45–17:00 UTC** | **EE181 sensor 4, serial 222016000109CE** |
| **10418**; **18 Apr 2024, 12:00 EST** | **27.3 °C** | **F_ATemp = “<0> ”**, including the trailing space | **18 Apr 2024, 16:45–17:00 UTC** | **EE181 sensor 3, serial 21281600077814** |

**Keep original fields and derived claims visibly separate.** The saved CSV fields include **StationCode = “acebpmet  ”** (source padding), **DatetimeStamp**, **Frequency = 15**, **ATemp** (°C), **F_ATemp** and a separate blank **F_Record**. Each **DatetimeStamp** belongs to the station and is reported in **Eastern Standard Time (EST, UTC−05:00) all year**, **not daylight-saving local time**; conversion to **17:00 UTC** is **DERIVED**. The [NOAA/CDMO parameter reference](https://www.nerrsdata.org/data/parameters.cfm) explicitly states that meteorological averages apply to the interval **preceding the source timestamp**. Hence the **16:45–17:00 UTC** windows are **project-calculated nominal 15-minute periods**, **not** original per-sample timestamps printed in the CSV. The source records identify the **station and air-temperature field**, **not the probe serial**. Attribution is a **DERIVED time/role/station join** to the operator's separate dated probe-use record, defensible for the two selected intervals strictly away from the swap date.

**Quality belongs to the measurement and its source, not equipment condition.** In the operator's [QAQC code definitions §11, printed p. 11](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=11), flag **0** means **passed initial quality checks**, not proof of calibrated accuracy or health of a particular probe. The **F_ATemp** token is **parameter-specific**; it must not be substituted by **F_Record**, applied to other fields or transformed into an invented confidence probability. The source's exact token with whitespace is preserved separately from a human-readable interpretation. **The 29.1 °C versus 27.3 °C difference is not evidence of a probe-induced temperature change**: the dates, weather and physical units differ, and no paired calibration comparison was performed.

**Negative control (do not publish as a third accepted measurement):** The stored [archive evidence](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json) also identifies **CSV line 10322, 17 Apr 2024 at 12:00 EST**, temperature flag **“<-3> [GPR] (CSM)”**. The operator independently describes this timestamp as **rejected** due to uncertainty during the operating-system update. A numeric temperature value, if present, **does not override that rejection flag**. The case has **two selected passed-initial-check temperature reports**, not an assertion that no other archive rows exist. [Operator §13, printed p. 13](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf#page=13).

### 12.4 Who senses, who averages, who reports — and who might own a DataStream?

| Distinct question | **What the original source supports** | **What is still a model/encoding choice** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **What environmental subject and sensing equipment?** | Ambient air-temperature observation at **acebpmet**; two **different physical EE181 units** in the temperature/RH role during distinct service periods, identified by serial **from equipment history**. | Whether a particular CSAPI producer represents the physical EE181, the whole station, or a process/observer relation as the observer/producing System; not decided merely by the CSV. |
| **Who makes the reported 15-minute summary?** | Station's **CR1000X** acquires signals and stores/produces the **15-minute average of 5-second temperature samples** according to its CDMO-supplied program. Logger 2 serial **13916** is identified in the historical operator record. | Whether to attach a stream to **station**, **logger** or **probe**, and how to communicate the participating physical observer versus aggregating/reporting equipment in a tested Profile. The complete active 2024 code/sample stream is unavailable. |
| **Who transfers and publishes it?** | The station includes a **TX321 GOES transmitter**, plus alternate manual/LoggerNet workflows; NOAA/CDMO and reserve process/review/archive the published summaries. | A carrier's transmitter, MQTT/GOES transport, storage database or public API endpoint is **not automatically** the observing hardware or owner of an OGC DataStream. Do not turn one transport path into a universal graph. |
| **What is known about time and quality?** | Source stamp in **fixed EST**, historical **15-minute average**, preserved **F_ATemp** flag; the original source does **not** independently give the time when the result became available. | Nominal averaging bounds are **derived**; assigning the stamp as **resultTime** is not original evidence, and an instant cannot silently replace an average's period. Per-measurement quality is **not** probe condition. |
| **Where did this happen?** | **Station-level** coordinates, tower/mounting context and dated physical-use periods are documented in the reserve metadata. | A particular probe's independent geometry and a machine-readable CSAPI **Deployment** link are **not** established by those records. Station location is **not** a substitute for exact probe position. |

**The explicit unresolved DataStream question:** In the **offline candidate fixture**, the project chose **one station-oriented air-temperature summary stream with per-record EE181 probe attribution**; the parent represents a persistent station summary across the probe swap. In the **live 52°North experiment**, the project instead attached **one scalar stream under each individual probe**, then **one additional archival-report stream under each** to preserve the original QA flag and nominal averaging window. Both arrangements are *project-authored* and were helpful for specific tests; **neither is the original operator's CSAPI model or an adopted Profile rule**. The source evidence alone does not adjudicate which physical, functional or reporting System should own the DataStream. [Offline example and check](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-wire/README.md); [actual live bridge report](https://github.com/OS4CSAPI/glaux-client/blob/work/details-view-20261023/Docs/Design/BENNETT_OBSERVATION_CONTEXT_BRIDGE.md).

**Do not count representations as measurements:** The earlier live 52°North test contains **two original scalar Observations** and **two richer archival-report Observations** (four API resources) referring to just **two independent NOAA source-temperature records**. The archival-report DataRecord preserves **source stamp, calculated window, air value, original F_ATemp token and project-attributed probe UID** after a tested server round trip. This is **VERIFIED_RUNTIME for preservation and retrieval**, not proof of a native interval-valued Observation phenomenonTime, independently established resultTime, permanent unit Deployment, interoperable third-party producer or fully general Glaux discovery. The server's JSON handling and the alternative SWE JSON test remain **separate technical research**, not Step 08's mandate. [Live preservation/limitations](https://github.com/OS4CSAPI/glaux-client/blob/work/details-view-20261023/Docs/Design/BENNETT_OBSERVATION_CONTEXT_BRIDGE.md); [prior standards/encoding decision](../Candidate_Specification/Observation_Encoding_Decision_31OCT2026.md).

### 12.5 What the operating evidence does—and does not—allow us to conclude

**We can say:** There is an operator-described **historical station** with separate **sensing probes**, **CR1000X logger**, **GOES transmitter**, **published station summaries** and **time-scoped equipment-use records**; two selected real temperature summary records can be associated **by a reproducible cross-source temporal join** with **different serial-numbered EE181 units**. The station remains the monitoring referent through the replacement, rather than silently becoming a newly constructed station because one probe changed. This is much stronger *historical identity/role* evidence than a device manual, while still covering only two selected measurements and incomplete operating context.

**We cannot say:** That the CSV itself encodes either EE181 serial, that a replaced unit changed the weather reading, that a documented last calibration assures the two records' accuracy, that the exact swap minute is known, that a 2024 “current” label means 2026 live condition, that the exact logger program/firmware state at each instant has been independently authenticated, or that a CSAPI Deployment/probe geometry or fully correct time/quality Profile binding already exists. We also **cannot infer all missing station properties from the manual's generic features**, nor present the annual CSV as a completely analyzed operating-System dataset.

**Questions for Step 09 (not answers selected today):** Across Dragino's **one external probe/two quantities**, AirGradient's **multiple modules/raw and processed outputs**, Campbell's **separate sensor/logger/program**, and Bennett's **identified time-scoped probe swap and reporting station**, which roles and relations recur? Which are genuinely different, and which cannot be compared at the same evidentiary level? Does a proposed whole-System frame distinguish **persistent host/station identity**, **changeable physical components**, **reporting/aggregation equipment**, **data representations**, **time/quality and source provenance** without inventing one physical unit per measurement, an installed state from a manufacturer example, or duplicate observations? The later original-donor review—not this case—must be free to revise or replace every early candidate category.

### 12.6 Direct-source verification, limitations and next handoff

**Verified during Step 08, 1 November 2026:** the original [ACE Basin NERR 2024 meteorological metadata, updated 18 August 2025](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf), as a **16-page PDF** with visually inspected original pages for **reporting/time (printed p. 2), station structure (p. 3), individual EE181 serials/dates (p. 6), CR1000X and TX321 (p. 11), flags (pp. 11–12) and the April maintenance/OS-update windows (p. 13)**; [NOAA/CDMO original meteorological parameter and timestamp convention](https://www.nerrsdata.org/data/parameters.cfm); [previous pinned two-row CSV evidence](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json); [offline source-aware reader](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-wire/README.md); and [earlier live bridge verification report](https://github.com/OS4CSAPI/glaux-client/blob/work/details-view-20261023/Docs/Design/BENNETT_OBSERVATION_CONTEXT_BRIDGE.md). **Current original annual CSV bytes were not re-fetched in this prompt**; preserve the 31 October snapshot fingerprint and do not assert an additional independent current archive extraction or modified dataset. Publisher distribution terms (§7, printed p. 4) call for appropriate NERR credit/contact for data-based publication; do not bulk-republish original records/manuals by convenience.

**Step 08 changes:** this **source-native reconstruction is appended to the existing cumulative workbook**; the original JSON snapshot, reader, donor/source sheets, candidate DIM/Profile and application/server are unchanged. No new standard clause, domain model entity, CSAPI stream or Deployment, Glaux route, test result, Cloudflare deployment or 52°North/Oracle server mutation has been created.

**Historical handoff after Step 08 (Step 09 is now completed in [§13](#13-step-09--four-source-native-arrangements-compared-without-choosing-a-chassis)):** compare the **four differently supported cases side by side**, showing source-grounded recurring roles, legitimate structural differences, explicit contradictions, missing operating evidence and **questions for competing donor-native whole-System frames**. Do **not** choose the chassis, force the prior F-01–F-05 meanings, update Profile SHALLs, implement a shared client or start donor adjudication while performing that comparison. **Gate B is still open until that comparison is completed and reviewed.**


## 13. Step 09 — Four source-native arrangements compared, without choosing a chassis

**Status: Step 09 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026. Phase B / Gate B: REACHED WITH EXPLICIT EVIDENCE LIMITATIONS.** This is the **comparative interpretation** of the four separately completed source-native reconstructions in [§9 Dragino](#9-step-05--dragino-s31-lb-source-native-reference-system), [§10 AirGradient](#10-step-06--airgradient-open-air-max-source-native-reference-system), [§11 Campbell/ClimaVue](#11-step-07--campbell-cr1000x-and-first-generation-climavue-50-source-native-arrangement), and [§12 Bennett's Point](#12-step-08--bennetts-point-actual-station-time-scoped-probes-and-historical-temperature-reports). It **does not** start the planned Step 10 choice of competing scopes, complete a new donor study, impose the older five candidate foundation headings, settle CSAPI DataStream ownership, or declare an integrated DIM. The sources, versions, and unresolved qualifications in §§6 and 9–12 remain controlling.

### 13.1 What are we actually comparing?

Imagine using one client to open the details of each subject. **Dragino** presents a defined battery-powered communications node with a cabled probe; **AirGradient** presents a product assembly containing several sensor devices, daughterboards and local electronics; **Campbell** documents a **pair** consisting of an independent integrated weather instrument and a separate datalogger; **Bennett's Point** describes a real continuing **station** with a logger, transmitter and physical probes that served the temperature role at different dates. **Those are different source-described boundaries.** The comparison should test whether some common treatment can remain accurate despite that difference, not decide by fiat that every example is one identical box.

| Case and source description | Which boundary the source actually establishes | Evidence standing and legitimate use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Dragino S31-LB** — [manufacturer manual §§1–3](https://wiki.dragino.com/docs/LoRaWAN-End-Node/temperature-humidity-sensors/s31-lb/) and [pinned decoder](https://github.com/dragino/dragino-end-node-decoder/blob/635417693adb215b32ac367c8e8909d9d609a120/S31-LB%26S31B-LB/S31-LB_v1.3_TTN_decoder.txt) | The **S31-LB node**, an **external three-metre SHT31 probe**, their measurement/reporting connection, and the **external LoRaWAN network** it can use. The external gateway is **not** thereby an internal component. | **SOURCE_FACT — reference design**, **SOURCE_EXAMPLE — published payload/history formats**; no authenticated unit, installed region/configuration, command session or physical operating history. |
| **AirGradient Open Air Max** — [pinned hardware README](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/pcb-openair-max-main/blob/89aacf5013fc0ffe4b988a2e3c5a227bce3c4983/README.md) and [pinned firmware README](https://github.com/Open-Air-Foundation/firmware-openair-max/blob/84e479a415d81151e7a81aea9a2f7449a15e98cc/README.md) | A **gas-equipped O-M-1PPSTON-CE monitor assembly** with an ESP32-C6 main board, **two physical PMS5003 devices**, other sensing modules, battery/solar power, and LTE/Wi-Fi paths. Cloud/carrier services are separate. | **SOURCE_FACT — design**, **SOURCE_EXAMPLE — distinct device and cloud message shapes**; no installed board/firmware pairing, real joined record, gas calibration or verified physical-unit serial. |
| **Campbell CR1000X + first-generation ClimaVue 50** — [08/2024 sensor manual Tables 7-1/8-1](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/us/manuals/climavue50.pdf) and [2019 CR1000X program example](https://s.campbellsci.com/documents/eu/manuals/climavue50.pdf) | **One integrated weather-sensing assembly** attached over SDI-12 to **a distinct programmable logger**; the source supports that arrangement, not a verified installed weather station. A later [MQTT logger-status example](https://help.campbellsci.com/datalogger-api/mqtt/status.html) is a **different source/subject**. | **SOURCE_FACT — design and interface**, **SOURCE_EXAMPLE — program and logger-only status**; no actual sensor numerical reply, joined logger/sensor status history or verified execution. |
| **Bennett's Point (ACE Basin)** — [2024 operator meteorological metadata §§2, 4, 9, 11–13](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf) and [pinned archive evidence](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json) | An **identified historical monitoring station**, with **CR1000X logger 13916**, **GOES transmitter**, and time-scoped **EE181 temperature/humidity probe serials** that changed on **16 April 2024**. The station published 15-minute temperature summaries. | **SOURCE_FACT — dated actual equipment and station record**, **DERIVED — two selected measurement-to-probe attributions, UTC and nominal intervals**, and **VERIFIED_RUNTIME only for earlier bounded CSAPI read-back**. Not a complete installed-System history or independently verified current telemetry. |

**Evidence levels are not rankings of product quality.** The three manufacturer cases can support rigorous design-level relationship checks without demonstrating actual installations. Bennett's Point is stronger for *particular past physical identities and time*, but comparatively thin on complete running configuration and all possible system functions. The supplementary historical **Raspberry Shake R9D92** EHZ/HDF header check from [§7](#7-step-04--bounded-attempt-to-acquire-linked-operating-evidence) remains **supplementary and conditional**, **not a fifth primary case or a fully joined operating dataset**.

### 13.2 Side-by-side: ask what the native sources actually say

These **questions arise from the four arrangements**, not an assertion that the rows are new DIM classes, required modules, or a preselected foundation. Each cell is **source-grounded within its case's evidence standing**; the table does **not** establish that its entries are semantically equivalent across vendors.

| Source-driven question | Dragino S31-LB | AirGradient Open Air Max | CR1000X + ClimaVue 50 | Bennett's Point |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Where is environmental sensing realized?** | **One external SHT31** measures temperature **and** humidity. | **Two PMS5003 physical units**, plus CO₂, SGP41, SHT4x and electrochemical sensing modules/elements. | **One integrated ClimaVue weather instrument** has multiple weather-sensing functions. | **Individually serialized EE181 probes**, serving the same station temperature role at different dates. |
| **Who gathers, computes or packages outputs?** | **S31-LB node** reads probe, packages native payload and transmits. | **ESP32-C6/firmware** gathers module data and sends device reports; cloud examples are separate representations. | **CR1000X program** requests SDI-12 values, transforms/aggregates and logs them; sensor also has internal timing/aggregation. | **Station CR1000X** records 5-second samples and produces 15-minute station summaries; NOAA/CDMO separately reviews/publishes. |
| **How are parts or roles linked?** | Physical cable from external probe to node; **LoRaWAN gateway** is outside the node. | Plug-in boards and two directly attached PM devices in an assembled enclosure; cloud/carrier are outside. | **SDI-12 cable, C/U terminal, sensor logical address**; separate logger and sensor. | **Station/equipment-use history** relates logger, transmitter and **dated probe service intervals**; not a complete deployed CSAPI graph. |
| **Which identity evidence exists?** | Model **S31-LB** and code-defined **Node_type**, but no inspected Device EUI/serial for a unit. | Described **O-M-1PPSTON-CE** configuration; commercial **-CE-S** suffix and cloud placeholder serial do not prove installed identity. | Product types and default **SDI-12 address 0**; **no acquired sensor/logger serial**. Publisher status **loggerID** is a placeholder. | Station **acebpmet**, **CR1000X serial 13916**, two distinct **EE181 serials**; CSV **does not contain those probe serials**. |
| **What do the reported values actually represent?** | **FPort 2** temperature/humidity, battery and flags; **FPort 3** example history; **FPort 5** possible device status. | Device **PM/environmental/raw electrode** fields, cellular batches, Wi-Fi samples and separate cloud/raw/corrected values. | **aR7!** defines **14 sensor positions**; separately published logger **Status** has **31 fields** and is **not weather data**. | Two archived **ATemp** 15-minute air means and parameter-specific **F_ATemp** quality tokens; current server has **four representations of two measurements**. |
| **What does time or freshness establish?** | Published history contains epoch bytes; rendered date depends on decoder time zone; not a unit's proven history. | Normally three-minute sampling and nine-minute **cellular batching**, Wi-Fi one-sample reports; possible cached retransmission, version-dependent intervals. | **aR7! can return a prior reading**; example logger scan period differs from sensor internal measurement timing. | Source noon **fixed EST** station stamps, **derived nominal prior-15-minute interval**, dated probe use; exact changeover time and independent result availability **unknown**. |
| **Which controls, state or support are evidenced?** | Primary battery/LoRaWAN, commands, alarms, defaults and possible replies; **no actual applied setting or command outcome**. | Solar/three-cell battery/USB-C, cellular/Wi-Fi, settings portal, LED/mode definitions; **no verified active setting or observed faults**. | Separate power for logger/sensor, SDI-12 commands, CRBasic processing and **logger-only** example diagnostics; **no linked current operating state**. | Operator maintenance, logger operating-system update, dated equipment use and source QA; **not** current probe health, running program export or complete command history. |
| **What is the chief missing evidence?** | Authenticated unit/probe IDs, applied configuration and real device records. | Authenticated assembled unit, matched hardware/firmware, valid gas calibration and joined reporting history. | Authenticated installed pair, actual **aR7!/aI!/aV!** replies, running program and real weather/status history. | Complete same-station configuration, unobserved sample-level history, exact swap minute, true **resultTime**, probe-specific Deployment and final stream semantics. |

**A genuinely important comparison:** two numbers labelled **temperature** may mean **SHT31 ambient reading**, **ClimaVue ambient air temperature**, **humidity-sensor temperature**, **logger PanelTemp**, an AirGradient **unresolved AFE signal**, or a **15-minute station mean**. Source wording, physical subject, unit, processing, time and provenance must remain attached to the relevant claim. The fact that several values have a similar English label **does not make their original meaning or observation process equal**. [Campbell sheet](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md), [AirGradient sheet](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md), [Bennett evidence](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json).

### 13.3 What recurring patterns actually survive—and their limits

**A. There are physical arrangements and information-bearing outputs, but they do not have one universal shape.** Dragino's single probe has **two** measured properties; AirGradient has **multiple** physical devices but still more reported fields; ClimaVue is **one** externally connected sensing assembly with **fourteen** defined weather values; Bennett has **different identifiable probes at successive times** but a continuing station. **Analyst inference:** a future common representation must be able to express those distinctions **without inferring a physical object for every result field**. This does **not** prove that every constituent merits a standalone System, that every connection is a containment relation, or that the final boundary must be a particular root class.

**B. The actor that senses need not be the actor that collects, transforms, stores or publishes.** In Dragino, the **probe** and reporting **node** have different roles. AirGradient's modules and main board share an enclosure while the cloud remains external. The ClimaVue and CR1000X are separate instruments; Bennett's logged average and NOAA archival QA add still more processing/reporting roles. **Analyst inference:** physical connection, containment, observation, processing, persistence and publication cannot safely be treated as one relation merely because a common client ultimately displays the information. **Which roles belong to the eventual DIM boundary and how CSAPI DataStream ownership works remain open.**

**C. Identity and time are local to the actual referent.** The Dragino decoder's model label is **not** an authenticated Device EUI. Campbell's SDI-12 address is **not** a physical serial. AirGradient's example location ID or placeholder serial does **not** establish a monitor. At Bennett, the same **EE181 serial** can have two distinct service periods, while station identity continues through replacement; the actual CSV temperature row contains no probe ID. **Analyst inference:** a candidate whole-System description must not silently merge **model, individual, address, station, deployment period and information-record identity**. Which source-native identifiers are appropriate and when is a later standards/architecture question.

**D. Output values and their provenance can change before they reach a user.** AirGradient's **raw electrodes versus derived-looking cloud gas outputs**, Campbell's **absolute versus adjusted pressure and carried-forward wind**, and Bennett's **archived mean, quality and derived probe attribution** expose several different steps. Dragino's FPort 3 history format and the local-time rendering issue supply a distinct example of **decoder presentation versus original epoch**. **Analyst inference:** a common display must know the level of meaning supported and refrain from calling all returned data instantaneous, direct, fresh, calibrated or independently observed. This does not yet establish one shared observation schema or a valid standards encoding for every case.

**E. Supported capabilities and actual operating state are not interchangeable.** Three manufacturer design cases document commands, firmware interfaces, mode/power behavior and reporting formats **without one verified corresponding physical unit**. Bennett provides **dated maintenance and physical-use history**, but not a complete command/acknowledgement log or current health state. Source defaults, authored example settings and successful manual commands **are not measured effective state**. The eventual meaning of configuration, status, action and lifecycle must be justified separately rather than inferred from available fields or familiar UI tabs.

These are **analyst comparative findings based on cited source evidence**, not new adopted DIM entities, a mandatory five-piece structure, interface rules, formal conformance tests, or evidence of full cross-vendor compatibility.

### 13.4 Specific misleading shortcuts the sources let us reject

| Tempting shortcut (**REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE**) | Concrete counterexample and required qualification |
| --- | --- |
| **One reported property or channel = one physical sensor.** | One **SHT31** produces two values; one **ClimaVue** exposes fourteen positions; AirGradient has two actual PM devices and many PM fields. Counts of reported fields do **not** establish physical component cardinality. |
| **One physical sensor = the equipment that reports, aggregates and publishes.** | ClimaVue **sensor** ≠ CR1000X **logger**; Bennett EE181 **probe** ≠ persistent **station/logger/GOES/CDMO** reporting roles; Dragino external probe ≠ LoRaWAN **node**. |
| **A matching name, model or address identifies the same physical unit.** | A decoder's **Node_type**, Campbell **SDI-12 address**, and AirGradient illustrative **serialno** have different authority; Bennett demonstrates that actual equipment serial and service interval must be distinguished from the stable station code. |
| **Any temperature, humidity, pressure or gas field is a directly equivalent environmental observation.** | Campbell **PanelTemp** is **logger panel temperature**, not ClimaVue or Bennett air; ClimaVue RH is **fraction** before program conversion; air versus sensor-internal temperature differs; AirGradient **AFE/measure4** unit remains unsettled, raw gas electrodes are not validated ambient gas concentrations. |
| **Response time or a timestamp always marks a new, instantaneous physical measurement.** | Campbell **aR7!** can return the prior value; AirGradient cellular reporting **batches and may resend**; Bennett reports an **average of earlier samples**, with only a *derived nominal window*; Dragino history displays epoch time through a local-zone formatter. |
| **One API Observation resource = one independent source measurement.** | Bennett's live test has **two scalar plus two richer archival-report resources** for **two original station readings**; record/representation count is not sample count. Multiple AirGradient message examples also are **not one joined observed stream**. |
| **A documented power value or command proves battery health or effective configuration.** | Dragino **8,500 mAh design capacity** ≠ remaining percentage; AirGradient panel/battery volts ≠ SOC; Campbell **Battery**, **LithiumBattery** and **SW12Volts Boolean** refer to different logger values; no device commands were executed by this corpus work. |
| **Published coordinates or a maintenance date fully define a Deployment.** | Campbell example **[0,0,0]** and AirGradient example **0/0** are placeholders; Bennett has a **real station site** but not independently verified per-probe geometry or the exact **16 April** swap minute. |

**These counterexamples are source-to-model falsification inputs for later steps, not claims that a given product violates any standard.** Avoid transforming a negative example into a new universal class or mandatory cardinality rule.

### 13.5 Different evidence gaps require different remedies

| Gap kind | Grounded example | What later work is actually permitted to conclude |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Missing authenticated individual/operating evidence** | Dragino no unit EUI/history; AirGradient no serial-to-hardware/configuration/history join; Campbell no active pair/program/SDI-12 reply. | Design-level interpretation may be tested; *deployed identity, active state and actual command results* remain **UNKNOWN** rather than scored as successful interoperability. |
| **Missing processing/quality context** | AirGradient source cautions against treating gas values as calibrated truth; ClimaVue program transforms and may carry forward values; Bennett has **parameter QA** but lacks true result availability time. | Preserve raw vs derived, validity and origin as stated. An absent algorithm/time is **UNKNOWN**, not a zero or permission to invent. |
| **Source version/meaning inconsistency** | Dragino example decimal/mode/date formatting; AirGradient hardware/firmware chip and timing-guide differences; Campbell 2019 code print anomaly/2021 package not tested; older Bennett station equipment and current-time interpretation. | Retain source wording and edition/context; comparison does **not** resolve disagreement by choosing the most convenient value. |
| **Unresolved domain/standard relationship** | What the bounded whole System is when separate instruments form a reporting arrangement; which System *observes*, *aggregates* or *owns* a stream; what an attachment, host role or time-scoped installation means. | Needs genuinely competing alternative explanations, targeted original donor analysis and explicit decisions in later **Steps 10–31**. No standards-derived conclusion is reached in this source comparison. |
| **Unverified implementation transformation** | Bennett offline station-led stream versus live probe-led streams; source QA/window held in a provisional archival-report representation; other manufacturer examples not run through the same reader. | This is **NOT TESTED** for general Profile-aware cross-vendor Glaux interoperability; a working specific HTTP GET or passing offline fixture is not a general conformance PASS. |

**The same-unit/same-configuration/same-history evidence gap remains unresolved.** This comparison did not collect another telemetry corpus or reopen the previously closed acquisition steps. Bennett's two real records are credible for the chosen historical attribution question; they are **not** representative of all sensor state, command, maintenance or cross-vendor operating variability. Raspberry Shake's additional historical header evidence does not close that gap or grant republication rights.

### 13.6 Questions to carry forward into neutral donor and scope review

The following are **unanswered comparison questions**, **not** established DIM categories or donor equivalences:

1. **What bounded subject are we describing?** Can one approach account for a manufactured sensing node, an assembled multisensor monitor, a **two-instrument** weather measuring arrangement and a persistent station with replaceable hardware? Which physical/functional/system boundary would exclude important information? **Do not choose an answer until Step 10 alternatives are compared.**
2. **What relationships does each source actually assert?** Distinguish *internal constituent*, *cabled connection*, *addressed device*, *separate logger*, *reporting service*, and *equipment in a role over an interval*. Which relationships could be confused if forced into one part-of tree?
3. **What is being identified, and at what granularity?** Product/kind, particular physical unit, bus/channel address, station, service interval, data record, data publisher, code/firmware release and digital representation are not automatically the same referent. Which donor treatments are actually capable of preserving these differences?
4. **Who performs sensing and who produces or publishes a result?** Can an original-observer/processor/reporter distinction survive for Dragino, Campbell and Bennett? What evidence is needed before deciding the *owning* CSAPI DataStream or whether an observation belongs to a logger, probe or stable station?
5. **What exactly is an output, observation, transformed result or status?** How can one source-defined SHT31 output, multiple PM quantities, ClimaVue aR7! positions, program-adjusted values, and real 15-minute station summaries be represented *without inventing component identity, freshness, time or quality*?
6. **What is supported and what was actually selected/done?** Which donor-native mechanisms distinguish declared capability, default, chosen configuration, health/condition, requested command, acknowledgement, completed effect and maintenance/part replacement? Which of these are not supported by the present examples?
7. **Where do power, communications, security, software, diagnostics and lifecycle belong?** The sources contain whole-System supporting functions that are **not themselves observations**, but their presence must not cause the analysis to choose power or any old V1 framework by familiarity.
8. **What evidence and exceptions must remain visible to a generic client?** A human should be able to see **manufacturer specification**, **published illustration**, **historical observed record**, **project-derived attribution**, **unknown value**, or **tested server behavior** without mistaking any of them for another. The DIM, Profile and Glaux each have different responsibilities; none is settled by this table.

**Planned process, not started here:** Step 10 will construct **multiple possible whole-System frames** and their failure cases from these source questions. Steps 11–14 then revisit original **AAS / OPC UA Devices / PA-DIM; ISA / The Open Group SOSA; IEEE 1451 / SensorML; SAPIENT / OMS / SOSA-SSN / Web of Things** evidence on its **own terms**. No later donor may be dismissed merely because an earlier five-heading candidate lacked a slot; nor does a donor become an accepted chassis by its presence in this research plan. The 182 source artifacts remain a nonuniform corpus, **not 182 equally studied independent standards**.

### 13.7 Phase B / Gate B review and exact stopping point

| Gate B test from the 43-step plan | Check | Decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Can a human distinguish all four actual source-described arrangements?** | Sections 9–12 and the §13.1–13.2 side-by-side show **one external-probe node**, **one multi-module assembled monitor**, **one separate sensor/logger pair**, and **one actual historical station with a dated probe replacement**. | **MET at their declared documentary/historical scope.** |
| **Do the sources' missing facts and provenance remain plain?** | Three cases lack authenticated individual operation; Bennett has two selected source readings, separately derived attribution and an incomplete full-System record. Source versions, rejected assumptions and unresolved timestamps are explicit. | **MET for Step 09 evidence disclosure**, not an operational-dataset completeness PASS. |
| **Did a single vendor's interface, the existing five candidate headings, a CSAPI resource type or a UI tab determine the whole System?** | Each case is read in its **native physical/reporting roles**, with only analyst comparison stated. The counterexamples rule out shortcuts; no universal boundary, mandatory classes or DataStream owner is adopted. | **MET as a process and comparison boundary.** |
| **Did this step prove a DIM, Profile, shared interpreter or deployed interoperability?** | No new DIM/Profile rules, conversions, code, cross-case interpreter tests, server writes or user-facing integration were performed. | **NOT TESTED, explicitly outside Gate B**; remaining Phase C–G work is necessary. |

**Gate B decision: REACHED WITH EXPLICIT EVIDENCE LIMITATIONS.** The four source-native arrangements can now be compared and their known unknowns are visible **without imposing the model they are intended to inform**. The missing complete operating corpora and unresolved station/probe/stream semantics remain real constraints for later claims, but they **do not require inventing facts or redefining the narrow Phase B acceptance condition**. The successful gate means **we know what questions to take to the standards**, not that we have already answered them.

**Changes made in Step 09:** added **this one cumulative, human-readable comparison section** to the existing workbook; did **not** change earlier primary source audits, the source sheets/frozen corpus, candidate DIM, candidate CSAPI Profile, Glaux interpretation code, production application branch, Cloudflare deployment or the live 52°North/Oracle server. This was **source-to-source analysis**, not an original-source re-acquisition, a runtime test or a standards conformance check.

**Historical handoff after Step 09 (Step 10 is now complete in [§14](#14-step-10--three-competing-ways-to-bound-the-whole-sensing-system-not-a-chassis-decision)):** enumerate **two or more genuinely alternative, source-reasoned ways to bound and organize the whole sensing System**, explain what each would accommodate and where each might fail against the four cases, and **leave all alternatives provisional**. Do **not** pick the chassis, draft normative DIM/Profile classes, execute Steps 11–14 donor reviews or change the live client/server during Step 10.


## 14. Step 10 — Three competing ways to bound the whole sensing System (NOT a chassis decision)

**Status: Step 10 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026. Phase C exploratory comparison only; Step 11 NEXT.** This step derives **three deliberately competing boundary hypotheses** from the **four source-native cases** in [§§9–12](#9-step-05--dragino-s31-lb-source-native-reference-system) and the [neutral Step 09 comparison (§13)](#13-step-09--four-source-native-arrangements-compared-without-choosing-a-chassis). They are **analyst-generated, testable alternatives**, not definitions found verbatim in any standard, approved DIM classes, a final “chassis,” an application Profile, or three modules that must all appear in the eventual architecture.

**The question:** When a Profile-aware client displays “this System,” *what makes that referent one coherent whole*? Is it the **physical unit**, the **set of sensing/processing/reporting roles producing a result**, or the **installation that persists as equipment comes and goes**? Different original sources emphasize different answers. The eventual DIM may require a different formulation, multiple distinguishable perspectives, or a fourth alternative introduced by the donors. **Do not privilege a familiar V1 name, the prior F-01–F-05 candidate meanings, or a CSAPI resource type as the answer.**

### 14.1 Three distinct provisional boundaries

| Alternative boundary — **working label only** | What would identify one “whole” | Relationships it **must be able to express** | What is initially *outside* that boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **A — Physical equipment unit or assembled product** | An identified hardware unit/assembly (or, without a real unit, a manufacturer-defined product arrangement): enclosure, node, logger, or instrument as the chosen physical referent. | Individual unit vs model; **physical constituent** vs separately connected equipment; interfaces; sensing element vs output property/channel; support electronics; hardware revision; condition/configuration **for the correct particular unit**; external hosts and installations as **associated**, not automatically contained. | Separate logger, gateway, station, archive or network service **unless the physical boundary is explicitly a manufactured assembly containing it**. A connection alone does not make a component internal. |
| **B — An observing and reporting arrangement** | The bounded arrangement that performs a stated sensing/reporting **function or result-producing responsibility**; may use multiple individually identifiable instruments, processors and transports. | Which actor actually senses, which acquires/processes, which retains/publishes; physical/logical/communication associations; supported method vs executed activity; input-to-output transformations; freshness, quality and times; support configuration and controls; **multiple functions on shared equipment**. | Other unrelated capabilities, independent equipment lifecycles and infrastructure **not needed** for the chosen reporting responsibility. The boundary depends on which function/report is chosen and cannot be inferred from a value or DataStream alone. |
| **C — A continuing monitoring installation or station** | A site-, installation- or operator-defined monitoring arrangement that persists through some equipment changes and whose continuity is established by a station/installation identifier and dated history. | Persistent station identity; role assignments to distinct serial-numbered hardware **over intervals**; installation/host relationships; environmental subject/site; logger/transmitter/reporting; replacements, maintenance and calibration history; provenance of historical station reports. | Uninstalled product types, unsited portable devices and equipment operating outside a named continuing installation **unless separately related**. A product's ability to be installed does **not** create a documented station. |

**These are competing answers to the scope question, not automatically mutually exclusive real-world objects.** A physical instrument can serve in an installation and participate in an observing arrangement. But **choosing one as the primary organizing referent** changes what a common client identifies first, what remains contextual, and which data become misleading if silently attached to that referent. The alternatives are therefore genuine *modeling choices to investigate*, not three synonyms for “System.” Their use here is **CANDIDATE_MAPPING/analyst hypothesis**, not SOURCE_FACT or normative OGC language.

### 14.2 Test each boundary against all four source arrangements

Read each cell as **strength / possible failure if that boundary is forced as the sole primary whole**. These are **source-reasoned predictions**, **not executed DIM/Profile/client tests, or numerical scores**.

| Original case | **A — Physical equipment** | **B — Observing/reporting arrangement** | **C — Continuing installation/station** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Dragino S31-LB** ([§9](#9-step-05--dragino-s31-lb-source-native-reference-system)) | **Strength:** clear S31-LB node and *one* cabled external SHT31 with two measurement properties. **Failure risk:** treating an external probe as internal merely because it is connected, or ignoring the node–probe relationship if only the node counts. | **Strength:** one temperature/humidity reporting responsibility spans SHT31, S31-LB and LoRaWAN path. **Failure risk:** misidentifying external gateway/network as owned equipment or dropping node battery, alarms and configuration because they are not directly measurements. | **Strength:** could track a node deployed at a location *if there were deployment evidence*. **Failure risk:** **no installed site/unit exists in our evidence packet**; calling its manufacturer reference one operating station would fabricate history and identity. |
| **AirGradient Open Air Max** ([§10](#10-step-06--airgradient-open-air-max-source-native-reference-system)) | **Strength:** the main board, multiple sensor boards, **two PMS5003 devices**, CE card and power equipment are described as one assembled product. **Failure risk:** counting output fields as components or treating cloud/carrier as internal hardware. | **Strength:** preserves distinct PM, CO₂, meteorological and gas-measurement functions and local/cloud processing roles. **Failure risk:** if defined *per output or function*, a single physical monitor could be fragmented into many “whole Systems,” obscuring shared configuration, power and identity; raw gas values cannot be promoted to validated concentrations. | **Strength:** could eventually represent a long-running air-quality installation with device swaps and site-specific validity. **Failure risk:** the cloud **locationId**, **serialno** and zero coordinates in examples are **placeholders**, not a verified installation or equipment history. |
| **Campbell CR1000X + ClimaVue 50** ([§11](#11-step-07--campbell-cr1000x-and-first-generation-climavue-50-source-native-arrangement)) | **Strength:** preserves separate physical identities of a ClimaVue instrument and CR1000X logger. **Failure risk:** selecting **only the sensor** loses logger programming/processing and logger-only status; selecting **only the logger** treats a separately attached sensing unit as incidental. Physical connection is not enough to identify a single manufactured unit. | **Strength:** captures the **two-instrument SDI-12 measurement arrangement** and its program-derived or carried-forward values. **Failure risk:** multiple logger programs or attached sensors can make the boundary fluid; the **2019 program**, **2024 manual** and **2026 logger example** cannot be combined into an executed arrangement. | **Strength:** plausible for a real deployed weather station with replaceable instruments. **Failure risk:** the current source case is a **reference pairing, not a documented installed station**; the MQTT example's **[0,0,0]** is not site geometry. |
| **Bennett's Point** ([§12](#12-step-08--bennetts-point-actual-station-time-scoped-probes-and-historical-temperature-reports)) | **Strength:** names real EE181 serials **222016000109CE** and **21281600077814**, distinct from CR1000X logger **13916**. **Failure risk:** a probe-rooted whole would appear to change when the probe is replaced even though **acebpmet** remains the same station; choosing the logger alone loses the particular sensing probe's history. | **Strength:** explicitly distinguishes **EE181 sensing**, **CR1000X five-second acquisition/15-minute averaging**, GOES transfer and NOAA/CDMO archival report/QA. **Failure risk:** defining the whole as a particular measurement stream may underrepresent the station's persistent identity and maintenance; it does not resolve **station-led versus probe-led DataStream** ownership. | **Strength:** directly supported by operator station identity and two time-scoped EE181 installations; the same station continues across **16 April 2024** replacement. **Failure risk:** the station label must not erase separate serials, treat **2024 as current**, create an exact swap minute, or assign the published station geometry as each probe's independently verified position. |

**The comparison is intentionally asymmetrical.** Manufacturer product/design documentation is legitimate evidence for physical arrangements and supported functions, but it **cannot** verify an actual deployed unit or continuing station. Bennett's operator evidence is stronger for historical installation identity but still does not supply the complete running configuration, true independent result-availability times, or per-probe CSAPI Deployment graph. A candidate must fail or remain **NOT TESTED** where it requires missing evidence, rather than being declared correct because we filled the gaps with plausible details.

### 14.3 What would disprove or substantially weaken each alternative?

- **A (physical-unit primary):** If a client using only that root cannot faithfully describe a documented **separate logger + sensing assembly** without falsely making one a constituent of the other; or cannot distinguish **Bennett's continuing station** from changing probe identity, it fails the desired **whole-System representation** under that scope. It can still be a valid **equipment subview**; the failure would be treating that view as the **exclusive organizing subject**.
- **B (observing/reporting-function primary):** If the same device's **power, configuration, command/response, communications, maintenance and physical individuality** disappear whenever a client focuses on one output, or if the model invents a service/process link not established by source data, it fails **whole-System coverage/source fidelity**. If a single sensing function is not well defined in a manufacturer reference, do **not** create a fielded process instance from a product spec.
- **C (persistent-station primary):** If the client has to invent a **deployment or site identity** before it can use a perfectly documented uninstalled S31-LB, AirGradient or ClimaVue product/reference arrangement, it fails the **product/design-level use cases**. If the station root silently makes all attached physical units interchangeable or converts operator use-end dates into exact deployment endpoints, it fails **Bennett's actual historical evidence**.

**Possible but not chosen:** the standards may support **separately represented equipment, functions and installation associations** with one *declared subject of interest* per client request rather than one universal all-purpose root. This is another **hypothesis to examine**, **not** permission to declare the problem solved by “use all three,” draft three mandatory classes, or assume every source naturally supplies all three perspectives. The exact boundary, identity, cardinality, composition/association semantics and availability of evidence remain unresolved.

### 14.4 Questions that would differentiate the alternatives during fair donor review

The table below assigns **research questions**, not preliminary answers, donor “wins,” equivalences or promised normative features. The named donor families are the existing [plan's targeted sources](../PLAN_DIM_Profile_V2_Whole_System_Recovery_to_Delivery_v0.1_31OCT2026.md#phase-c--give-the-donors-a-fair-role-in-the-chassis-decision-1015), **not authorities for conclusions in this Step 10**.

| Next source review | Ask of the **original edition and actual clauses** | How the answer could change the boundary analysis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Step 11 — Asset Administration Shell (AAS), OPC UA Devices and PA-DIM** | Does the source-native model distinguish **asset instance, device/system/type, physical parts, separately connected devices, hosted representations, deployment/installation and lifecycle**? What is the exact scope of each relationship? Is a station treated differently from a product instance? | Could favor **A's equipment view**, establish an honest **C-style continuing asset/installation**, show a needed **multi-referent combination**, or rule out a shortcut; **Devices** coverage must **not** stand in for **PA-DIM**. |
| **Step 12 — Integrated Sensor Architecture (ISA) and The Open Group SOSA** | How are **physical constituent, functional role, platform/host, separately managed equipment, sensor, controller, command and health reporting** distinguished? Does a named sensing capability imply any physical membership or system-wide status? | Could strengthen or undermine any of A/B/C, especially **Campbell's separately managed sensor/logger** and **AirGradient's multiple hardware/functions**. An original diagram or concept may suggest a **fourth frame**, not merely confirm our three. |
| **Step 13 — IEEE 1451, SensorML and relevant observing descriptions** | What source-native concepts distinguish **physical transducer hardware, addressed channel, network-capable application processor/host, implemented process, multiple outputs, the individual unit and its reusable type**? What editions/clauses actually support these distinctions? | Critical to **Dragino's one SHT31/two properties**, **ClimaVue's one assembly/14 values**, and the **observer versus producer** issue. Public IEEE 1451/NIST material exists, but an inspected technical example is **not** authority for unexamined standard editions. |
| **Step 14 — SAPIENT v2, OMS, SOSA/SSN and Web of Things** | What is the observer, observed subject, observation procedure, process/processor, reporting agent, command/action outcome, and *record/thing/digital representation*? Does the source support persistent system identity across replacement or deployment periods? | Could refine **B's observing arrangement**, **C's temporal station account**, or a different common subject. Data stream ownership, normative time mapping and command semantics must be checked against the **actual source texts**, not deduced from matching labels. |

**Cross-cutting tests to retain for every donor:** (1) an **external cable** is not automatically physical containment; (2) **one physical unit may supply multiple properties**, and **one function may involve more than one device**; (3) **a reference design is not an installed instance**; (4) **a persistent station identity need not equal its current probe identity**; (5) **source-record quality, current equipment condition, request, acknowledgement and measured effect** are different questions. Do not require a donor to support every one of these; a source can be authoritative for a bounded contribution without supplying the entire DIM.

### 14.5 Status of the boundary question and exact next step

**Step 10's result:** there are **at least three substantive and different candidate frames** with explicit scopes, required relationships, cross-case advantages, concrete failure tests and original-donor questions. All three remain **OPEN**. We **have not selected** an exclusive physical-unit root, an observation/function root, an installation root, a hybrid, a new overarching scope name, the old five F-01–F-05 candidates or an inherited V1 power-centric architecture. **The decision is reserved for the later original-source reviews and Step 15.** Neither sources nor the present test compel a premature winner.

**Original evidence backing the comparison:** the [Step 09 four-case source/reference matrix](#13-step-09--four-source-native-arrangements-compared-without-choosing-a-chassis), [Dragino S31-LB manual and decoder (§9)](#9-step-05--dragino-s31-lb-source-native-reference-system), [AirGradient pinned hardware/firmware and source examples (§10)](#10-step-06--airgradient-open-air-max-source-native-reference-system), [Campbell 08/2024 sensor and separate logger/program sources (§11)](#11-step-07--campbell-cr1000x-and-first-generation-climavue-50-source-native-arrangement), and [Bennett's operator metadata and pinned historical rows (§12)](#12-step-08--bennetts-point-actual-station-time-scoped-probes-and-historical-temperature-reports). Step 10 is **an analytical synthesis of already checked evidence**, not a new independent standard audit, an executed mapping/client test or new acquired live data.

**What was changed:** only this **one cumulative workbook section**, plus the plan/README execution checkpoint. Existing raw-source records, prior audits, the candidate DIM/Profile manuscript, older PITONs, Glaux code, Cloudflare deployment and Oracle/52°North CSAPI resources remain untouched. Do not update the PITON again until its next planned continuity checkpoint or necessary chat reset.

**Historical handoff after Step 10; Step 11 is now complete in [§15](#15-step-11--original-aas-opc-ua-devices-and-pa-dim-donor-native-whole-system-questions):** — examine **original Asset Administration Shell, OPC UA Devices and PA-DIM source material**, on their native terms, specifically for asset/device instance and type, physical composition, attachments/hosting, representations and lifecycle. Record exact editions/clauses and unsupported material, allow concepts **outside the three Step 10 alternatives** to remain visible, and stop **before** ISA/SOSA Step 12 or any chassis selection.


## 15. Step 11 — Original AAS, OPC UA Devices and PA-DIM: donor-native whole-System questions

**Status: Step 11 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026. Step 12 NEXT; Gate C still OPEN.** This is a **targeted original-source inspection**, not a new 182-artifact survey or confirmation exercise for the three Step 10 candidate boundaries.
