It uses **IDTA AAS Part 1 Metamodel v3.2**, **OPC Unified Architecture Part 100: Devices v1.05.0**, and **OPC 30081 PA-DIM v1.02.0**, as marked by the publishers on the accessed original HTML editions. **Original standard statements**, **our comparative interpretations** and **unexamined issues** remain separate. The older [donor contribution overview](../Concept_Donor_Comparison/Donor_Contributions.md) and [side-by-side terminology](../Concept_Donor_Comparison/Donor_Terms_Side_by_Side.md) were used to locate known omissions, but are **not substituted for the standards**.

### 15.1 In ordinary language — the original donors contribute different things

**AAS first asks what asset is being digitally described.** The **Asset Administration Shell** has a unique identity as a description/representation; the asset it represents has its own kind and asset identifiers. AAS makes a **type asset** different from an **instance asset**, and allows a *role* asset kind; **Submodels** describe asset aspects, while **Entity** and **RelationshipElement** can represent constituent items and explicit relations. This is potentially useful for equipment identity and representation, **but the AAS Shell itself is not the physical probe, logger or station**. It is also not a universally accepted temporal Deployment model. [Original AAS Shell and AssetInformation metamodel](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/core.html); [types and instances](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/annex/general.html); [Entity and RelationshipElement](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/submodel-elements.html).

**OPC UA Devices starts with a Device and its information/topology representation.** The standard defines a Device in real-world terms, explicitly allows a Device to be a topology of other Devices or parts, and gives **ComponentType** a broader range than **DeviceType**, including **software components**. It distinguishes the vendor's **Model** nameplate information from **DeviceType**, which is an OPC UA ObjectType defining the **representation's structure**. **ConnectsTo** documents a communication/topological relation, **not universal mechanical containment**. Separate **offline and online Objects** may expose configuration versus real device parameters; **offline configuration is not evidence that a field unit is currently in that state**. [Original Devices §§3.1.5, 4.1, 4.5.2, 4.6–4.7, 5.5, 6.1–6.3, 9.4](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/).

**PA-DIM is not just another name for Devices.** **PADIMType** is a **subtype of Devices ComponentType**, **not** a subtype of Devices **DeviceType**. Its source [§7.2, Table 109](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.2) makes specific inherited nameplate fields mandatory within its PA-DIM model, applies device-health and administration/signal interfaces, and defines optional **SignalSet**, **SubDevices**, and **DeviceConditionSet** structures. **Signals, process variables, subdevices and condition variables serve different native purposes**. PA-DIM also links types/properties to **IEC 61987 Common Data Dictionary** entries through **IRDI identifiers**; those provide semantic vocabulary, not proof any selected equipment complies with PA-DIM. [Original PA-DIM §§6.1, 6.3, 7.1.2, 7.2, 7.22, 7.24–7.25](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/6.1).

### 15.2 Accountable source-native findings — not forced equivalence with A/B/C

The IDs below identify **this Step 11 source-inspection record**, not standards-assigned concept IDs or new model classes. Terms and references stay with their source editions; all proposed DIM relevance is **analyst comparison only**.

| Record and exact original locator | Source-native meaning actually established | Contribution / rejected shortcut / boundary still open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **AAS11-01 — AAS Part 1 v3.2, core: AssetAdministrationShell and AssetInformation** ([original](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/core.html)) | Shell inherits **Identifiable**, has **assetInformation (1)** and **submodel references (0..*)**; AssetInformation distinguishes its **assetKind**, **globalAssetId**, **specificAssetId**, and **assetType**. Constraint **AASd-131** requires **globalAssetId OR at least one specificAssetId**, not both necessarily. The Shell's own identity differs from the asset identifier. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** Shell ID = physical serial; AAS **assetType** = CSAPI **assetType** category. The AAS field can identify a **type asset**; its semantics are not inherited by a similarly spelled OGC property. Source-specific AAS cardinalities **are not DIM cardinalities**. |
| **AAS11-02 — AAS v3.2, General: Types and Instances / AssetKind** ([original](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/annex/general.html)) | Types and particular assets can have distinct life-cycle data; **AssetKind includes Type, Instance, Batch, Role, NotApplicable**. Its example has **two real temperature-sensor instance assets of one type** and **separately identified Shells**. One metamodel handles type and instance assets; the document does **not** require every instance to have a corresponding type asset. | Helps ask about **Bennett's two same-model probes and repeated service** without treating model=unit. **Role** is a source-native asset kind, but **not automatically a dated station job assignment or our final chassis**. The example illustrates what AAS *can* describe, not source evidence of the reserve's AAS adoption. |
| **AAS11-03 — AAS v3.2: Submodel / Entity / EntityType / RelationshipElement** ([core](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/core.html); [elements](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/submodel-elements.html)) | A **Submodel** describes a specific aspect of an asset, not automatically a physical component. **Entity** represents parts of composite assets and differentiates **CoManagedEntity** (no separate Shell) from **SelfManagedEntity** (has its own Shell). **RelationshipElement** connects referenced elements and relies on **semanticId** for relation meaning; **AnnotatedRelationshipElement** can add information. | Introduces a potentially important **management/representation granularity** distinction beyond Step 10's physical/functional/station labels. **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** Submodel = physical sensor; co-managed = physically integrated; RelationshipElement = automatically time-scoped Deployment. Exact role/installation/lifecycle semantics require a defined relation, not the existence of a generic carrier. |
| **DI11-01 — OPC UA Devices Part 100 v1.05.0, §§3.1.5, 4.1, 4.5.2, 4.6–4.7** ([Device](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/3.1.5), [ComponentType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.6), [DeviceType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.7), [nameplate](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.5.2)) | Device can be a physical entity or topology; **ComponentType** represents devices or parts including mechanical/software parts. **DeviceType** is an **abstract ObjectType** of representation structure, while manufacturer **Model** is a separate vendor nameplate property. The **DeviceType** table includes serial/revision/nameplate information and ConnectionPoints; mandatory nameplate fields can be represented by **specified default empty values when unsupported**. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** DeviceType = product model; a required OPC UA property with **empty default** = authenticated installed serial. The original Device definition is broader than a single sensor chip and already complicates a rigid single-enclosure chassis, but **does not prescribe our DIM's root**. |
| **DI11-02 — Devices §§5.5, 6.1–6.3 and 9.4** ([ConnectsTo](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/5.5), [host](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/6.1), [online/offline](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/6.3), [modular devices](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/9.4)) | **ConnectsTo** is a symmetric **nonhierarchical network connection**; **DeviceTopology** concerns devices, networks and connection points. A **Device Integration Host** can expose device information regardless of whether it is stored on-device or in a datastore. Separate **offline configuration** and **online physical-device** Objects can represent different information authorities. **Modular Devices** can expose physically distinct modules in a configurable **SubDevices** collection. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** connected-to = contained-in; server/host representation = original sensing unit; **offline** desired settings = observed current state; listed supported module type = installed module. This supports multi-view representation and independent reporting/hosting questions **without** proving a deployment interval or CSAPI relationship. |
| **PADIM11-01 — PA-DIM OPC 30081 v1.02.0, §§4.1, 6.1, 6.3, 7.2 Tables 109–110** ([overview](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/6.1), [PADIMType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.2), [general rules](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/6.3)) | **PADIMType** inherits from **Devices ComponentType**, applies nameplate/health/administration/signal interfaces, and uses **IEC 61987/IRDI semantic dictionary references**. Table 109 includes **Model, SerialNumber, ProductInstanceUri, AssetId, DeviceHealth** and optional **SignalSet**, **SubDevices**, **DeviceConditionSet** with specific source-local modeling rules. | **NOT** an independent duplicate of Devices; it **specializes** the device-information model for process automation. **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** PADIMType = Devices DeviceType; a mandatory PA-DIM field = actual value present in our reference data; a dictionary classification = proof of manufacturer compliance. |
| **PADIM11-02 — PA-DIM §§7.1.2, 7.22, 7.25 and 8.2** ([ISignalSet](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.1.2), [SignalSet](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.22), [SignalType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.25), [AnalogSignalVariableType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/8.2)) | **ISignalSetType** exposes process variables; optional **SignalSet** contains typed **SignalType** Objects, including analog and discrete specializations and **SignalTag**. Signal objects and variables are standardized information-model elements. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** each SignalType Object = one physical probe/transducer; every gas electrode or PM field = a proven installed sensor; signal output = a particular 15-minute historical Observation. The source enables structured signals but does not itself prove the **physical performer**, measurement freshness, aggregation chain or cross-vendor CSAPI stream binding. |
| **PADIM11-03 — PA-DIM §§7.1.9, 7.2, 7.24** ([device condition interface](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.1.9), [PADIMType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.2), [condition set](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.24)) | **DeviceConditionSet** can carry device and component condition. The optional **GeneralDeviceConditionSetType** includes **InternalTemperature, ResidualLife, PowerOnDuration, OperationDuration** and **OperationCycleCounter**, with original IEC dictionary definitions and units. The **DeviceComponentConditions** pathway can address particular device components. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** InternalTemperature = environmental temperature; ResidualLife = battery percentage or observed sensor readiness; **condition** = source-observation QA such as Bennett F_ATemp. These facts do not establish a preferred V2 **power-first** module. Condition and signals are **distinct PA-DIM source scopes**, not one universal status field. |

### 15.3 How the donors affect the three OPEN Step 10 alternatives

**The donor findings are allowed to change the questions**, not simply “vote” for A, B or C.

| Existing working boundary | Real support from examined originals | Challenges, gaps, and possible reframing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A — physical equipment unit/assembly** | AAS asset-kind and self/co-managed component descriptions; Devices defines a physical Device and **Modular Device**, including an assembly of other units; PA-DIM adds typed device/subdevice/identity structures. | **AAS Entity management standing and Devices software ComponentType differ from physical membership**. A manufacturer product model is not Devices DeviceType; a topology need not imply enclosure ownership. An **asset-plus-description-plus-relations** perspective deserves examination alongside simply “one device as root.” |
| **B — observing/reporting arrangement** | Devices explicitly distinguishes **Device Integration Host**, communication links and online/offline values. PA-DIM separates **SignalSet**, condition and specialized process-device types; AAS can describe capabilities, relationships and asset aspects. | **None of the inspected passages defines our complete end-to-end observer → logger → archival publisher chain**, settles CSAPI **DataStream.system**, or automatically preserves result-time and probe attribution. Combining these mechanisms into such a chain would be **our synthesis**, not donor-mandated equivalence. |
| **C — continuing station/installation** | AAS broad asset scope includes equipment and systems, and type/instance life cycles. Devices can model modular equipment and device lifetime; online/offline state may support distinction from an installation's desired configuration. | **The inspected clauses do not establish a standardized station identity across serial-specific instrument replacements**, exact **time-scoped installed-as** relationships, or probe-specific Deployment geometry. AAS generic RelationshipElement may *express* a named relation when supplied with semantics, but **does not provide that replacement relation by itself**. C stays open. |
| **Potential additional framing question, NOT a fourth adopted chassis** | AAS distinguishes **physical asset vs digital shell**, **co-managed vs self-managed entities**, and **aspect-oriented Submodels**; DI distinguishes **equipment vs host representation**, **communication topology**, **online vs offline**; PA-DIM provides **specialized device-information views**. | Could the eventual DIM need an explicit **management/representation boundary and asserted-information provenance**, alongside physical/functional/installation referents? This is a legitimate **new donor-native possibility**, not a preapproved class or permission to declare a hybrid winner. It must remain an open question for Steps 12–15. |

**Cross-case consequences within current evidence limits:**

- **Dragino S31-LB** has one cabled SHT31 reporting two properties. AAS Entity or Devices Component/ConnectionPoint *could be analytical comparators*, and PA-DIM signals provide a different view of output information, but **Dragino's manual does not say it implements any of these standards**. An external cable is **not** proof of containment, and a pair of output fields are **not two self-managed Shells or PADIM SubDevices**.
- **AirGradient Open Air Max** actually documents a **multi-module product/reference assembly** and two PMS5003s, making Devices **Modular Devices** and AAS composite-asset vocabulary relevant *at design scope*. The published firmware's SPS30 alternative and spare connectors are **supported possibilities**, not installed modules. PA-DIM **SignalSet** examples offer a question about raw vs processed outputs; the original gas calibration caveat cannot be erased by borrowing an industrial signal class.
- **Campbell CR1000X + ClimaVue 50** describes **separate physical devices** connected by SDI-12; Devices **ConnectsTo** suggests a *connection relation*, not that the weather sensor is manufactured inside the logger. The 2019 program and 2026 logger status **do not constitute one online, installed OPC UA DeviceTopology**; PA-DIM's **InternalTemperature** is not ClimaVue air and the publisher's **PanelTemp** is specifically the logger wiring panel.
- **Bennett's Point** has actual dated **EE181 physical serials** and a continuing identified station. AAS type/instance and asset relationships raise questions about how to represent a returning physical unit and a persistent station, but **no time-bounded relation is mandated by these clauses**. Nor may Devices online/offline mean “before vs after swap” or PA-DIM **DeviceConditionSet** mean NOAA observation QA. The **two real NOAA measurements vs four server representation resources** remain an independent source-fidelity test, not a standards result.

### 15.4 Authority, exclusions and original-edition references

**Exactly inspected directly on 1 November 2026 (original publisher HTML):**

- **Industrial Digital Twin Association (IDTA).** *Specification of the Asset Administration Shell: Part 1 — Metamodel*, **v3.2**. [Core: AssetAdministrationShell, AssetInformation and Submodel](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/core.html); [General: Types and Instances](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/annex/general.html); [SubmodelElements: Entity, EntityType, Capability, RelationshipElement](https://industrialdigitaltwin.io/aas-specifications/IDTA-01001/v3.2/spec-metamodel/submodel-elements.html). This covers specified metamodel attributes, selected original examples and directly checked relationship rules. **Not** a full Part 2 API, AASX security/conformance test, industry digital-twin adoption survey, or actual installation history.
- **OPC Foundation.** *OPC Unified Architecture, Part 100: Devices*, **v1.05.0**, §§**3.1.5, 4.1, 4.3, 4.5.2, 4.6–4.7, 5.5, 6.1–6.3, 9.4**. [Official edition and section navigation](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/); [DeviceType Table 35](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.7); [online/offline §6.3](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/6.3); [modular devices §9.4](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/9.4). This is a bounded original textual/structural review, **not** complete Part 100 conformance analysis, a released instance NodeSet test or physical-firmware validation.
- **OPC Foundation and FieldComm Group.** *OPC UA for Process Automation Devices — Process Automation Device Information Model (PA-DIM)*, **OPC 30081 v1.02.0**, §§**4.1, 6.1, 6.3, 7.1.2, 7.1.9, 7.2 Tables 109–112, 7.22, 7.24–7.25**. [Original §6.1 model overview](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/6.1); [§7.2 PADIMType](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.2); [§7.24 condition definitions](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.24). Other listed analyzers, specific gas/flow profiles and complete Part 8 variables/Part 9 command execution were **not** reviewed in depth. PA-DIM is **separately inspected here**, not inherited as “already studied” from Part 100.

**Source confidence and limitations:** The publishers' original HTML explicitly identifies the editions above. All cited source-native claims were checked within the named clauses; the comparisons and suggested questions are **project synthesis**, not standardization-body declarations. The three product reference cases still lack authenticated field-unit records, and Bennett remains a bounded historical archive/serial attribution case. **No claim that any of the four systems implements AAS, OPC UA Devices or PA-DIM; no verified transformation, module census, active device health, or vendor/standards equivalence**. There is no wholesale primary-source verification of the remaining 182-artifact donor corpus in this step. Formal CMOS-18 bibliography/detail can be assembled from these exact edition records in later publication drafting.

### 15.5 Step 11 decision record and handoff

**Completed:** original-edition source statements, precise local standard clauses/fields, 8 accountable native-meaning records, explicit rejected equivalences, a non-forced comparison with **A/B/C plus a newly noticed management/representation question**, and case-specific limitations. The donor results **may revise or expand** the earlier framing; **none is selected as our DIM's dominant chassis**. This step did not reopen closed donor campaigns, modify the candidate DIM or Profile, declare PA-DIM/Devices conformance, build Glaux, or run a CSAPI server experiment.

**Still OPEN:** the physically/functional/installation referent and their relations; management versus physical composition; attached versus contained equipment; individual versus product type, communication address and digital Shell/AddressSpace identity; source and processing roles; **time-bounded station equipment changes**; exact source-to-Profile/Glaux mappings and real operational evidence gaps. **Gate C stays OPEN** until the other donor-specific steps and Step 15.

**Historical Step 11 handoff (Step 12 now complete in [§16](#16-step-12--isa-60-and-the-open-group-sosa-physical-functional-visible-and-managed-relationships)):** — inspect the **original Integrated Sensor Architecture (ISA)** and **The Open Group Sensor Open Systems Architecture (SOSA)** materials for physical hardware, visible/virtual members, functional realization, controllers/hosts, supported control and condition/management relations. Allow these donors to **reframe or falsify** the existing A/B/C alternatives or this step's new questions. Do **not** automatically continue into IEEE 1451/SensorML (Step 13), select a chassis, update model SHALLs, change the app or server, or call this a completed DIM.


## 16. Step 12 — ISA 6.0 and The Open Group SOSA: physical, functional, visible and managed relationships

**Status: Step 12 COMPLETE, 1 November 2026. Step 13 NEXT. Gate C remains OPEN.** We reread **four preserved primary PDFs** at their exact original passages: Army **Integrated Sensor Architecture (ISA) Software Design Document, 6.0 revision 5 (31 October 2023)**; **ISA Data Model Specification, Release 6.0 revision 12 (31 October 2023)**; **The Open Group Sensor Open Systems Architecture (SOSA) Reference Architecture Edition 1.1, C239 (June 2023, September erratum)**; and the **separate Edition 1.0 SOSA Domain-Specific Data Model (April 2022)**. Original source evidence is distinguished from the project’s proposed applicability. This is **not** the separate W3C/OGC Sensor, Observation, Sample, and Actuator / Semantic Sensor Network (SOSA/SSN) lineage, which belongs in Step 14.

**Why the donor exercise matters:** One source chiefly describes **who can be discovered, what it exposes, and which actions can reach it through software**; the other explicitly describes a **bounded Sensor** with physical, functional, product/implementation, hosting and management distinctions. A common client that treats every advertised network participant as one identified physical sensor—or every function and manager as one internal hardware part—would misstate the source. **Neither source thereby settles the DIM chassis** or proves the three manufacturer reference cases implement the standards. The earlier [ISA/SOSA challenge research](../Donor_Challenge/04_Other_Structural_Challenges.md) is preserved for historical comparison, but the findings below were **checked afresh from the original documents**, without the old obligation to defend F-01 through F-05.

### 16.1 What ISA actually describes

The **ISA Software Design Document §3 (printed pp. 6–7; PDF pp. 14–15)** explicitly describes ISA as a **software-only interoperability solution** that relies on the host platform’s networks, computing and authoritative time source. In **§4.2.1 (printed p. 9; PDF p. 17)**, an **ISA Component** implements the ISA interface and participates in ISA services; an **ISA Controller** is a component providing core services to connected participants. A joined network participant is **not by definition a physical sensing chip, a piece of equipment, or a separately serialized unit**.

The decisive passage is **§4.2.2 (printed pp. 10–12; PDF pp. 18–20), including Figure 2**, visually rechecked in the original PDF. An **ISA Composition** reaches the wider network through a **Composition Interface Component (CIC)**. Composition members may use ISA or their native interfaces. A **visible member** is announced to the network with its own capabilities/status; an **opaque member** is not announced, and its exposed capabilities appear as the CIC’s. A visible non-ISA member may be a **virtual ISA Component**, with an ISA identifier assigned in relation to its CIC. Of the visible members, **transparent** means externally taskable and **translucent** means visible but not externally taskable. The CIC can itself execute, broker, or relay a requested task. **Visibility, taskability, reporting source, interface identity and actual physical equipment are different source-level questions**. They are not synonyms for contained-versus-external, installed-versus-uninstalled, or healthy-versus-faulted.

**ISA's Component Capability Description (CCD)** is explicitly described in **§5.1.1 (printed pp. 15–16; PDF pp. 23–24)**. A component **declares Properties** about information it can report regarding itself, **Observables** describing observation information it can publish, and **Commands** it can be asked to execute. It also reports capability **ready** and where applicable **reporting** states; actual **Status** messages carry property values, while Requests and Responses concern commands. These are not all one “sensor characteristic,” actual observation, executed command or current equipment condition.

The original **ISA Data Model Specification, release 6.0 revision 12**, lists **Properties / Observables / Commands** by service and **Capability Bins (§2, printed pp. 3–8; PDF pp. 18–23)**. Examples include entity detection, meteorology, electrical and general-purpose capabilities. Its **Document Notes (PDF p. 2)** independently distinguish extension markings **Custom**, **Nominated** and **Accepted**; **Accepted** means suggested for use and expected to transition later, **not already standardized in that release**. Even a primary PDF therefore cannot be mined as if every listed extension had uniform normative standing.

### 16.2 What The Open Group SOSA actually describes

In **Edition 1.1 §4.1.1.1–.4 (C239, printed pp. 21–22; PDF pp. 47–48)**, the primary **SOSA Sensor** is made of **one or more Sensor Hardware Elements**, which may be physically grouped or distributed **on or within its host platform**. A **Host Platform** is an associated **vehicle/structure/pod** which may supply power, cooling, navigation, network and tasking. The platform may also contain **processing outside the Sensor boundary**. The document restricts its Sensor types to **Communications, Electro-Optical/Infrared, Electronic Warfare, Radar and Signals Intelligence**, individually or combined. Thus **SOSA's whole Sensor is compelling source-native scope evidence**, but not a general definition that automatically covers our environmental monitors, or a claim that every mount/platform is a part of the sensor.

The **Edition 1.1 §4.2, Table 4.2-1 (printed pp. 22–24; PDF pp. 48–50)** and **§5.1 (printed pp. 29–30; PDF pp. 55–56)** separate **logical SOSA Modules**, which encapsulate functions, behavior and interactions, from **SOSA Infrastructure**, which hosts execution and interconnects. One SOSA Sensor may implement only the relevant subset of Modules; it need not produce one physical board or system resource per Module. The **System Manager** handles Sensor hardware/software discovery, configuration, control, health and security; the **Task Manager** coordinates **mission** tasking, priorities and outputs. These source-defined management and mission roles are distinguishable from one another and from the physical device itself.

**Edition 1.1 §6 and §6.1 (printed pp. 38–39; PDF pp. 64–65)** distinguishes **managed** from **unmanaged** components and permits **indirect management**, where a second component implements a management interface for the first. It also explicitly separates **in-band management** over the normal message interconnect from **out-of-band hardware management** that can remain available when the normal network is unavailable. **Who exposes management status need not be the physical component whose status is being managed**, nor does an available System Manager imply that a specific bit of health data was obtained.

The **Edition 1.0 Domain-Specific Data Model (DSDM)** provides additional **edition-specific** relationship definitions, **not automatically Edition 1.1 cardinalities**:

- **§1.1.3.15, printed p. 13 (PDF p. 13):** **Component_** is a physical **or software** unit with functional boundaries, purpose and interfaces. **§§1.1.3.68–.69, pp. 36–37:** **Module_** and **ModuleImplementation** distinguish defined functional units from executable implementation association.
- **§1.1.3.82–.83, printed p. 42:** **Product** is a manufacturer’s hardware or software product; **ProductInstance** associates a manufactured Product with a **functional Component_**. A manufactured product, functional Component and executable ModuleImplementation are **not the same source type**.
- **§1.1.3.54, printed pp. 30–31:** **HostPlatform** associates a Sensor with a Platform; **§1.1.3.92, printed p. 46:** **SensorComponent** expresses **component is part of Sensor**. These are **two different relationship kinds**, with original DSDM cardinalities attached to **that original edition only**.
- **§§1.1.3.89–.91, printed pp. 44–46:** the **Sensor** carries separate **healthStatus, operationalState and configurationState**, while **SensorBuiltInTest** and **SensorBITResult** separately concern a test’s association and its **executed, dated result**. Health status, configuration, fault, mission task and executed built-in-test result are not one scalar meaning.

### 16.3 Accountable original-source records and non-equivalences

These **ISA12 / SOSA12 codes are local research provenance identifiers only**. They are not standards-assigned fields, a newly accepted DIM taxonomy or a conformance record. All proposed implications are **analyst interpretations** of the original terms.

| Record and original source passage | Native meaning directly supported | What we must **not** claim |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **ISA12-01 — SDD 6.0r5 §§3, 4.2.1, printed pp. 6–9** | Software-only architecture, host dependencies, ISA Component and Controller as interface/service participants; virtual ISA network uses actual underlying networks. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** network participant = independently verified physical board or sensor System; Controller = physical host/platform or general CSAPI producer. |
| **ISA12-02 — SDD §4.2.2 / Fig. 2, printed pp. 10–12** | Composition and CIC can expose **visible, opaque, virtual, transparent or translucent** members; member capabilities may be attributed to the member or mediated by CIC. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** externally discoverable children = installed physical constituents; CIC-reported data = proof CIC is the actual observing hardware; hidden = physically absent; visible = remotely taskable. |
| **ISA12-03 — SDD §5.1.1 and DMS §2, printed pp. 15–16 and 3–8** | CCD declarations: **Property**, **Observable**, **Command**; readiness/reporting status, actual property reports, request/response and observable data have differing roles. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** a declared capability = current value, physical quantity, completed command or live observation; an ISA Capability Bin = manufacturer model/kind. |
| **ISA12-04 — DMS 6.0r12 Document Notes, PDF p. 2** | **Custom/Nominated/Accepted** extension markings are distinct from standardized entries and may change on adoption. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** every DMS entry, especially marked extensions, is a uniform normative ISA 6.0 requirement or a ready-made DIM class. |
| **SOSA12-01 — Edition 1.1 C239 §4.1.1.1–.4, printed pp. 21–22** | One **SOSA Sensor** can contain multiple distributed hardware elements; **Host Platform** is a distinct mounting/provisioning context; **Sensor Pod** is another source-specific relationship. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** one enclosure/chip = whole SOSA Sensor; platform = component automatically; published five-defense-modality Sensor definition = universal environmental/industrial sensor domain. |
| **SOSA12-02 — Edition 1.1 C239 §4.2 Table 4.2-1, §5.1, printed pp. 22–24, 29–30** | Functional **Modules** and **Infrastructure** distinguish what a Sensor does from execution-support hardware/software; **System Manager** and **Task Manager** have different responsibilities. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** one function = one physical module or one separately identified System; mission task management = configuration/health management or a CSAPI Procedure. |
| **SOSA12-03 — Edition 1.1 C239 §§6–6.1, printed pp. 38–39** | Managed/unmanaged and direct/indirect management; normal **in-band** versus separate **out-of-band** hardware access. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** manager’s interface identity = physical target, any returned status = target’s directly observed current state; managed membership = mounted physical part. |
| **SOSA12-04 — Edition 1.0 DSDM §§1.1.3.15, .68–.69, .82–.83, pp. 13, 36–37, 42** | **Component_** can be hardware or software; **Module_**, **ModuleImplementation**, **Product** and **ProductInstance** are distinct concepts/associations. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** manufactured product = functional Component = executable Module = hardware element = individual station; an ID in the source data model proves real serial-number attribution. |
| **SOSA12-05 — Edition 1.0 DSDM §§1.1.3.54, .92, pp. 30–31, 46** | **HostPlatform** association and **SensorComponent** part-of association have different participants/meaning. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** hosted-on = permanent physical constituent of Sensor; these Edition 1.0 relationship cardinalities are inherited as Edition 1.1/CSAPI/DIM requirements. |
| **SOSA12-06 — Edition 1.0 DSDM §§1.1.3.89–.91, pp. 44–46** | **Sensor health/operational/configuration** state fields and **built-in-test/dated test result** are different source statements. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** configuration = health = BIT result = action outcome = source observation QA. A publisher’s possible status definition does not establish an actual installed-unit status. |

### 16.4 How this donor evidence challenges the OPEN boundaries and the four cases

**A — primary physical equipment unit/assembly.** SOSA's **multi-element Sensor** is a serious **native alternative** to an always-single-enclosure definition. Its distinction between a Sensor and Host Platform prevents automatically treating the mounted host as a manufactured sensor part. Yet SOSA's specific five-modality scope and technical conformance architecture mean it **cannot simply become the V2 universal chassis**. ISA adds a different challenge: a network-visible Component **does not have to correspond one-to-one to physical equipment**. Choosing equipment as the primary referent would still need independent discovery/management representation, physical type-versus-instance evidence, and externally attached components.

**B — observing/reporting arrangement.** ISA's CIC can **broker or relay** capabilities and present a member opaquely or virtually; its CCD can declare observables and commands distinct from actual reports. SOSA separates **functional Module**, realization by Component, **System Manager versus Task Manager**, and Sensor data path. These strengthen the need to distinguish **who observed, who exposed the capability, who processed/published the result, and who accepted a command**. However, neither inspected source establishes a general **station-led versus probe-led OGC DataStream.system** rule, exact physical-observer provenance for all managed outputs, or runtime conformance of our Glaux case. The boundary hypothesis cannot be accepted by borrowing these interface names.

**C — continuing installation/station.** SOSA Host Platform is **associated but distinct** from a SOSA Sensor; ISA's platform hosts physical infrastructure for interface participants. Neither is by itself equivalent to a **persistent environmental monitoring station with dated instrument role replacements** like Bennett's Point. The DSDM ProductInstance and SensorComponent relationships do not supply an automatic **effective-from/effective-to** equipment history, and an opaque/virtual ISA identity is not a durable unit serial. Station continuity, physical replacement/role intervals and actual history **remain independent issues for later evidence/donor review**.

**Additional open dimensions not reducible to A/B/C:** source-native **external exposure versus physical existence** (ISA); **functional module versus physical/software implementation versus manufactured Product** (SOSA DSDM); **hosting versus constituent membership** (SOSA); **system housekeeping versus mission/task behavior**, including **direct/indirect management** (SOSA); and the provenance of **the reporter/relay versus actual equipment subject** (ISA and SOSA). These are **meaning questions, not five new classes or an automatically winning hybrid**. Their effect on final architecture must await the rest of Phase C.

| Case used as a counterexample, NOT an ISA/SOSA conformance claim | What the original ISA/SOSA meanings force us to ask | Unsupported assertion to refuse |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Dragino S31-LB** | Could an interface expose **node-level capabilities on behalf of a cabled SHT31** while preserving one physical probe and its two outputs? Does the reporting node differ from source of an environmental quantity? | The actual Dragino node **is** an ISA CIC or SOSA Module; two outputs imply two hardware or network members; a documented LoRaWAN design is an observed session. |
| **AirGradient Open Air Max** | Does the **two-PMS5003 multi-module assembly** require separate physical/functional/module identities? Could a common interface omit or virtualize physical child devices without asserting they are absent? | The product **implements** ISA opaque/virtual members or SOSA components; each raw/calculated gas channel is a separately authenticated sensor; source examples verify calibration. |
| **Campbell CR1000X + ClimaVue 50** | How does a **separate SDI-12 sensor and logger** compare to a network-representing component or SOSA functional processing chain? Which physical parts can be called host, external or constituent in the **actual evidence**? | CR1000X is certified as an ISA Controller, a SOSA Host Platform or a deployed SOSA Sensor; a later separate logger Status example is a live weather-system condition record. |
| **Bennett's Point** | Can we represent **one continuing station**, different EE181 physical probe serials across dated use, a logger-averaged result, and the **separate transmitter/archive reporter**, without losing who is directly exposed, who senses, and who is historically attributed? | ISA network IDs or SOSA DSDM SensorComponent associations **supply the actual 16 April swap minute, native CSAPI Deployment, probe-specific coordinates or source resultTime**; four server Observation resources mean four physical measurements. |

The correct next step is **not** to promote a physical-unit, functional Sensor, management Component or platform relationship to an adopted DIM merely because The Open Group or ISA has a similar label.

Record authority limits and permit additional native concepts; **do not** perform the Step 14 SAPIENT/OMS/WoT review, decide the chassis (Step 15), or implement new Profile/Glaux code during Step 13.


## 17. Step 13 — IEEE 1451 transducer/channel evidence and SensorML 3.0 process/system semantics

**Status: Step 13 COMPLETE, 2 November 2026. Step 14 NEXT; Gate C OPEN.** This bounded donor-native review combines the **publisher's official IEEE 1451.0-2024 scope**, **NIST-confirmed 2024-linked public IEEE 1451 Playground documentation**, the owner's **14 October 2026 public-source reconstruction and manifest**, and the **full approved OGC SensorML 3.0 standard (OGC 23-000, 2025-07-16)**. It does **not** import the older five DIM foundation meanings as mandatory slots, claim all IEEE/ISO technical requirements verified, adopt an IEEE↔OGC binding, determine a winning chassis, or perform Step 14's SAPIENT/OMS/SSN/WoT review.

### 17.1 What we learned in ordinary language

**IEEE 1451 helps us understand a sensing channel and its technical self-description, not automatically an entire installed System.** Its currently published architecture separates the **Application (APP)** as network client, **Network Capable Application Processor (NCAP)** as network server, and **Transducer Interface Module (TIM)** on the transducer side. The same published scope distinguishes network services, transducer services, **Transducer Electronic Data Sheets (TEDS)**, and reading or controlling actual transducer data. This architecture has genuine relevance to sensing devices and their communication, but **an APP, NCAP, TIM, transducer channel, physical sensing element and observed property are not six interchangeable physical equipment identities**. [IEEE Standards Association, IEEE 1451.0-2024, published 26 June 2024, public scope](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/1451.0/11001/).

**The public technical depth is substantial.** The [IEEE 1451 Playground Transducer Channel TEDS page](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/transducer-channel-teds/), which explicitly cites the 2024 standard's **§6.5 and Table 19**, enumerates channel type, calibration key, physical units, design measurement limits, worst-case uncertainty, self-test, sample representation, sampling periods, delays, timing source, timing error, modes and buffering. Its [Meta TEDS page](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/meta-teds/) cites **§6.4 and Tables 13, 49–50** and documents a module-level UUID, maximum channel count, control/vector grouping and channel proxies. [NIST's 1 December 2024 news report](https://www.nist.gov/news-events/news/2024/12/nist-and-university-researchers-present-online-tool-support-ieee-standards) explicitly identifies the Playground as an IEEE 1451.0-2024 teaching/validation tool; its [2025 IEEE publication record](https://www.nist.gov/publications/ieee-1451-playground-web-based-tool-standard-education) independently documents its development and testing. **These original current-revision-linked sources are legitimate detailed technical donors.** They are **not** a complete independent reading of the licensed 2024 normative SHALLs or a fielded TIM/TEDS test.

**SensorML 3.0 contributes a broader process/system-description mechanism.** Its core model treats hardware detectors, physical sensor systems, and computational transformations as **processes with identified inputs, outputs, parameters and methodology**. It distinguishes **SimpleProcess**, **AggregateProcess**, **PhysicalComponent**, and **PhysicalSystem**, supplies **typeOf** inheritance to a more general process description, supports configuration and modes, uses **SWE Common** data structures, and has separate **history, validTime and Deployment** mechanisms. This makes it possible to ask where technical channel descriptions might fit while preserving a multi-equipment system or measurement-processing chain. **It does not itself declare an IEEE TEDS field to be a SensorML item, nor resolve the physical observer/CSAPI DataStream producer or generate actual Observations.** [OGC 23-000, version 3.0, approved 2 June 2025 and published 16 July 2025](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html).

| **14 October 2026 MERLIN IEEE 1451 public-source reconstruction and source manifest**, user-preserved Library files | **PRIOR_PROJECT_TECHNICAL_RESEARCH:** examined source/trail, edition guards and quantitative channel information were recovered and compared with original links, rather than dismissed because a full PDF is not licensed. [Earlier documented correction](../Donor_Challenge/03_IEEE1451_Challenge.md#0-corrective-evidence-finding--ieee-1451-is-technically-usable-as-a-dim-donor-28-october-2026). | A prior report is **not** original IEEE authority, a new frozen donor, a final adopted Profile or proof the Playground implements every aspect of 2024. Historical NIST/STIM explanations and ISO/IEC/IEEE 21450/21451 **2010** editions remain separate evidence from IEEE 1451.0 **2024**. |
| **OGC Sensor Model Language (SensorML) Encoding Standard**, **OGC 23-000, v3.0.0**, published **16 July 2025**: [approved HTML](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html), [approved PDF](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.pdf) | **FULL_PUBLIC_NORMATIVE_TEXT_INSPECTED for selected clauses**, including **§§7.1–7.2, 8.2.2, 8.2.4–8.2.9, 8.3–8.6, 8.8–8.9** and relevant original Requirements **2–5, 11–15, 19, 23–24, 31–34, 38–44**. The official PDF's **Figure 18 (p. 66)** and **Figure 22 (p. 75)** were visually checked, confirming physical-system process composition and a separate deployment description. | Exact clauses are scoped to the **standard's own derived models, encodings and requirements classes**, not to our future DIM/Profile as copied SHALLs. No full JSON-schema conformance test, CSAPI Part 1/Part 2 mapping or actual profile-aware Glaux evaluation was run. |
| **Historical and proposed interoperability context**, [NIST IEEE 1451–OGC SWE demonstration (2009)](https://www.nist.gov/publications/service-oriented-sensor-data-interoperability-ieee-1451-smart-transducers), [NIST-hosted proposed IEEE 1451/WoT ontology (2025)](https://www.nist.gov/publications/ieee-14510-based-web-thing-wot-ontology) | **HISTORICAL_IMPLEMENTATION / AUTHOR_RESEARCH** for the possibility of carrying source semantics into another representation. Useful precedents to keep on the bibliography trail. | Neither paper proves **an adopted IEEE–OGC correspondence**, an actual 2024 CSAPI Profile, a source-certified 1451 mapping, or current Glaux interoperability. The Web of Things substantive donor review remains **Step 14**, not this step. |

### 17.3 IEEE 1451 — module, addressed channel, technical metadata and actual readings

**Source-native architecture:** The IEEE publisher's scope assigns **network-facing APP/NCAP interactions** and **NCAP-to-TIM transducer services** different responsibilities. Reading a TEDS is different from reading actual sampled data; setup/control services are different from successfully performing a requested action. A TIM can have **multiple addressed channels** within the source description, and the Playground explicitly describes shared Meta TEDS information and channel-specific TEDS. **This does not prove a TIM is one chip, every physical sensing element is one channel, a channel is an independently managed unit, or a channel is one CSAPI DataStream.**

| Step 13 record, exact public locator | **What the source documentation actually makes available** | **Meaning boundary / rejected equivalence** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **IE13-01 — IEEE 1451.0-2024 publisher scope** ([official](https://standards.ieee.org/ieee/1451.0/11001/)) | **APP, NCAP, TIM**, network-service and transducer-service commands/replies, **TEDS** versus transducer-data access. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** service role = physical hardware identity, process performer, type description or CSAPI System UID. Official public scope does **not** establish a full structure diagram, normative TIM/channel hardware cardinality or required client Profile. |
| **IE13-02 — Playground Meta TEDS §6.4 / Tables 13, 49–50** ([original](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/meta-teds/)) | Shared module/channel-access description: **UUID**, **MaxChan**, **CGroup**, **VGroup**, geographic vector grouping, **Proxies**, shared hold-offs and **TestTime**. These demonstrate module/shared scope and group/proxy relationships distinct from individual channel metadata. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** UUID = authenticated individual vendor serial or CSAPI UID; **MaxChan** or a group member list = count of physical chips; grouped/proxied channel = a proven physically installed independent system. The exact 2024 mandatory/conditional field rules are **NOT VERIFIED**. |
| **IE13-03 — Playground TransducerChannel TEDS §6.5 / Table 19** ([original](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/transducer-channel-teds/)) | Channel-level **CalKey, ChanType, PhyUnits, LowLimit, HiLimit, OError, SelfTest, MRange**; **Sample** digital representation and **DataSet** repeat/origin/step/units/pre-trigger semantics. These are **detailed technical descriptors**, not vague generic characteristics. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** design operating range = actual observed values, worst-case uncertainty = measured error of a specific 2024 sample, calibration key = verified successful calibration, channel type = manufactured part or a whole-System product model. |
| **IE13-04 — Playground TransducerChannel TEDS timing/sampling** ([original](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/transducer-channel-teds/)) | **SPeriod, UpdateT, WSetupT, RSetupT, WarmUpT, RDelayT, TestTime**, sample-time **TimeSrc, InPropDl, OutPropD, TSError** and sampling/buffering modes. This is valuable for **where sampled time comes from and how a device/channel can behave**, when those attributes are actually supplied. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** a published sampling-period capability = effective unit configuration or actual sample timestamps; **TSError** = a measured historical observation's QA flag; sensor buffer/report time = true phenomenonTime or resultTime. No device values were acquired. |
| **IE13-05 — Playground General/Optional TEDS and separate family pages** ([General](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/gereral-teds/), [Optional](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/optional-teds/), [PHY](https://iml.ubi.pt/playground/docs/ieee1451/teds/phy-teds/)) | **TLV** generic TEDS format; documented **Calibration, Frequency Response, Transfer Function, text-based, Geographic Location, Units Extension, Manufacturer-defined, Security, Time Synchronization** and other technical description options. **PHY TEDS** explicitly refers to the **separate IEEE 1451.5-2007** wireless publication. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** every optional TEDS is automatically installed, populated or readable; a calibration description certifies current calibration; an IEEE geographic-location description = actual deployed probe geometry; **1451.5-2007 PHY TEDS requiredness = 1451.0-2024 obligation**. NIST/Playground documentation makes technical reuse possible without that claim. |

**Direct donor value:** IEEE 1451 supports **channel-level quantitative and behavioral meaning**, including limitations, conversions/data representation, calibration context, timing and grouped channels. This is much richer than a manufacturer-model string, a generic “temperature” label or an undifferentiated sensor-device list. **The bounded DIM question is how a particular channel description relates to physical equipment and an observation/reporting function**, not how to make TIM or NCAP the universal whole-System chassis.

### 17.4 SensorML 3.0 — a broad process model, not a renamed IEEE hierarchy

**SensorML's normative conceptual unit is a Process.** Under **§§7.1–7.2, Requirements 2–5**, its process descriptions concern **inputs, outputs, parameters, methodology, unique identification and discovery metadata**. A physical detector can take an environmental **ObservableProperty** as input and yield a SWE Common **Quantity**, while a mathematical correction can be a non-physical process. That allows descriptions of an actual sensing path without requiring one output per physical child. **SensorML itself is a process-description standard; actual observations and data streams remain distinct information artifacts**. [Original SensorML §§7.1–7.2](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html#_process_definitions); [§8.2.9.1 and Requirement 11](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html#_inputs_outputs_and_parameters).

| Step 13 record and original clause | **Native standard meaning** | **Limit on the eventual DIM/CSAPI/Profile** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **SML13-01 — §§7.1–7.2, Requirements 2–5** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | Process-based **physical or computational operations**, each with source-defined inputs, outputs, parameters, methodology and identifying/discovery metadata. Model/instance descriptions are allowed. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** process schema = actual completed process execution or individual timestamped observation; process UID = authenticated device serial merely by being unique. |
| **SML13-02 — §§8.3–8.6, Requirements 19, 23–24, 31–33** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | **SimpleProcess** (no internal sub-process modeling; position irrelevant), **AggregateProcess** (sub-processes and explicit connections; position irrelevant), **PhysicalComponent** (physical process not further decomposed where position matters), **PhysicalSystem** (physical aggregated processing system with components and connections). Original **PhysicalSystem Figure 18** directly checked in the official PDF. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** all four are physical equipment subclasses; every IEEE channel requires a PhysicalComponent; every connected logger/probe pair must be one intrinsic manufactured unit; every component connection means permanent physical containment. Context and intended modeling granularity matter. |
| **SML13-03 — §8.2.9.1 and Requirement 11** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | Inputs/outputs/parameters can reference **ObservableProperty** or **SWE Common data components**, including Quantity and aggregates. Related measured values can be expressed as **DataRecord** rather than fragmented physical components. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** output = physical chip, an IEEE Channel TEDS = SensorML output by definition, and every DataRecord field = a different CSAPI DataStream. The actual semantics must be source-bound. |
| **SML13-04 — §8.2.9.3, Requirements 13–15, plus §8.8** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | **typeOf** links a more specific process description to a **resolvable general process**; **configuration/Settings and modes** express selected/restricted options; SensorML distinguishes configurable descriptions from **configured** ones. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** typeOf = arbitrary text model number or a source-native IEEE hardware type; supported default/mode = demonstrated installed effective setting. No model instance or active configuration can be invented for our three reference cases. |
| **SML13-05 — §§8.2.2.6, 8.2.4–8.2.8** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | **validTime** qualifies the validity of a **description**, **capabilities** can qualify process outputs for discovery, **characteristics** can include **power, weight, material, lifetime**, and **history Events** can record maintenance/calibration/configuration changes. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** process validTime = the phenomenonTime of an actual observation; power characteristic = battery remaining capacity or live health; an optional history carrier = documented installation or event. |
| **SML13-06 — §8.9, Requirement 44, Deployment and DeployedSystem, Figure 22** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | **Deployment** separately describes where, why and how a system is deployed. It can reference **platform, location and deployedSystem**, with a **DeployedSystem** referencing the **system** and its **configuration**. Original PDF Figure 22 checked; the Deployment description is separate from the physical/process identity. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** a manufacturer brochure = a deployed system; published station point = verified separate probe geometry; a source equipment-use date automatically creates an exact Deployment event/clock; a generic deployment class resolves the **station-led versus probe-led DataStream** decision. |
| **SML13-07 — §8.2.9.2 / §8.3.2 and §8.4** ([official](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html)) | A **ProcessMethod** describes how inputs/parameters produce outputs; aggregate processes include **connections** between component processes. The standard can express meaningful **processing chains** without assuming the compute engine/network implementation. | **REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE:** a declared method is proof of runtime execution, a manufacturer example program is the installed program, or modeled connections prove real links and processing. The Campbell 2019 source anomaly/2021 unseen program boundary remains. |

**Important precise normative scope:** these SensorML Requirements are **obligations within their identified OGC requirements classes and appropriate encodings/implementations**. They are **not new SHALLs authored for this DIM or CSAPI Profile**. The original [SensorML §8.6](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html) uses a **weather station** as a typical example of a PhysicalSystem and explicitly notes physical and computational elements in a sensing system; it does **not** say that every commercial weather-sensor output, logger Status value, station DataStream or physical unit must become a separate SensorML System or Procedure.

### 17.5 Four concrete cases: where the standards illuminate and where evidence stops

| Source-native case and ground truth | **High-value IEEE 1451 question** (**analyst hypothesis**, NOT vendor compliance) | **High-value SensorML question** (**candidate mapping**, NOT tested) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Dragino S31-LB (§9):** one external **SHT31** probe measures both temperature and humidity; the reporting **LoRaWAN node** is distinct. | Could channel-specific descriptions preserve **two measurement functions/units/ranges/sampling behavior** without inventing **two physical probes**? Who would correspond to a TIM/NCAP-like role, *if* native IEEE services existed? **No actual IEEE TEDS or channel address is in the Dragino packet.** | Could the node and external probe be described as **different physical processes linked to output paths**, with manufacturer product model separate from a particular unit and its settings? **Do not force external cable to mean inside-one-enclosure membership.** |
| **AirGradient Open Air Max (§10):** two **physical PMS5003** devices plus other modules; 17 device example fields and different cloud example fields; gas raw signals have calibration warnings. | Could **module/shared metadata, channel grouping, quantitative range/uncertainty and raw-vs-calibrated output meaning** preserve the difference between physical sensor count and many reported quantities? **No TEDS or validated gas calibration record was obtained.** | Could an aggregate of physical and computational processes represent **multiple sensing modules**, reporting/processing stages and distinct raw/corrected outputs? Source firmware/hardware versions and example cloud paths must **not** be treated as one verified pipeline. |
| **Campbell CR1000X + first-generation ClimaVue 50 (§11):** **one integrated weather instrument** defines fourteen **aR7!** output positions; a distinct **CR1000X** runs historical example transformations, while a separate 2026 publisher Status message is **logger-only**. | Could a **multichannel transducer description** organize diverse sensor output paths without treating every output as an independently identified chip? **SDI-12 address is not a documented IEEE channel address, and there is no 1451 compliance proof.** | Could **physical sensor, logger, program-derived conversion/carry-forward process, and logger Status** remain distinct? **Simple/Aggregate process meaning** is promising, but the 2019 code listing is not compiled/verified for an installed station. |
| **Bennett's Point (§12):** persistent station **acebpmet** and logger **13916**, EE181 **222016000109CE → 21281600077814** across 16 April 2024 maintenance, two **real selected** 15-minute temperature summaries. | Could technical descriptions be scoped **per physical EE181 unit and measurement channel** while keeping the **station** referent stable? **No source operator TEDS, timing-error fields, actual sensor-native sample logs or 1451 identity is documented.** | Could **typeOf, history, validTime, and separate Deployment/DeployedSystem** help express **same product kind / different units / dated use** without making the station position the probe's independent location? The exact swap minute and true resultTime are **not supplied**. The ongoing station/probe/stream decision **remains open**. |

### 17.6 What is a real alignment, what is merely analogous, and what stays open?

**Supported directly as concepts in the separately cited originals:**

1. IEEE 1451 has **network-side and transducer-side service roles** plus **TEDS**; Playground current-linked sources have **Meta** (module-level) and **TransducerChannel** metadata with granular technical attributes.
2. SensorML has **identified process descriptions, type/instance reference, physical/logical process distinctions, inputs/outputs/parameters, process methods, configuration, validity/history and deployment**.
3. Both bodies supply useful ways to think about the **described subject and data semantics**, but **neither original source checked here declares an exact IEEE Channel → SensorML Component/Output/Capability/Procedure/CSAPI DataStream mapping**. The public IEEE source does not require that our model be reorganized around NCAP/TIM; SensorML source semantics are broader and need actual property definitions.

**Candidate correspondence questions — not source-declared equivalence or adopted rules:**

| IEEE-origin semantic / scope | Potential native SensorML/SWE carrier to **evaluate later**, never assume | Why it needs specific source/standards work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| TIM/shared **Meta TEDS**, channel group and proxy relationships | Identified physical process/equipment description, appropriate process/connection or source-specific characterization | Need **verified identity, module/physical granularity and proxy semantics**; no automatic one channel = one Component. |
| Channel **PhyUnits**, limits, uncertainty, self-test and calibration context | **SWE Quantity/QuantityRange**, SensorML **capabilities/characteristics**, parameterized process or explicitly source-qualified metadata | **Design range** and **worst-case uncertainty** are not a real observation quality outcome; exact 2024 datatype/conditionality and source-defined property URIs require checking. |
| Channel **sample format, sampling mode, timing source and delays** | SWE data components/stream schema, SensorML outputs/modes/settings; possibly separately authored observation/time provenance | SensorML output description is **not** a CSAPI observation result with validated phenomenonTime/resultTime, nor a producer's actual runtime decoder. |
| IEEE **TEDS retrieval vs transducer data read and control** | SensorML **description versus method/capability/configuration**; later CSAPI observation/command bindings as separately justified | A declared service is not a completed act or a CSAPI Procedure; original 2024 normative obligations and translation must remain source-qualified. |

**The new problem these sources make visible:** **how to preserve a small, technically rich, channel-level description and its owning physical/contextual scope inside a genuinely whole-System model**. The sensor itself, its measured property, addressable channel, self-description, sampled signal, process, and API resource have **different potential identities**. A common client should not force one of them to stand for all the others. This is an empirically grounded challenge, **not an eighth universal DIM class or a completed solution**.

### 17.7 Authority gaps, not invented fixes, and handoff

**Direct source verification achieved:** official IEEE 1451.0-2024 publisher page; NIST 2024 Playground announcement and 2025 publication; **original Playground Meta, Channel, General, Optional, PHY and Intro tutorial pages**, including their *different edition* references; original **OGC SensorML 3.0 official HTML and PDF**, with two UML figures visually checked; and re-use of the **14 October 2026 user-preserved IEEE reconstruction/source manifest**. Original source locators are above. The earlier [correction ensuring IEEE receives serious donor consideration](../Donor_Challenge/03_IEEE1451_Challenge.md) remains valid, but this step contributes fresh cross-case and SensorML-specific analysis. No original standard text or Playground source code was rehosted in this GitHub report.

**Still unverified:** complete licensed **IEEE 1451.0-2024** normative rule text, conditional field mandatoryness, cardinalities, conformance and exact delta from 2007/2010; the linked IEEE schema repository's inaccessible contents; whether any four selected sources is a real IEEE 1451 implementation; actual TIM/NCAP/channel/metadata identity joins; effective sensor calibration/sampling/timing values; any executable IEEE→SensorML/SWE/CSAPI conversion or independent conformance test; what a generic Glaux client could recover; and unresolved station **DataStream ownership, average period and resultTime** problems. **UNKNOWN stays UNKNOWN**. No other candidate donor was excluded or demoted because its vocabulary differs.

**Impact on the open Step 10 frames, not a chassis selection:** a physical equipment-primary frame must accommodate **channel-specific functional descriptions** without multiplying physical devices. An observing/reporting-arrangement frame must distinguish **sensor-produced raw output, processing method, measurement data and network mediation** without losing the physical unit's identity or equipment management. A continuing station frame must allow **per-unit technical descriptions and dated deployments** but not infer installed state from a product datasheet or a source framework. The extra **management/representation granularity** already identified in Steps 11–12 remains a question, and IEEE adds **channel access and timing/quality description scope**. None of A/B/C, an apparent hybrid, IEEE 1451 or SensorML 3.0 is selected as the *sole* organizing chassis in Step 13.

**Changes:** append only this **cumulative workbook section** and update **the existing plan/README execution checkpoints**; do not rewrite the old IEEE challenge, controlled corpus, prior PITON, candidate DIM/Profile, Glaux app, Cloudflare deployment or 52°North/Oracle CSAPI resources. **Gate C remains OPEN.**

**Historical Step 13 handoff (Step 14 now complete in [§18](#18-step-14--sapient-v2-oms-30-w3cogc-sosa-ssn-and-web-of-things-observation-action-time-and-representation)):** — review the original **SAPIENT v2, OGC Observations, Measurements and Samples (OMS) 3.0, W3C/OGC SOSA/SSN and W3C Web of Things** source texts for the still-open observing, reporting, control, time and representation questions. Keep editions and formal-versus-proposed mappings distinct, allow **new donor-native alternatives**, and stop **before Step 15 chassis comparison or any DIM/Profile implementation**.


## 18. Step 14 — SAPIENT v2, OMS 3.0, W3C/OGC SOSA-SSN and Web of Things: observation, action, time and representation

**Status: Step 14 COMPLETE, 2 November 2026. Step 15 NEXT; Gate C remains OPEN.** This is the last targeted **original-donor review** of Phase C, not a chassis decision. It examines the **BSI Flex 335 SAPIENT v2.0:2024-03** original 116-page PDF plus pinned Dstl protobuf files, the owner's original complete **OGC Observations, Measurements and Samples (OMS) 3.0 (OGC 20-082r4)** PDF, the published **W3C/OGC Semantic Sensor Network (SSN) 2017 Recommendation** and the newer **26 October 2026 “2023 Edition” Working Draft**, and original **W3C Web of Things (WoT) Architecture 1.1 and Thing Description 1.1 Recommendations**. No claim of full 182-source coverage, new standard equivalence, new DIM class or an executed client test follows from this work.

### 18.1 What the four donors contribute in ordinary language

**SAPIENT v2 describes message/network participation and operational actions.** A Node registers capabilities, supported modes, task definitions, status reporting and equipment information; later StatusReport, Task, TaskAck, DetectionReport and Alert messages describe different kinds of activity. The message-sender UUID **is not automatically a physical serial number or the observer**, especially when a Node can be fusion/software/platform equipment. A supported Task is distinct from a requested, accepted, completed or failed Task. This is a bounded **interface control specification**, not a universal physical-system ontology. [BSI Flex 335 v2.0](https://www.bsigroup.com/siteassets/pdf/en/insights-and-media/insights/brochures/bsi-flex-335.pdf), §§3.1, 4.1–4.11 and 6.2–6.6.

**OMS 3.0 describes an Observation as an actual performed act.** An identifiable **Observer** performs an Observation using a reusable **ObservingProcedure** to determine the **ObservableProperty** of a **featureOfInterest**, returning a **result**. A **Host** can group observers (including at a monitoring facility), and **Deployment** assigns Observer to Host. OMS says **phenomenonTime** is when the result applies, including the interval for an average; **resultTime** is when the result became available. These are not a logger, probe, timestamp, procedure or observed environmental subject all bearing the same identity. [OGC 20-082r4 / OMS 3.0](https://docs.ogc.org/as/20-082r4/20-082r4.html), original §§8.2.2–8.2.11 and 8.4–8.8.

**W3C/OGC SOSA-SSN gives formal relationships for systems, sensors, observations and deployment.** The **19 October 2017 Recommendation** has SOSA/SSN classes and relations including Sensor, Observation, Procedure, System, Platform, Deployment, madeBySensor, usedProcedure, implements, hasSubSystem and isHostedBy. It explicitly permits sensor-like software and human observers in its scope. The newer **“2023 Edition” has a 26 October 2026 W3C Working Draft**, with proposed Asset/System/Deployment organization, a closer OMS RDF extension and revised term/namespace arrangements. **That draft is not a new Recommendation or a revision already binding CSAPI Part 1/Part 2.** [2017 Recommendation](https://www.w3.org/TR/2017/REC-vocab-ssn-20171019/), [W3C edition/status history](https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-ssn/all/), [dated 2026 draft](https://www.w3.org/TR/2026/WD-vocab-ssn-2023-20261026/).

**WoT separates a Thing from its description and a potentially different virtual/proxy identity.** **Thing Description 1.1** describes reusable **Property, Action and Event interaction affordances**, data schemas, HTTP/other protocol Forms, security metadata and links; **Thing Model** describes a template that may lack any fielded endpoint or instance serial. **WoT Architecture 1.1** describes proxy **Intermediaries** that may expose another Thing under a different digital ID, and **Digital Twins** that may cache values, queue commands or aggregate several Things. **A client receiving information does not thereby know which physical sensor produced it or whether a reported value is fresh.** [Thing Description 1.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2023/REC-wot-thing-description11-20231205/), §§1, 5–6 and 9; [WoT Architecture 1.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2023/REC-wot-architecture11-20231205/), §§5.7 and 9.2.2.

**Keep acronyms separate:** W3C/OGC **Sensor, Observation, Sample, and Actuator (SOSA)** and **Semantic Sensor Network (SSN)** are a **different lineage** from **The Open Group Sensor Open Systems Architecture (SOSA)** reviewed in [Step 12](#16-step-12--isa-60-and-the-open-group-sosa-physical-functional-visible-and-managed-relationships). No shared acronym establishes a formal equivalence or adoption.

Draft OMS/CSAPI alignments must not become issued API obligations. |
| **W3C WoT Architecture 1.1 and Thing Description 1.1**, W3C Recommendations **5 December 2023**. [Architecture](https://www.w3.org/TR/2023/REC-wot-architecture11-20231205/), [TD](https://www.w3.org/TR/2023/REC-wot-thing-description11-20231205/) | Original TD §§1, 5–6, 9 (Thing, Thing Model, properties/actions/events, forms, links, security) and Architecture §§5.7, 9.2.2 (Intermediary, proxy, twin/caching) inspected. | Distinct **architecture versus description/encoding standards**. Neither establishes that any of our four vendor cases is WoT-conformant, that a proxy's ID is a physical serial, or that cached data is current. |

### 18.3 Native findings — do not flatten the meanings into one DIM slot

The **SAP14, OMS14, SSN14 and WOT14** labels below are **local source-evidence keys only**, not vendor field IDs, published ontology mappings or new DIM classes.

| Evidence item and original locator | Source-native relationship or behavior | Explicit non-equivalence or open issue |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **SAP14-01 — BSI §§3.1.10, 4.1/Table 1, 6.2.4** | **Node UUIDv4 node_id** identifies message **sender**, independent of destination, the payload's separate record IDs, or underlying product serial. Multiple source-defined sensing, software, platform and fusion roles exist. | **Node UUID ≠ authenticated physical sensor UID ≠ manufacturer model ≠ reported detected object or observation.** A software/fusion Node cannot be forced into a physical chip class. |
| **SAP14-02 — BSI §§6.2.1, 6.2.5, 6.3.2** | **Registration** declares capabilities, supported modes, task/report definitions and configuration, including recursive **sub_components**; later StatusReport gives **current mode**, system state and **NEW/UNCHANGED** flags. | Supported mode ≠ active state; status definition ≠ report value; subcomponent description ≠ independently identified physical unit; SAPIENT **battery percentage** ≠ Dragino capacity or logger supply voltage. |
| **SAP14-03 — BSI §§6.5.7–.9/Tables 86–87** | Separate **Task** and **TaskAck** messages with **task_id**; outcomes **ACCEPTED, REJECTED, COMPLETED, FAILED**; an Error handles a malformed request differently. | **Available command ≠ request sent ≠ acceptance ≠ completed physical effect ≠ detection report**. Acknowledgement alone cannot prove the commanded change occurred. |
| **SAP14-04 — BSI Table 15 and pinned Dstl registration.proto** | **Directly reconfirmed mismatch:** BSI Table 15 visually specifies **config_data Protobuf field serial 9**; pinned Dstl **registration.proto assigns reporting_region to field 9 and config_data to field 10**. | **OPEN source discrepancy**, not a silently repaired wire binding, a presumed official erratum, or reason to discard the donor. Before any mapping, version/producer authority must be checked. |
| **SAP14-05 — BSI Table 1 Note 3 and pinned sapient_message.proto** | **Directly reconfirmed mismatch:** BSI outer **timestamp** is the **time of message content** (e.g. detection time), whereas Dstl's pinned Protobuf comment calls it **UTC time the message was sent**. | Content time ≠ transport/send time. Neither is automatically OMS phenomenonTime/resultTime. **Do not invent a resolution**. |
| **OMS14-01 — OMS §§8.2.2, .6–.10 (pp. 48–51)** | **Observation** is a performed act using an **ObservingProcedure** by an **Observer** to determine an **ObservableProperty** of a **featureOfInterest**, producing a **result**. | Environmental feature/medium ≠ instrument housing; a measured result ≠ physical probe, procedure description or hardware condition. |
| **OMS14-02 — OMS §§8.4–8.6 (pp. 54–57)** | **ObservingProcedure** is reusable steps/methodology; an **Observer is an identifiable individual**, possibly a physical instrument, implemented algorithm or human. **§8.5.2 Note 5** says ObservingProcedure cannot describe a **sensor instance** but can describe a **sensor type**. | A procedure/method ≠ the particular physical sensor that performed the act. Observer ≠ necessarily a single hardware chip; identical procedure across units does not establish one physical unit identity. |
| **OMS14-03 — OMS §§8.2.3–.5 (pp. 48–49)** | **phenomenonTime** is the time to which the result applies, and **for an average of several samples it is their interval**; **resultTime** is the instant the result became available; **validTime** indicates the period of useful applicability. | Bennett's fixed-EST station stamp plus prior-interval convention yield a **DERIVED nominal averaging interval**; actual result-availability time is **UNKNOWN**. An API surrogate is not original resultTime evidence. |
| **OMS14-04 — OMS §§8.6–8.8 (pp. 56–59)** | **Host** is a grouping of Observers, such as an environmental monitoring facility **or survey campaign**. **Deployment** records assignment of **Observer to Host**; the Observer may be physically **or organizationally** attached. | Host ≠ necessarily manufactured enclosure or mounted platform; existing station location does not prove each probe's independent geometry or exact swap instant. |
| **SSN14-01 — 2017 W3C Recommendation** | Sensor, Observation, usedProcedure, madeBySensor, **ssn:System**, hasSubSystem, hosted-on, Deployment and implements are distinct ontology constructs; source Sensor scope can include human/software participation. | Ontology link ≠ source-specific physical-serial join; subsystem relation ≠ a command/status relationship; W3C SOSA ≠ Open Group SOSA. |
| **SSN14-02 — 26 Oct 2026 Working Draft with explicit qualification** | The proposed revision expands **sosa:Asset/System/Deployment**, develops an **OMS RDF Extension**, reworks core **sosa:/ssn:** term placements, includes possible CSAPI alignments and a **non-normative System Capabilities appendix**. | **Working Draft is not 2017 Recommendation and not approved CSAPI v1.0 vocabulary**. Do not infer final class axioms or migration from an inaccessible full Oct 26 snapshot; keep edition-specific namespaces and adoption decisions open. |
| **WOT14-01 — WoT TD 1.1 §§1, 5–6, 9** | **Thing** can be physical or virtual; TD **Properties/Actions/Events** are *interaction affordances*, with data schemas, Forms, links and security; **Thing Model** is a reusable **interface template** without actual endpoint/serial/location. | TD id ≠ verified hardware ID; PropertyAffordance ≠ live measured state; ActionAffordance ≠ successfully executed action; Thing Model ≠ a fielded physical unit. |
| **WOT14-02 — WoT Architecture 1.1 §§5.7, 9.2.2** | **Intermediary proxy** may publish same interactions using **new digital identifier and communications metadata**; **Digital Twin** may aggregate devices, cache states, queue actions and simulate behavior. | Cached/twin value ≠ fresh direct reading from physical unit; proxied ID ≠ original observer serial; queued command ≠ completed act. The source of an assertion is an important separate question. |

**Do not turn these thirteen findings into thirteen mandatory classes.** Some are source-defined roles, some are transitions in a message lifecycle, some are temporal attributes, and some concern a description or its publisher. Their contribution is to **challenge what must remain distinguishable**.

### 18.4 Four-case falsification questions

| Existing reference/evidence scope | New SAPIENT / OMS / SSN / WoT challenge | Unsupported leap to refuse |
| --- | --- | --- |
