Cloud locationId and 0,0 are illustrative; firmwareVersion=null and example serial are not a real device. | D02 2024 sensor manual, D03 2019 program, D04 2021 package and D07 2026 MQTT Status are **not joined**. Placeholder loggerID and [0,0,0] geometry are not installed identity/location. | **Original:** acebpmet, distinct logger **13916**, TX321 **2335**, two actual serialized EE181 probes; two original temperature rows. **DERIVED:** nominal UTC averaging intervals and likely contributing probe. Four server records represent two original measurements. Native resultTime UNKNOWN. |
| **X23-Q: Data quality/diagnostics** | Specified sensor accuracy/range (**C-I08–11**), calibration command options (**C-C12–14**) and status/flag schemas. No verified calibration, live unit condition or full sample QA. No-data history code ≠ a zero measurement. | Raw electrode and gas index fields **A-F07/08, A-F13–17** plus September manufacturer calibration cautions. A09 offline Boolean is a *service example field*, not measured device-wide health. Raw electrodes not accepted as calibrated ambient concentrations. | Sensor watchdog/uptime **definitions D-V04/05** versus **unjoined logger diagnostics D-S01–S31**, 13 detailed definitions explicitly on hold. Logger PanelTemp is not air temperature; possible carried-forward wind value **D-C02** not fresh measurement. | **Original:** selected air temperature means F_ATemp **<0>** passed initial QA; rejected 17 April F_ATemp **<-3> [GPR] (CSM)** remains negative control. Per-serial last-calibration dates are metadata, not QA/health of all results or current condition. |
| **X23-M: Maintenance** | Possible OTA, battery and calibration management **C-O12/C-C12–14**; no date/technician, actual calibration or repair event. | Replaceable battery cells **A-P06** and calibration/test request switches **A-C06/08** are possibilities, not installed maintenance, performed calibration or successful firmware update. | Sensor verification/address functions and calibration-correction **definitions** exist; no installed maintenance event, executed query or confirmed version. | **Actual operator history:** two EE181 physical probes across three date-only service periods; broader 16 April service interval and separate 17 April logger OS update, with original last-calibration dates. Precise swap instant, later current use and live condition UNKNOWN. |

**Source integrity result:** the same subject-role separation survives all seven comparisons at the *available source level*. Design/default/report-schema, actual original historical event, and derived attribution cannot silently replace one another. This **does not** prove that a common executable CSAPI Profile can express and the client can interpret all seven domains; those are later gates.

### 27.3 Original donor evidence and its precise limits

**Source-native checks were limited to directly relevant passages**, including additional original power donors. The earlier V1 Power Source model is *historical research*, not authority to select power as V2's primary foundation. The 182 artifacts are not 182 independent standard votes.

| Original source and inspected area | What is genuinely useful for a whole-System interpretation | What does NOT follow |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **IETF [RFC 7326](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7326.html), EMAN Framework, September 2014, Informational, §§6.2 and 6.6; [RFC 7461](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7461.html), Energy Object Context MIB, March 2015, Standards Track; [RFC 7460](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7460.html), Monitoring and Control MIB for Power and Energy, March 2015, Standards Track** | Original framework distinguishes **Device, Component, Power Interface**, power-source versus metering/aggregation relationships, and energy management **context** versus monitored Power State/consumption. Power relationships are different from data-network topologies. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: power interface = data-network interface; wattage/nameplate capacity = measured current use; original EMAN classes/MIB rules = mandatory DIM classes or proof our cases implement SNMP. The informational 7326 does not inherit the 2015 MIB normative status. |
| **IETF [RFC 7577](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7577.html), Battery Monitoring MIB, July 2015, Standards Track, §4 batteryEntry** | Distinguishes **batteryDesignCapacity, batteryActualCapacity, batteryActualCharge, batteryActualVoltage**, charge states/cycles and identity; the capacity/charge concepts have their own units and source-specific unknown encodings. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: Dragino design 8,500 mAh = actual remaining charge; AirGradient three-series-cell arithmetic = known pack capacity; sample voltage = battery percent. IETF MIB requirements govern relevant MIB implementations, not generic CSAPI. |
| **OPC UA [Devices Part 100 v1.05.0 §5](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/5), [§6.3](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/6.3), [§8](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/8)** | **ConnectionPoint/Network/Protocol**, offline engineering versus online device representation; software/firmware/configuration updates with target, version, transfer/installation distinctions and §8.3.7 authorization/device/package authenticity. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: supported network = active session; update available or downloaded = installed and effective; OPC UA security/SoftwareUpdate SHALLs = our Profile requirements. None of the four cases was verified as OPC UA compliant. |
| **OPC UA [PA-DIM v1.02.0 §7.24](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/7.24)** | Optional component-relevant **InternalTemperature, ResidualLife, PowerOnDuration, OperationDuration and OperationCycleCounter**; original ResidualLife is device/component degradation, not necessarily battery state of charge. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: PA-DIM condition = NOAA observation QA, ambient temperature, battery remaining percent or whole-System health. |
| **OGC [SensorML 3.0, 23-000 §8.2.2](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html) and preface/security considerations** | **validTime, capabilities/characteristics, history, documentation and securityConstraints** for external-model *document markings*; separate supported/configured process parameters in §8.8. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: a marked document = an authenticated API session; description validTime = observation phenomenonTime or physical probe use dates; possible setting = observed effective state. |
| **W3C [WoT Thing Description 1.1 Recommendation, 5 December 2023, §6.3.4](https://www.w3.org/TR/2023/REC-wot-thing-description11-20231205/)** | Original TD **securityDefinitions/security** denote interaction-access mechanisms at Thing/form scope, distinct from document markings. The requiredness belongs to WoT TD conformance. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: example authentication scheme = installed unit policy, command permission or DIM-wide required field; SensorML security marking = WoT interaction security. |
| **OGC [OMS 3.0, 20-082r4 §§8.2.3–.4 and 9.2.5](https://docs.ogc.org/as/20-082r4/20-082r4.html)** ([preserved original PDF index](../Primary_Sources/OGC_20-082r4_OMS_3.0.0.md)) | Phenomenon time for an average applies to the sample interval; **resultTime** means when result became available; **resultQuality** belongs to a particular observation result. | REJECTED_EQUIVALENCE: source QA = physical probe diagnosis; station source timestamp = independently verified resultTime or software update time. |
| **Previously inspected IEEE 1451, ISA, The Open Group SOSA and SAPIENT v2 native evidence** ([§17](#17-step-13--ieee-1451-transducerchannel-evidence-and-sensorml-30-processsystem-semantics); [§16](#16-step-12--isa-60-and-the-open-group-sosa-physical-functional-visible-and-managed-relationships); [§18](#18-step-14--sapient-v2-oms-30-w3cogc-sosa-ssn-and-web-of-things-observation-action-time-and-representation)) | Public IEEE Playground TEDS timing/calibration/optional security descriptions; ISA mediated visibility; Open Group SOSA managed status versus physical/functional roles; SAPIENT capability, reported state, percent, Task and TaskAck. | Do not claim full IEEE 1451.0-2024 normative inspection, any of these as native interfaces to our four manufacturer cases, resolved SAPIENT field/time discrepancies or inherited class/cardinality requirements. |

**Three distinct security topics remain unresolved:** (1) SensorML *document/attribute security marking*, (2) WoT TD *interface interaction security declarations*, and (3) actual deployed *user/device/command authorization* such as those discussed for OPC UA software update. The vendor's ability to hold Wi-Fi credentials or use OTAA keys is a fourth, source-specific configuration question. **None of our four cases yields a verified installed security-policy packet.** We neither collected nor published keys/tokens.

### 27.4 Four-case falsification and negative controls

These are **source-to-candidate analytical checks**, not executable source→CSAPI→client runtime tests. Each check names the invalid equivalence to reject.

| ID | Honest source interpretation | Rejected shortcut |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T23-01 | C-I04 8,500 mAh is a battery design rating; C-S05 describes potential voltage at a node. | Battery rating = current remaining charge or a measured percent. |
| T23-02 | A-P01–07 power pathways and A-C10–12 distinct transmission paths have different subjects; 0.7.0+ source modes are edition-conditional. | A power connection = a network route; cellular cache = Wi-Fi cache; design mode = current device state. |
| T23-03 | D-S03, D-S04, D-S22–31 are logger-local supply/port-status fields within one publisher example. | All are ClimaVue voltages or whole-station battery health; zero errors means fault-free lifetime. |
| T23-04 | 17 April Bennett logger OS update concerns CR1000X 13916; probe history is a different source relationship. | Software event = another probe swap, exact Deployment time or completed CSAPI command. |
| T23-05 | Dragino OTAA registration and AirGradient portal/credential options are vendor design evidence, not authenticated sessions. | Supported credential field = a known secret, verified access rule or permission to execute a command. |
| T23-06 | Bennett original F_ATemp QA token qualifies a temperature summary; per-serial calibration dates and rejected 17 April record have other roles. | QA pass = verified physical EE181 health; rejected value becomes third accepted mean. |
| T23-07 | A01/A02 source revisions and A09 example versions/placeholder identifiers are separate artifacts. | Pinned firmware commit = unit-installed running release or a cloud example locationId = physical serial. |
| T23-08 | Two EE181 physical units served three source date-scoped periods; broader station maintenance was 09:45–13:30 EST on 16 April. | Three periods = three probes; date-only use = exact timestamp; station downtime clock = individual swap minute. |
| T23-09 | RFC energy topology, OPC UA SoftwareUpdate, PA-DIM condition and WoT TD security carry *different* native donor requirements. | Every donor class, numeric field, optional feature or SHALL automatically becomes our DIM requirement. |

**Outcome:** the Step 23 candidate qualifiers are **source-justified for the types of assertions we possess**. They have not yet been assembled and tested as one coherent DIM; that is precisely the Step 24 responsibility.

### 27.5 Coverage decisions, remaining gaps and release consequences

| Question | Source-grounded decision | Explicit gap / treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Power and communications scope | **Documentary coverage is useful** if power source/consumer and interface/relay/publisher are distinct, with exact referents and original source scope. | **No complete live power/network graph.** A/C/D are reference arrangements; B has historical station communication context without current operating metrics. Defer binding to Phase E. No adopted power ontology. |
| Software and firmware | **Can distinguish design version, installed software claim, update event and source provenance**; B has one actual logger OS update. | **Incomplete installed unit/program lineage** across all four cases. A broad firmware-management interoperability claim would be unproven. |
| Security and command access | Original document markings, WoT interaction security and OPC UA authorization are different mechanisms. | **Material conditional release blocker for any future Profile claiming action authorization:** no authenticated installed security/permissions record. Later specify, acquire real authority or exclude those claims; do not silently invent policy. |
| Provenance, QA, maintenance | **Bounded coverage is supported** for source-authored design/examples versus historic observations and dated operator events; original/derived/unknown labels have actual cases. | No comprehensive calibration/health history; no actual resultTime for Bennett; time/identity and stream ownership gaps stay visible. |
| Connected DIM/Profile/client | Candidate DIM Step 23 records narrow cross-cutting qualifiers; workbook contains original comparisons and negative checks. | **Not yet integrated or runtime tested.** Step 24 must join and falsify them; Steps 25–31 address original OGC bindings; Steps 32–39 implement and test the same interpreter. |

**Important release distinction:** a source-qualified *reference* model may legitimately expose that a product supports a battery type or LoRaWAN interface while showing **UNKNOWN** installed voltage, credentials or deployment. That is useful model fidelity, not failed data parsing. However, an application claiming to execute authenticated commands, infer live battery health or map individual physical deployed nodes **needs the evidence that supports those stronger claims**. The absence of such data constrains the release scope rather than authorizing made-up values.

### 27.6 Completion and next step

**Step 23 completed:** examined the original three manufacturer/system-sheet inventories and established Bennett operator history; inspected original IETF RFC 7326, 7460, 7461, 7577, OPC UA Devices v1.05.0, PA-DIM v1.02.0, OGC SensorML 3.0, W3C WoT TD 1.1 and original OMS 3.0 passages at the bounded scope stated above; reused documented IEEE/ISA/SOSA/SAPIENT evidence. Recorded **seven-domain, four-case comparison**, nine falsification/negative controls, gaps/release implications and [one candidate DIM Step 23 qualification section](../Candidate_Specification/DIM.md#step-23--cross-cutting-whole-system-services-assertions-and-evidence-limitations). No original Bennett records, firmware, CSAPI Profile, client code or API were changed.

**Step 24 NEXT, ONLY on the next owner “proceed”:** consolidate **Steps 16–23** into **one readable, connected candidate DIM and relationship diagram**, identify each major relation's original case/donor pedigree and any genuine alternatives, and **attempt falsification across all four differently evidenced arrangements**. If something does not fit, repair or explicitly exclude it instead of redrawing the source or declaring a success. **Gate D remains open until the integrated test**, and no Profile commitments or app work belong to Step 23.


## 28. Step 24 — Integrated whole-System DIM falsification and Gate D

**Completed 2 November 2026. Gate D: REACHED WITH QUALIFIED DOCUMENTARY SCOPE; Step 25 NEXT (NOT STARTED).** The actual candidate architecture is in [Candidate DIM Step 24 — one connected relationship map and R24 register](../Candidate_Specification/DIM.md#step-24--integrated-source-bounded-whole-system-dim-and-relationship-falsification). This workbook section is the bounded **integrated model / diagram / original-source falsification record** required by the [43-step plan](../PLAN_DIM_Profile_V2_Whole_System_Recovery_to_Delivery_v0.1_31OCT2026.md), not an executable CSAPI mapping, vendor conformance report or Glaux client test. It **reuses** the original published evidence recorded in [Dragino §9](#9-step-05--dragino-s31-lb-source-native-reference-system), [AirGradient §10](#10-step-06--airgradient-open-air-max-source-native-reference-system), [Campbell §11](#11-step-07--campbell-cr1000x-and-first-generation-climavue-50-source-native-arrangement), [Bennett §12](#12-step-08--bennetts-point-actual-station-time-scoped-probes-and-historical-temperature-reports), [targeted original donor checks §§15–18](#15-step-11--original-aas-opc-ua-devices-and-pa-dim-donor-native-whole-system-questions) and [cross-cutting Step 23 §27](#27-step-23--cross-cutting-whole-system-coverage-and-evidence-gaps). **No original source, frozen packet, server representation or application implementation was modified.**

### 28.1 What the integrated model now explains — and why this is more than eight headings

The integrated [DIM Step 24](../Candidate_Specification/DIM.md#step-24--integrated-source-bounded-whole-system-dim-and-relationship-falsification) answers a practical question: **If I look at a sensing arrangement, which participants do I have evidence for, what does each do, which information or events are theirs, and how confident am I about the relationships?** It begins with a **declared focal subject at a known source standing**. That focus can be a manufacturer reference design, an actual identified individual device, or a source-identified continuing station. A reference pairing is **not** silently converted to a new installed System.

It connects participants through separately typed, source-qualified claims: physical-constituent versus externally supplied/separately connected versus Host/manager relationship; equipment/software realizing sensing, processing, publishing and management; distinct native interfaces and output forms; declared observing/control capability versus performed historical activity; original report field and environmental characteristic versus equipment diagnostic; supported setting versus as-applied state; request/acknowledgement versus independently evidenced physical effect; and an individual unit serving a role at a Host during its **documented date precision**. A separate source assertion/representation layer prevents a derived source join, digital proxy, manufacturer example or duplicate API record becoming new real equipment or new observations.

The [Mermaid relationship diagram](../Candidate_Specification/DIM.md#integrated-relationship-diagram--analytical-propositions-not-software-classes) shows **one connected conditional topology**. Its R24-01–R24-15 register makes the directional meaning of every compact edge checkable. The diagram's dotted arrows **do not** imply every focal subject has those links; source support is the entry condition. The diagram and register both **refuse** universal manufactured-part containment, a mandatory Host, required real observations for product references, preselected DataStream owner, fixed cardinalities, or one universal energy/security package. Donor/native standard structures remain potential carriers, **not required subclasses or ontology votes**.

### 28.2 Explicit falsification method and decision thresholds

Each test below starts with a **concrete possible failure**, not the desire to give a PASS. We compare the unified R24 relation propositions against original evidence **at that source's actual standing**, trying to force an impossible equivalence. A **documentary conceptual FIT** means this source can be described **without the incorrect inference**. It does **not** demonstrate an executable transformation, native standards conformance, a genuine joined fielded dataset, interoperability, or a client display.

The bounded Gate D criteria are: (a) a justified focus and evidence level for **each** case; (b) non-interchangeable physical, functional, interface, output and report/observer relations; (c) preserved actual-versus-possible/derived distinctions, including source time precision and original count of measurements; (d) a meaningful **connected** relationship representation, not only eight separate lists; (e) explicit original donor pedigree and alternative boundaries; and (f) contradictions **recorded and repaired/excluded/open** instead of fabricating data. A material contradiction which requires creating an actual individual from a reference manual, converting fields into physical units, or inventing source times would **fail this gate** until revised.

### 28.3 Four-case adversarial reconstructions, not four equivalent field installations

**Dragino S31-LB — documentary reference, not authenticated live hardware.** Declare one **manufacturer reference arrangement** for the radio/battery node and one *externally cabled, supplied* SHT31 probe, at [C source sheet §§1–5](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md). The SHT31 realizes **two environmental sensing functions**; the node implements the radio/reporting role, with **LoRaWAN FPort 2 sampling / FPort 3 datalog / FPort 5 status** distinct output forms and a documented primary-battery design. A FPort number is **not** an independently addressed IEEE 1451 transducer channel; battery voltage is not ambient temperature or an authenticated remaining-charge measurement. The typed **supplied-with/external connection**, **realizes-function**, **output-field** and **design capability** relations provide a faithful description without inventing a physical serial, installed Probe Deployment, a real gateway session, an actual Observation from the illustrative packets or performed calibration command. **Result: FIT for source-supported reference arrangement; actual-device semantics UNTESTED.**

**AirGradient Open Air Max — documentary multi-sensor assembled reference, not a deployed monitor history.** Declare the [A documented O-M-1PPSTON-CE gas-equipped reference](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md), with **two distinct PMS5003 physical sensors on PM1/PM2 hardware sockets** and additional CO2, SHT4x, SGP41, gas/ADC and connectivity modules. The **physical connector PM1 is not the reported PM1.0 property**, and PM2 is not PM2.5; output quantities/fields and physical device counts are separate model questions. Pinned hardware **89aacf5**, firmware **84e479a**, firmware-0.7.0+ operating-mode guidance, cellular/Wi-Fi examples and separate manufacturer cloud outputs **are not a verified single installed hardware/software/telemetry packet**. Raw gas-electrode fields and publisher's gas-calibration warning must remain native/caveated rather than certified pollutant values. The typed **constituent**, **interface**, **function**, **property**, **source version** and **quality** claims can all hold without converting a cloud location ID or [0,0] coordinates into an actual station. **Result: FIT at design/reference scope; installed version, calibration and operating condition UNTESTED.**

**Campbell CR1000X + first-generation ClimaVue 50 — documentary two-product pairing, not an attested installed station.** Declare a reference **arrangement** with a distinct weather-sensing ClimaVue and a separately programmable CR1000X logger; source wiring specifies sensor power, returns and an **SDI-12 data bus**, plus a **factory-default** sensor address 0 (not the installed address). The ClimaVue **aR7! response has fourteen ordered value positions** without fourteen physical units or fourteen independently bus-addressed transducers. Logger **PanelTemp** describes logger panel temperature, **not ambient air**. The **2019 program text, 2021 downloadable example reference, 2024 sensor manual and 2026 MQTT logger Status example** are independently published, **not one actual installation/operating history**. The R24 model preserves physical versus reporting functions, source-defined field positions, processing possibilities and logger-local power/status examples without joining incompatible publication contexts or inventing a real site. **Result: FIT for reference two-device connection/functional interpretation; installed program, device identities and live result quality UNTESTED.**

**Bennett's Point — actual historical persistent station with changing equipment.** Declare actual station **acebpmet**, separate CR1000X logger serial **13916**, GOES transmitter TX321 serial **2335** and two individual EE181 probes, under the original [2024 ACE Basin operator meteorological metadata](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf), [source-evidence snapshot](../Candidate_Specification/examples/bennetts-point-ee181.json) and [dated service §26](#26-step-22--dated-equipment-use-and-persistent-station-identity). Individual EE181 **21281600077814** served **4 May 2022–8 June 2023**, returned **16 April 2024** and was reported still in use **as of 31 December 2024**; EE181 **222016000109CE** served **8 June 2023–16 April 2024**. These are **two physical individuals, three dated service statements, one continuing station**, not a new station on each replacement or a third physical probe. The original use boundaries have **day precision**; the wider **16 April 09:45–13:30 EST** shutdown/maintenance does *not* provide exact serialized-probe removal/installation clock times. The separate **17 April logger OS update** concerns the CR1000X, not another EE181 swap. Station coordinates are **not an independently measured surveyed geometry for either probe**.

The original NOAA/CDMO archive supplies **two selected real 15-minute mean air-temperature reports**: **15 April 2024 12:00 fixed EST = 29.1 °C**, and **18 April 2024 12:00 fixed EST = 27.3 °C**; original parameter QA token **F_ATemp = "<0> "** is preserved at source token fidelity. The project's nominal **16:45–17:00 UTC** averaging intervals and likely contributor-probe serial assignments are **DERIVED from separately evidenced source conventions/service history**; the original individual rows have no serial or independently known **resultTime**. The **17 April rejected source row** remains a negative control, not a third accepted air-temperature measurement. Our earlier running server has **four Observation representations of these two source measurements**, not four source acts. At conceptual level the model keeps **reported result, source quality, processing/relay roles and derived unit attribution distinct**; it does **not** yet identify with certainty an OMS Observer for the final logger-aggregated report, claim full OMS resultTime completeness, settle CSAPI DataStream producer binding or emit a machine-readable Deployment. **Result: FIT for persistent station, two serialized probes, dated service and qualified source-result preservation; exact OMS/CSAPI bindings and complete runtime fidelity UNRESOLVED.**

### 28.4 Integrated negative-control results and model repairs/limitations

| Test | Attempt to falsify the unified model | Outcome under original evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **F24-01 Focus/standing** | Require an individual manufacturer serial and Host for Dragino, AirGradient or Campbell because one focal System is required. | **REJECTED:** design/reference focus is allowed and explicitly differentiated from authenticated individual. No fabricated hardware (R24-01/02). |
| **F24-02 Composition vs connection** | Force Dragino external SHT31 and Campbell separate ClimaVue into internal physical constituent tree; infer every exposed service is a part. | **REJECTED:** sourced **supplied-with, separately-connected and host/functional** relations differ from contained constituent (R24-03/04/05). |
| **F24-03 Module count vs property** | Split Dragino two reported temperature/humidity values into two SHT31 probes, or AirGradient PM1/PM2 sockets into PM1.0/PM2.5 environmental properties. | **REJECTED:** equipment count, interface/connector address and reported-property field occupy different referents (R24-06/07/08). |
| **F24-04 Response positions vs units** | Turn Campbell fourteen SDI-12 aR7! positions into fourteen independent sensors or physical addresses. | **REJECTED:** fields in **one** source-defined response; bus address is distinct, and **default 0** is not real installed readback (R24-07/08/10). |
| **F24-05 Observer vs relay and environmental subject** | Use Campbell PanelTemp as ambient weather, or equate Bennett EE181 serial, logger, GOES sender and CDMO publisher as the same source Observer for a completed 15-minute mean. | **REJECTED:** field/observed property/subject and sensing, processing, reporting roles stay separate. **OPEN:** exact OMS Observer and final producer assignment for the Bennett aggregate (R24-08/09). |
| **F24-06 Product examples as actual activities** | Treat Dragino history-payload example, AirGradient device/cloud illustrative values and Campbell logger Status publisher example as three authenticated live Observation series. | **REJECTED:** source examples may describe report forms but **do not prove actual field acts, installed versions or matched unit identity** (R24-01/08/09/10). |
| **F24-07 Current state, commands and security** | Declare AirGradient power mode selected, Campbell sample firmware running, SAPIENT/Dragino command completion or remote authorization from manuals, field definitions or malformed ACK templates. | **REJECTED:** defaults, desired values, actual reported effective state, request/acknowledgement/effect and real authorization are distinct; installed facts UNKNOWN (R24-10/11/14). |
| **F24-08 One station vs repeated devices** | Make Bennett's returning EE181 serial 21281600077814 into a second instrument or create a new station on 16 April 2024. | **REJECTED:** source-identity scopes plus **three separate use-period assignments** support one physical returning individual and one continuing station (R24-01/12). |
| **F24-09 Time / location precision** | Convert 16 April date-only EE181 transition into precise 09:45 or 13:30 EST swap or independent coordinates for each physical probe; turn 31 December 2024 as-of into a removal date. | **REJECTED:** maintenance window, use dates, document as-of and site geometry qualify different subjects; exact individual clocks/locations UNKNOWN (R24-05/12/13/14). |
| **F24-10 Duplicate source events / QA** | Count four server Observation resources as four original NOAA measurements; turn F_ATemp pass into whole-equipment health or assign unknown original resultTime from a source stamp. | **REJECTED:** two original source means, duplicate representations and DERIVED window/attribution remain distinct. **OPEN:** exact Part 2/OMS time/QA encoding, real resultTime and stream ownership (R24-09/14/15). |
| **F24-11 Whole-System cross-cutting completeness** | Require all four arrangements to have common live charge, network session, firmware version, security policy, maintenance history and health fields. | **REJECTED:** such clauses would manufacture unavailable evidence. Step 23 provides qualified, *conditional* support claims; selected domains remain UNKNOWN or excluded from stronger release claims (R24-14). |
| **F24-12 Diagram-to-text alignment** | Read the graph as required classes, a permanent containment hierarchy or a linear guaranteed output→Observation→successful command chain. | **REJECTED BY CAPTION + REGISTER:** dotted arrows are conditional source-attested propositions, kind/proxy IDs are separate, actual acts require evidence; cross-cutting conditions attach to real subjects. **LIMIT:** not formal UML/OWL validation or executable consistency test. |

**Result of attempted falsification:** the candidate interpretation **survived the bounded documentary contradictions**, because it restricts every relation by source and treats what is not established as unknown, while retaining the real structural differences among all four. It **did not pass** any previously missing normative field binding, runtime conformance, physical deployment test or comprehensive 182-donor assessment. The diagram and fifteen R24 relation propositions are mutually consistent at their intended conceptual scope, subject to later formal/editorial review.

### 28.5 The actual donor pedigree, not an invented standards merger

The integrated text is **our synthesis**, not a source-authored cross-standard supermodel. Its relevant original donor roles are different:

- **OGC Sensor Model Language 3.0 / OGC 23-000**, approved [§§7–8, particularly §8.6 and §8.8–8.9](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html): physical/aggregate processes, components/connections, typeOf/configuration and specific Deployment mechanisms. **SensorML's exact requirements are not this DIM's adopted base classes**; not every reference pairing is a populated SensorML PhysicalSystem.
- **OGC Observations, Measurements and Samples 3.0 / OGC 20-082r4**, original [§§8.2, 8.4–8.8 and §9.2.5](https://docs.ogc.org/as/20-082r4/20-082r4.html) (complete [source preservation index](../Primary_Sources/OGC_20-082r4_OMS_3.0.0.md)): particular act/Observer/Procedure/Host/Deployment versus time/quality. It **does not prove** a unique source-stated Bennett aggregate Observer, resultTime or actual CSAPI Deployment.
- **AAS Part 1 v3.2; OPC UA Devices Part 100 v1.05.0; PA-DIM OPC 30081 v1.02.0**, original targeted [review §15](#15-step-11--original-aas-opc-ua-devices-and-pa-dim-donor-native-whole-system-questions): asset type/instance versus Shell/representation; device ComponentType versus product Model; **nonhierarchical ConnectsTo**, online/offline configuration and optional equipment health/signal views. Their native class requirements are not DIM cardinalities.
- **ISA 6.0 and The Open Group SOSA C239 Ed. 1.1 / DSDM Ed. 1.0**, original [review §16](#16-step-12--isa-60-and-the-open-group-sosa-physical-functional-visible-and-managed-relationships): physical vs functional/mediated-visible members, Sensor versus Host platform, component/module/manager and direct/indirect management distinctions. **Do not confuse this defense SOSA with W3C SOSA/SSN**, or transfer its originally scoped definitions to every environmental product.
- **IEEE 1451.0-2024 public technical evidence and approved SensorML review**, original [§17](#17-step-13--ieee-1451-transducerchannel-evidence-and-sensorml-30-processsystem-semantics): module/channel Transducer Electronic Data Sheet descriptors, timing/calibration information and address scope, **without complete access to 2024 normative mandatory rules**. It informs the difference among a bus address, an addressed transducer channel and an output field; it does not force manufacturer units into a 1451 module hierarchy.
- **BSI SAPIENT v2, OMS, 2017 SSN Recommendation, separately 2026 SSN Working Draft, W3C WoT TD/Architecture 1.1**, original [review §18](#18-step-14--sapient-v2-oms-30-w3cogc-sosa-ssn-and-web-of-things-observation-action-time-and-representation): task/reply/status, Sensor/Observation and platform/Host vocabulary, proxy/description versus real asset; retain pinned **SAPIENT field 9/10 and wrapper time discrepancies**, draft normative-status limits and non-equivalence of sender UUID to a physical unit.
- **IETF Energy Management (EMAN) RFCs 7326, 7460, 7461, 7577**, reviewed [Step 23 §27](#27-step-23--cross-cutting-whole-system-coverage-and-evidence-gaps): power interfaces/supply/context, monitored power/energy and distinct battery design/actual capacity/charge. These enrich **conditional source-qualified power relations** rather than reopening the V1 power-first architecture.

The donor corpus contains **182 recorded artifacts**, not 182 independent standards or a completed census. Earlier/late sources may still change a relation, and **there is no claim a standards organization approved the working D structure**. Original source/edition/clause claims in the linked reviews retain precedence over this summary.

### 28.6 Gate D decision and exact unfinished work

**Decision: GATE D REACHED WITH DOCUMENTARY AND CONCEPTUAL LIMITATIONS.** This checkpoint means a human can follow one coherent source-bounded candidate representation across a one-external-probe node, one complex multi-module monitor, one separate logger/sensor pairing and one continuing station with a real probe replacement, **without forcing a source contradiction**. A passing *analytical fit* is not an executable interoperability PASS. R24-01–R24-15 and the relationship diagram are **candidate meanings only**, open to revision when additional actual data or standards-binding checks reveal a genuine mismatch.

**Unresolved alternatives deliberately remain visible:**
1. **Physical relation names and cardinality:** inclusion of an external supplied probe, attached logger, sensor-hosted role and digital representation may have source-specific boundaries; no one parent/part rule or universal class cardinality adopted.
2. **Bennett reporting/observing:** station-led versus probe-led CSAPI DataStream, particular OMS Observer for the logger-aggregated 15-minute result, actual publisher identity and source-native resultTime require later original CSAPI/OMS constraints. **Missing required observation data could prevent full binding**, even though historic source meaning is preserved.
3. **Source time/QA encoding:** date-only use and as-of records versus API instants, actual interval-valued phenomenon time, result quality, duplicate archival-report representation and true resultTime are **unresolved binding/runtime questions**, not evidence to manufacture new time values.
4. **Actual installed/operating data:** Dragino/AirGradient/Campbell reference designs do not establish fielded individual status, authenticated security permissions, actual control effectiveness, running program/firmware history, current system condition or maintenance.
5. **Normative/ontology proof:** no full 182-artifact review, comprehensive original IEEE normative access, formal ontology/UML constraints, universal data conversion, same-client interpretation or completed Glaux runtime testing yet.

**Gate D is NOT:** adopted publication-grade DIM, formally verified semantics or donor consensus, an existing OGC Profile requirement, a standards-conformant Bennett Observation/Deployment graph, actual source-to-CSAPI fidelity success, or a license to bypass the original evidence gaps. If Phase E reveals an impossible binding or if Phase F finds source loss, **revise the candidate or record a release blocker**; do not keep calling a contradicted approach D a success merely because Gate D was reached.

**Step 24 deliverables complete:** [one connected DIM section and Mermaid graph](../Candidate_Specification/DIM.md#step-24--integrated-source-bounded-whole-system-dim-and-relationship-falsification), fifteen source-qualified R24 relationship propositions, four case reconstructions, twelve deliberate negative controls and this **bounded, conditional Gate D result**. Original candidate DIM Steps 16–23 and their source evidence remain preserved. No CSAPI Profile amendment, issued command, new source dataset, Glaux application implementation, production Oracle/52°North operation, Cloudflare deploy or runtime validation was performed in this step.

**STEP 25 NEXT, only on the owner's next “proceed”:** begin Phase E by checking the **actual OGC API – Connected Systems Parts 1 and 2, SensorML 3.0, SWE Common 3.0 and OGC API – Features** clauses, constraints, media types and allowed bindings against this qualified DIM. Prepare a **binding register** clearly separating existing normative behavior, future Profile choices, implementation-dependent features and presently unsupported or unsafe claims. **Do not preempt Steps 26–31, change the Profile with unsourced SHALLs, implement client logic or write/deploy server data in Step 25.**


## 29. Step 25 — Original CSAPI and OGC standard requirements; evidence-qualified Profile binding inventory

**Status: STEP 25 COMPLETE, 2 November 2026; STEP 26 NEXT; Phase E / Gate E OPEN.** This is the first **standards/encoding inventory** for the [integrated source-qualified DIM Step 24](../Candidate_Specification/DIM.md#step-24--integrated-source-bounded-whole-system-dim-and-relationship-falsification) and its [four-case Gate D analysis (§28)](#28-step-24--integrated-whole-system-dim-falsification-and-gate-d). It does **not adopt one particular OGC resource carrier for each R24 relationship**, prescribe new Profile SHALL statements, implement/deploy anything, amend the [earlier candidate Profile](../Candidate_Specification/CSAPI_Profile.md), or claim any live server’s comprehensive conformance. [PITON v2.4](../PITON_DIM_Profile_V2_Continuity_v2.4_02NOV2026.md) is the most recent pre-Phase-E handoff; the plan/README track progress.

### 29.1 Plain language — the OGC standards provide mechanisms, not missing observations or installations

The integrated DIM describes **what is meant**: a genuine individual or source-described design, separate physical equipment and functions, native interfaces, observations, state/control and time-qualified service. A Connected Systems API (CSAPI) Profile would need to explain **which standard structures a producer must use so an unknown compatible client can recover those meanings**. A standard’s native resource or field is useful **only if the actual source establishes its referent**. The mere existence of an endpoint, property, schema or example cannot invent a deployed S31-LB, installed AirGradient firmware, Campbell station, Bennett probe timestamp or source original `resultTime`.

**Three different authorities must stay visible in every proposed binding:** **STANDARD** = an actual requirement of a named published OGC requirements class/encoding when the implementation claims that class; **PROFILE-CANDIDATE** = a project choice still requiring reason, agreed field semantics and test in Steps 26–31; **IMPLEMENTATION/EVIDENCE** = what our particular 52°North-backed server and primary case sources can actually support. Some standards allow a construct that the chosen server does not correctly support; conversely, a custom project JSON field can preserve information without becoming a normative Observation property. **No new universal class hierarchy, one-to-one donor crosswalk or field obligation is derived from a compatible name.**

### 29.2 Original source edition and scope register

| Source / original approved text | Scope examined for this step | Important authority limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **OGC API – Connected Systems Part 1, OGC 23-001 v1.0**, published **16 July 2025** — [original](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | §§8–13, 19, especially **§8 API common/identity**, **§9 System**, **§10 Subsystem**, **§11 Deployment**, **§13 Procedure**, and **§19.1 GeoJSON / §19.2 SensorML JSON**. | Requirements classes apply when claimed; a single `System` feature or GeoJSON payload does **not** prove every Part 1 class is implemented. SensorML media encodings are *separate conformance/representation obligations*. |
| **OGC API – Connected Systems Part 2, OGC 23-002 v1.0**, published **16 July 2025** — [original](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-002/23-002.html) | §§8–13 and 16: **`DataStream` / `Observation`, `ControlStream` / `Command` / `CommandStatus`, `SystemEvent`, schema query, `application/json` and `application/swe+json`** plus linked abstract tests. | Part 2 DataStream/ControlStream and format requirements classes are distinct; `application/json` and `application/swe+json` are not interchangeable. A publisher-provided example is not a native claim that these are implemented. |
| **OGC Sensor Model Language (SensorML) Encoding Standard, OGC 23-000 v3.0**, published **16 July 2025** — [original](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html) | §§7–8: process descriptions, `typeOf` general-to-specific, composition/connections, inputs/outputs and settings, §8.9 **Deployment/DeployedSystem**. | SensorML can describe a specified process/system/deployment; cannot fabricate physical-unit identity, station geometry, actual installed configuration or service dates. Its own requirements are **not automatically DIM requirements**. |
| **OGC SWE Common Data Model Encoding Standard, OGC 24-014 v3.0.0**, published **16 July 2025** — [original](https://docs.ogc.org/is/24-014/24-014.html) | §§8.2, 9 and **§10.2 JSON**: `Quantity`, `Text`, `Time`, `TimeRange`, `DataRecord` and declared JSON encoding. | `TimeRange` and original-quality `Text` may carry context in a correctly described record; they do **not** thereby become standard Observation `phenomenonTime` or `resultQuality` mappings. |
| **OGC API – Features Part 1 Core corrigendum, OGC 17-069r4 v1.0.1**, published **11 May 2022** — [original](https://docs.ogc.org/is/17-069r4/17-069r4.html), and **OGC API – Common Part 1 Core, OGC 19-072 v1.0.0** — [original](https://docs.ogc.org/is/19-072/19-072.html) | Feature collection, resources, landing/discovery and `conformsTo` via `GET /conformance`, API definition, standard filters where applicable. | Features `core` does not make every format or CRUD operation mandatory; a conformance declaration or one successful GET must be evaluated **per requirements class and actual implementation behavior**. |
| **OGC Observations, Measurements and Samples (OMS) 3.0, OGC 20-082r4** — [original approved text](https://docs.ogc.org/as/20-082r4/20-082r4.html), [owner-preserved original PDF index](../Primary_Sources/OGC_20-082r4_OMS_3.0.0.md) | Conceptual Observation, Observer, Procedure, Host/Deployment, **§8.2.3 phenomenon time**, **§8.2.4 result availability time** and §9.2.5 result quality; corroborates Step 19's original source review. | **An underlying conceptual meaning does not guarantee that a particular Part 2 JSON encoding carries all OMS semantics**. Source time not supplied by Bennett cannot be reconstructed from normative cardinality alone. |

**Schema/version verification:** Directly inspected the published-source companion [Part 2 JSON `observation.json`](https://github.com/opengeospatial/ogcapi-connected-systems/blob/master/api/part2/openapi/schemas/json/observation.json) and [`observationSchemaJson.json`](https://github.com/opengeospatial/ogcapi-connected-systems/blob/master/api/part2/openapi/schemas/json/observationSchemaJson.json) at the repository's accessible `master` branch. The Observation schema uses **`TimeInstant`** for both top-level times, requires `resultTime`, and documents `phenomenonTime` as defaulting to resultTime when omitted; the schema resource uses `obsFormat: application/json`, a SWE `resultSchema` or result link and optional `parametersSchema`. **These are a live, mutable companion repository retrieval, not a verified immutable hash of the exact 2025 issued schema on schemas.opengis.net.** In the Part 2 text, **§9.7 Table 6 labels both times required**, while the inspected `observation.json` does not list `phenomenonTime` in its JSON `required` array and gives the above default. **Flag the apparent conceptual-table / representation-specific distinction for later authoritative schema/requirements reconciliation**, not a license to declare an erratum or silently omit real phenomenon time. Do not derive any historical Bennett `resultTime` from that default.

### 29.3 Actual requirements-class and media-type boundaries

| Native carrier / mechanism | Original classes or requirements (when an API claims them) | Media/structure and interpretation ceiling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| API discovery / resource browsing | Part 1 **§8 /req/api-common** draws on Features/Common collection, landing and conformance requirements; Part 2 **§8 /req/api-common** adapts non-feature resources. | Check `GET /conformance` and actual collection/resource behavior. Presence of resources or a service-wide `application/json` response is **not** proof of a particular Part 2 encoding class or transactional capability. |
| Individual equipment/System and product Procedure | Part 1 **§9 /req/system**, **§13 /req/procedure**, identity distinctions §§8.4–8.5. | `System` and `Procedure` have independent identities and type semantics. In Part 1 §13.2.3, **Procedure is nonspatial**; a model datasheet is not a surveyed individual location. Model↔instance choice remains Step 26. |
| Subsystems and deployment | Part 1 **§10 /req/subsystem**, **§11 /req/deployment**, including deployment `validTime` and `deployedSystems`. | A physical subsystem, externally connected equipment, Host and **time-scoped deployment** are not the same relation. A resource may be encoded as GeoJSON or SensorML in their respective classes, **not automatically both**. |
| Part 1 GeoJSON and SensorML encodings | Part 1 **§19.1 /req/geojson** requirements 77–88; **§19.2 /req/sensorml-json** requirements 89–103. | Native media **`application/geo+json`** and **`application/sml+json`** have different property mappings, with separate expected schemas and conformance tests. A GeoJSON `properties.uid` is not interchangeable with the service's local `id` or a sensor's original serial. |
| DataStreams / observations and schema | Part 2 **§9 /req/datastream** requirements 3–16, especially **Requirement 11 `schema-op`**. | `DataStream.system` targets **one producing `System`**; all member Observations must share property set/result schema; `GET /datastreams/{id}/schema?obsFormat=...` must return an appropriate format-specific schema for supported format. A source has to establish the producing role; the standard does not choose a Bennett probe vs station/logger for us. |
| Ordinary JSON observation and control encoding | Part 2 **§16.1 /req/json**, requirements **93–106**, incl. `observation-schema`, `observation-constraints`, `commandstatus-schema`, `systemevent-schema`. | `application/json` Observation: parent stream's SWE `resultSchema` describes `result`, optional `parametersSchema` qualifies `parameters`; original JSON Observation schema top-level times are **`TimeInstant`**. The same media type **does not** imply SWE Common JSON format conformance. |
| SWE Common JSON observation/command encoding | Part 2 **§16.2 /req/swecommon-json**, requirements **107–114**, notably **109–111**. | Media **`application/swe+json`**: Observation Schema must provide **`recordSchema` + `JSONEncoding`**; Req110 calls for **at least one SWE `Time`** with designated `phenomenonTime` or `resultTime` definition URI; actual observation must match declared format. **A separate `TimeRange` field has not been proven to map to normative phenomenonTime**. |
| Other observation encodings | Part 2 **§§16.3–16.4** SWE text/csv/binary families with separate declared encoding rules/classes. | No automatic support from one working `application/json` resource or a MIME header; any Profile requiring an alternate encoding needs explicit capability and tests. |
| Controls / Commands / outcomes | Part 2 **§10 /req/controlstream**, §10.7 commands/status and §16.1 schema; also link to System and required source-visible issuer/status/time fields where supplied. | A reported `ACCEPTED` or `COMPLETED` is **native command status**, not independent verification of physical effect, successful calibration or operator authorization. |
| Maintenance and system events | Part 2 **§12 /req/system-event** and JSON `systemEvent` encoding §16.1.11; SensorML §8.9 is a different deployment carrier. | System events may describe maintenance, component replacement and firmware updates with their original evidence, but the standard’s illustrative type definitions contain **TBD placeholders**; no standard type URI or *already-issued Bennett SystemEvent* may be invented. |
| Power / diagnostics / security / provenance | SensorML §8.2, SWE Common quantities/records, original ancillary donor semantics (IETF EMAN RFCs, OPC UA Devices/PA-DIM, WoT TD etc; see [§27](#27-step-23--cross-cutting-whole-system-coverage-and-evidence-gaps)). | These carriers/terms have **specific original scopes**, not one universal mandated power/health/security/profile package. Part 1 security considerations do not select an authenticated API scheme. |

**Media verification rule for a later claim:** confirm (1) the actual declared conformance/representation class and format list, (2) correct `Accept` / content-type negotiation, (3) matching per-stream schema `obsFormat`, data component tree and encoding declaration, and (4) returned value structure truly validating against that format. **HTTP 200 + a claimed SWE content type alone is insufficient.** A production route or vendor support for LoRaWAN, SDI-12 or MQTT is **not itself** a CSAPI representation guarantee.

**Disposition of all B25 entries: INVENTORIED, NOT BOUND.** Native source clauses can be quoted as existing conditional standard behavior; **future Profile selections remain OPEN** until their designated Phase E steps. The register is **not** a proposed 18-SHALL Profile, a claim that each relation is mandatory in a single OGC resource, or a cross-standard class equivalence chart. The Profile may eventually exclude/conditionally scope unsupported representations.

### 29.5 Bennett's Point — explicit six-part source-to-encoding evidence ceiling

| Actual source meaning | What the native OGC carrier requires/allows | Open question or observed implementation limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **One real station acebpmet, two serial-numbered probes, three day-qualified service periods** | Part 1 can represent Systems and a Deployment with required `validTime` and attached `deployedSystems`; SensorML §8.9 also describes deployment/platform/location. | **No native machine-readable Deployment was created.** Source day-only use, as-of 2024 and unknown precise swap endpoints must not become exact UTC clock times or a claim of current 2026 use. |
| **Two original NOAA/CDMO 15-minute station-temperature means** | Part 2 Observation resources linked to DataStream, single producing System and invariant stream properties/result schema. | **Four prior API Observation resources represent only two source measurements**; stream producing System and exact performed Observer/processor roles not yet selected. |
| **Original station row timestamp 12:00 fixed EST; 15-minute averaging rule** | OMS `phenomenonTime` for an average refers to an interval; Part 2 `application/json` observation uses top-level `TimeInstant`. | **16:45–17:00 UTC interval is DERIVED** from prior-to-stamp convention, not original row endpoints. Normal JSON cannot carry interval natively in `phenomenonTime`; arbitrary field `window` preserves context but not normative mapping. |
| **Original independent result-availability time not given** | OMS distinguishes result availability from phenomenon time; Part 2 ordinary JSON `resultTime` is required `TimeInstant`. | **UNKNOWN source fact**. Filling JSON `resultTime` with the station stamp is a previously disclosed test surrogate, not source truth or a justified conformance determination. This may block full honest observational binding. |
| **Original `F_ATemp = "<0> "` (trailing space) on both source results** | SWE `Text` can preserve raw token; Part 2 `parametersSchema` and `resultSchema` provide typed structures in relevant encoding. OMS result-quality semantics remain separate. | Earlier server dropped top-level observation `parameters`; archival report result preserved QA and interval **as custom record context** but did not establish native OMS resultQuality. |
| **Station location, mounted Temp/RH role and dated service** | Part 1 spatial feature/deployment contexts can describe evidenced location/host and source-qualified time. | Station position/mount height **not independently surveyed individual EE181 geometry**. Do not automatically assign it to probe Systems or infer precise probe location/Deployment. |

**Negative interoperability checks to carry to later Steps 26–31:** (i) a product-only case generates an installed `System` or fake `Deployment`; (ii) data field count generates physical sensor/channel count; (iii) two stream encodings become two performed NOAA Observations; (iv) a scalar output’s source station stamp becomes guaranteed `resultTime`; (v) `TimeRange` in an arbitrary SWE `DataRecord` becomes normative phenomenonTime without explicit valid standard mapping; (vi) HTTP 200 and a SWE MIME header are called conformance without correct format schema/record; (vii) QA token is substituted as sensor condition; (viii) a SAPIENT ACK or illustrated control becomes an authorized verified effect. **These are not new executed runtime tests.**

### 29.6 Implementation check: what was actually observed versus still untested

Reused [31 October Observation Encoding Decision](../Candidate_Specification/Observation_Encoding_Decision_31OCT2026.md) and [PITON v1.9 live experiment record](../PITON_DIM_Profile_V2_Continuity_v1.9_31OCT2026.md), **not a fresh November 2 API probe**:

| Prior recorded test | Verified then | What remains unproved today |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ordinary `application/json` scalar/historical observations | 52°North/pygeoapi service accepted/stored/read back selected content and DataRecord preservation bridge. | Proper Part 2 JSON TimeInterval/quality semantics, honest unknown resultTime and final stream ownership. |
| `application/swe+json` retrieval with format query | HTTP 200 and SWE content-type header were seen, but **body was ordinary JSON Observation objects**. | Proper Part 2 Req109 `recordSchema/JSONEncoding` and Req111 SWE JSON Observation records were **NOT established**. |
| `schema?obsFormat=application/swe+json` | Returned ordinary `application/json` `resultSchema` rather than SWE `recordSchema`. | Real format-specific schema support remains unverified. |
| Observation-level `parameters` | Pinned upstream parser omitted them; archived live bridge placed native QA in an explicit result record instead. | Native observation `parameters` retention or mapped `resultQuality` remains unproven for this server. |
| `GET /conformance` and live client | Earlier conformance response advertised OGC API Common core; curated Bennett page read server data successfully using known IDs. | Neither Part 2 SWE JSON class nor a universal profile-aware Glaux discovery/interpretation is proven. Deployed server may have local adaptations. |

**Crucial distinction:** standards **allow** more than one result structure/format with separate obligations. An **implementation may lack** such support. The **source may lack** an underlying fact even if a format could encode it. These are three different failure layers; none is fixed by fabricating a resultTime, adding a user-interface label or copying the same observation under another resource ID.

### 29.7 Step result, precise next action and gate

**Completed Step 25:** reviewed actual approved versions of CSAPI Parts 1/2, SensorML 3.0, SWE Common 3.0, Features Core/Common and original OMS conceptual time/quality/observer distinctions; distinguished requirements-class applicability, native media and parent schemas; directly checked relevant companion Part 2 observation JSON schema with explicit mutable-source/official-schema caveat; produced **18-entry B25 binding inventory, six-part Bennett evidence ceiling and negative test list**, without declaring bindings or implementation success.

**GATE E REMAINS OPEN.** There are no new candidate Profile SHALLs, finalized System-kind/host/stream bindings, source facts, vendor-conformance claims, completed SWE conformance tests or server/client modifications. The prior candidate Profile stays intact for later discriminating source-backed revision.

**STEP 26 NEXT — ONLY on the owner's next “proceed”:** propose and challenge **individual System, product kind/Procedure, identity, physical and functional constituents, host and Deployment bindings**, with exact Part 1/SensorML source referents, positive and **negative** examples for all four case scopes. Preserve the required Bennett date-only/as-of qualifiers and do **not** pretend a compliant populated Deployment exists when actual exact endpoint or identity facts are missing. Do **not** preempt Step 27 DataStreams/observer roles, Step 29 observation time/quality encoding, Step 30 normative Profile drafting, Glaux code or live API changes.


## 30. Step 26 — Identity, kind, composition, Host and Deployment binding proposals

**Status: STEP 26 COMPLETE, 2 November 2026; STEP 27 NEXT; Gate E OPEN.** The substantive manuscript is [Candidate CSAPI Profile — Step 26](../Candidate_Specification/CSAPI_Profile.md#step-26--source-qualified-system-procedure-physical-relationships-and-deployment), containing **P26-01–P26-12 proposed field/relationship bindings** and two worked mapping examples. This workbook section records **the primary standard authority, original case data, rejected inferences and binding-decision limits**, not issued normative profile text. It does **not** adopt a new CSAPI requirements class, mint or write production resources, populate a Deployment, modify the application, or resolve Step 27 DataStream producing-System and Step 29 time/quality semantics.

### 30.1 In ordinary language — what becomes communicable, and what does not

We can now propose **where several verified whole-System meanings belong in existing CSAPI Part 1 and SensorML 3.0 structures**, rather than simply saying the standards possess some matching concepts. A manufacturer **model/type** may be published as a **Procedure datasheet**; a separately identified **individual sensor or continuing real station** may be described as a **System**; the individual and kind can be connected using **`systemKind`** or **`typeOf`**. The actual sources still control *whether those referents exist* and which identity each original string describes. A published product design, cloud example, program example and server local ID are not magically separate physical installations.

The decisive structural test comes from the original [CSAPI Part 1 §10.2](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html): **composition** of integral parts is treated with nested **Subsystems** while **aggregation** of equipment that may be attached to different parents at different times is treated using **Deployments**. Part 1 explicitly uses a **weather station with interchangeable sensors** as an aggregation example. We therefore cannot convert Bennett's returning EE181 probe into a second manufactured unit or hide its time-scoped role by permanent nesting under the station. Nor should the first three manufacturer-only cases generate actual deployed System units merely because their designs contain several modules or a cable.

**However, the standards' support for Deployment does not supply Bennett's missing timestamps.** Original operator equipment history gives *calendar-day-only first/last-use/as-of claims*, including the shared **16 April 2024** changeover day. The approved Part 1 §11.2 **`Deployment.validTime`** is required; the inspected companion **GeoJSON `deployment.json`** requires that field and **`common/timePeriod.json`** expects two ISO date-time instants or the special `now` token. Neither the source nor this standards inspection establishes the exact instant of physical replacement, UTC interpretation, endpoint inclusivity or 2026 continued use. Thus we propose the conceptual *association* but place the **machine-readable conformant Deployment binding on HOLD** rather than invent a midnight time or making “still used as of 2024” mean “still used now.” This corrects an overly decisive statement in the earlier 31 October Profile manuscript while preserving the historical text.

### 30.2 Exact approved-standard authority and companion schema distinctions

| Original publication/clause | Native standard statement and exact representation | Consequence and limited authority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [**OGC API – Connected Systems Part 1, OGC 23-001 v1.0, §§8.4–8.5 / Requirements 1–2**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | `id` unique across resources of each type, **`uniqueIdentifier`** a valid URI unique across the server; Part 1 **§19.1 Table 39** uses **GeoJSON `properties.uid`**, **§19.2 Table 48** uses **SensorML `uniqueId`**. | A project URI can identify an attested individual when issued with documented governance, but the original manufacturer/operator station code or serial is **not a URI provided by the source**, and local `id` is not a universal serial. No new URIs were minted here. |
| [**Part 1 §§9.1–9.2 Tables 4–7 and §13.1–13.2 Tables 14–16**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | **`System` instances** distinct from **`Procedure` type/datasheet** descriptions; required System `systemType`; optional `assetType`; optional `systemKind` link **to one Procedure**. `Procedure` types include both **sosa:Sensor datasheet** and **sosa:ObservingProcedure method**, which do **not** mean the same thing. Part 1 **§13.2.3 Requirement 24** says Procedure is nonspatial. | **Keep model, individual, reference pairing and observing method separate.** A source-led sensor kind may have a Procedure and no installed Systems. The station and EE181 individual are actual, but exact classification of station/Host and published model link remain proposals. |
| [**Part 1 §19.1 Requirements 80–82; Tables 39–41**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | GeoJSON `System.properties.uid`, `name`, `featureType`, optional `assetType`, and **`properties["systemKind@link"]`** pointing to a Procedure. | The path is native **when GeoJSON format class is implemented**. A field/path proposal alone is not a valid, deployed or source-authored GeoJSON record. No native additional source-identification property is invented here. |
| [**Part 1 §19.2 Requirements 92–96; Tables 48–50**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | SensorML `uniqueId`, `label`, `definition` and **`typeOf`**; `attachedTo` for subsystem parent, other links by relation; **Req95** requires applicable `PhysicalComponent` or `PhysicalSystem` for described hardware, `SimpleProcess` or `AggregateProcess` for simulations/processes. | SensorML class selection is **semantic and conditional**, not a universal imported hierarchy. A firmware/process role and physical assembly may need different source-described classes and process connections. |
| [**SensorML 3.0, OGC 23-000 §§8.2.2.3, 8.2.9.3, 8.3–8.6**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-000/23-000.html) | `identifiers[]` Term with `definition`, `codeSpace`, `value` for original ID types; `typeOf` a resolvable general process (Req13); atomic versus aggregate, physical versus computational processes, components/connections/interfaces. | Enables source-qualified serial/issuer and product type inheritance, but **does not assert manufacturer source URI issuance, physical deployment, an installed running program or observed execution**. A bare cable is not proof of a fully specified SensorML connection graph. |
| [**Part 1 §10.1–10.5 / Subsystems class, especially §10.2**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | Hierarchical **nested `System`** for **composition** of integral parts; **aggregation via `Deployment`** for detachable/reassignable equipment, with specific weather-station examples. | **Nested `Subsystem` ≠ all externally connected items**; a product design may have described physical subparts that are not independently identified live System resources. The original standard itself rejects our tempting interchangeable-probe-as-permanent-child shortcut. |
| [**Part 1 §11.1–11.2 Tables 10–11**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | **`Deployment`** separate Feature, required uniqueIdentifier/name/`validTime` and deployedSystems association, optional platform/location. **Platform conceptually any Feature**, while §19.1 GeoJSON **Table 43** maps `platform@link` to **System** and `deployedSystems@link` to System list. | Conceptual assignment for Bennett is supported, but **format-level treatment of non-System platform requires later review**. Host/platform ≠ permanent containment or source-stated unique physical probe coordinates. |
| [**Part 1 §19.1 Tables 42–43, inspected companion GeoJSON `deployment.json`, `timePeriod.json` and `timeInstant.json`**](https://github.com/opengeospatial/ogcapi-connected-systems/tree/master/api/part1/openapi/schemas/geojson) | `properties.validTime` is the encoded time period, plus links. Inspected `deployment.json` requires validTime; `TimePeriod` is an array of two `TimeInstantOrNow` values; `TimeInstant` has `format: date-time`. `now` as a special supported token does **not** mean historic “still in use as of 2024.” | **CANNOT claim** three valid, lossless Bennett machine-readable Deployment resources without a justified temporal granularity/interval model. Companion repository is **mutable as of this retrieval**, not independently re-hashed to the exact approved 2025 release schema. Do not create fake midnight instant bounds or pretend a date-only source had UTC exchange times. |
| [**Part 1 §19.2 Tables 51–52; SensorML 3.0 §8.9/Tables 2–3**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | `Deployment.validTime`, `platform`, `deployedSystems[]` with `DeployedSystem.system` and optional `configuration`; deployment location and specific deployed item separate from station feature. | Source requires support for the **actual individual, association and original time scope**. Simply publishing one `DeployedSystem` would not resolve the unknown Observer of a logger-aggregated NOAA result or warrant a calibration/configuration record. |
| [**Part 1 §9.2.3 Requirement 4 (location-time)**](https://docs.ogc.org/is/23-001/23-001.html) | A System's reported location, if given, is **latest known** unless a specific time snapshot requested. A GeoJSON System's `geometry` is a Point; `Procedure` has no geometry. | Original station `32.55934°N, 80.45456°W` is a **station-level source location**, not independently surveyed position of a particular serialized EE181. Historical service does not establish a probe's current 2026 geometry. |

**Original-source audit scope:** Published Part 1 and SensorML HTML were read directly in relevant clauses, and current companion GitHub JSON schema files were read for `system.json`, `procedure.json`, `deployment.json`, `deployedSystem.json`, `feature.json`, `commonDefs.json` and `timePeriod/timeInstant/timeInstantOrNow`. The **Part 1 companion master branch** is a **current later revision** and must not be represented as a byte-for-byte verified published 2025 schema snapshot. This is normative-source/representation assessment, **not schema-validating the actual cases**. No PDFs were analyzed or copied as part of Step 26.

### 30.3 Source→DIM→OGC mapping: four cases with different evidence strength

| Source-native subject and original evidentiary standing | Distinct R24 conceptual referents and associations | Conditional Part 1/SensorML binding to propose; precise withholding |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Dragino S31-LB** — [C source sheet §§1, 2 and 5](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Dragino_S31_LB.md), manufacturer **reference** node plus externally cabled three-metre SHT31 | Kind/model versus no verified individual (R24-01/02); node and external probe support/digital connection (R24-03/04); node radio reporting versus probe sensing (R24-06). | **CANDIDATE** one source-grounded sensor-kind `Procedure` (possibly SensorML type-level aggregate descriptions) and native source-described connected/supplied role. **NO** individual `System` identity, nested serialized probe unit, current location, deployment timestamp or current connection. `S31B` integrated variant not imported. |
| **AirGradient Open Air Max** — [A source sheet §§1–2](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/AirGradient_Open_Air_Max.md), pinned **gas-equipped O-M-1PPSTON-CE** reference | One product assembly description, ESP32-C6 board, **two separate physical PMS5003 devices** at PM1/PM2 sockets, other modules (R24-02/03/06); PM field meanings and output quantities distinct from connectors. | **CANDIDATE** type-level sensor datasheet `Procedure` and, if compatible with the source's physical/system descriptions, SensorML component/interface descriptions. **NO** individually identified PMS5003 Systems, valid installed serials, 0/0 location, actual fielded configuration or Deployment. Do not equate `CE` with `CE-S` or two connectors with PM1.0/PM2.5 environmental properties. |
| **Campbell CR1000X + first-generation ClimaVue 50** — [D source sheet §§1–4](../Controlled_Corpus/System_Data_Sheets/Campbell_CR1000X_ClimaVue50.md), *two separate manufacturer product reference kinds* | Two independently existing product descriptions with potential SDI-12 connection and distinct sensing/processing/reporting roles (R24-02/04/06/07), not an operator-attested station. | **CANDIDATE** separately identified `Procedure` for sensor type and logger type; source-expressible design connection/processing chain via appropriate SensorML process description **only if data flow and source ports are independently documented**. **NO** installed `System`/`Deployment`, actual logger program, serial from aI! pattern `SN-yyyyyyyy`, joined logger Status or station geometry. |

Original two air-temperature means are separate measurement evidence, NOT native individual serial assertions. | **CANDIDATE** separately identifiable station and two actual probe `System` resources, each with source-attested original identifier and optional published EE181 datasheet `Procedure` link. **P26 Deployment HOLD:** three source role-use statements can motivate three future dated Deployment associations, **but the required exact serialized validTime bounds, URI governance and as-of/endpoint semantics are NOT established**. Reuse the returning probe's identity; no current 2026 location/operating claim, duplicate new station or source-native station-observation probe serial. |

**Source role checks:** Bennett's actual continuing station can be represented as a distinct candidate `System` only when we separately decide whether it should be classified **`sosa:System`, `sosa:Sensor` or `sosa:Platform`** under Part 1 §9.2's primary-activity rules. A reference weather-station **product kind** might have different classification. `sosa:System` is an intelligible **fallback candidate**, but source semantics must be reviewed before adopting a Profile-wide station policy; similarly the original physical logger may be a distinct equipment `System` without making it the observer of the final station mean.

### 30.4 Bennett equipment chronology — exact original precision, never an invented deployment

The original [ACE Basin 2024 meteorological metadata, §9, printed p. 6](https://cdmo.baruch.sc.edu/waf/YearlyFiles/ACE%20Basin/meteorological/metadata/acemet01-12.24m.pdf) records exactly these three use-period *statements* for the two source-identified EE181 serials and one station:

| Service assertion | Source individual / station role | Original temporal meaning | Proposed representation standing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **B26-01** | EE181 serial **21281600077814**, Temp/RH sensor 3, station **acebpmet** | First described use **4 May 2022**, last listed use **8 June 2023**; **calendar date only**. | A real **equipment–station dated service relation** exists. **Not** a proven timestamped `Deployment.validTime` interval or a distinct third unit. |
| **B26-02** | EE181 serial **222016000109CE**, Temp/RH sensor 4, station **acebpmet** | First described use **8 June 2023**, last listed use **16 April 2024**; **calendar date only**. | Different real individual, same kind and station role. **No** source-stated precise exchange instant, overlap or independently known probe geometry. |
| **B26-03** | Returned EE181 serial **21281600077814**, Temp/RH sensor 3, station **acebpmet** | From **16 April 2024**, **still in use as of 31 December 2024**. | The **same** physical individual as B26-01 in another use period; an **as-of knowledge claim**, not a removal date or present-2026 deployment. |

**Additional source facts held separately:** 16 April **09:45–13:30 fixed Eastern Standard Time** covers wider station shutdown/maintenance, **not the serial-specific replacement clock**. The station point `32.55934°N, 80.45456°W` and Temp/RH mounting around **3.3–3.35m** are source station/role context, **not individually surveyed probes**. The distinct 17 April logger operating-system update applies to the CR1000X, not a probe. A source-published station value/quality does not prove the particular OMS Observer/CSAPI DataStream producer; leave Step 27 and Step 29 open.

**No date coercion:** **Neither `2024-04-16T00:00:00Z`, `2024-04-16T09:45:00-05:00` nor `2024-04-16T13:30:00-05:00`** can be asserted as the replacement instant from the operator use table. The endpoint `now` could be syntactically allowed by a schema, but is *semantically false as a present-2026 source claim* when the operator only reports still-in-use through 2024. If a later Profile supports a **qualified temporal envelope/uncertainty interval**, it must preserve original date assertions **as original**, identify any envelope as **DERIVED**, and not describe the bounds as exact deployment transitions. This option has NOT been specified or tested here.
